# PhiLogg — Changelog

Chronological, newest-first. Moved here from PROJECT.md's old "Status / changelog" section; see `PROJECT.md` for current-state architecture and `docs/*.md` for current-state feature descriptions.

- **Filter-Toolbar reordered: Before/After/Time range first** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested). `VIEWBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` (`philogg.html`) now lists `filterBefore, filterAfter, timeRangeFromSelection, filterForMessage, extractMessage, newFilter` — previously After came before Before, and Message/Extract sat between Before/After and Time range. Reasoning: Before/After/Time range are visible in every view, while Message/Extract are Context/Filtered-only (see the entry below) — putting the three always-visible actions first keeps their toolbar position stable across views, instead of shifting depending on whether Message/Extract are shown. Purely a reordering — no behavior, icon, or label changes. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 176a's `expectedActions` order-sensitive assertion updated to match.

- **"Filter after"/"Filter before" (and, for consistency, "Message"/"Extract") now actually work in Table and Plot, not just Context/Filtered** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested follow-up/correction). The earlier "show After/Before in Table and Plot" request had been read as "show that a filter is active" (see the `#tableFilterInfo`/`#plotFilterInfo` text entry below) — what was actually wanted was the row-action **buttons** themselves, which had been hidden on Table/Plot since an earlier session (they used to key off a single Context/Filtered log-row selection those views don't have). New `afterBeforeActionEntries()` resolves After/Before's input the same per-view way "Time range"/"Select" already do (marked Table rows, the Plot tab's visible viewport, or the Context/Filtered log selection) — but unlike Time range, a **single** resolved entry is valid too (that entry's own ts becomes the bound, the original single-row behavior); with **2+** entries the bound is now *inclusive* — "After" uses the **earliest** ts among them, "Before" the **latest** — rather than requiring one exact reference row. `updateViewBarRowActions()` no longer hides After/Before on Table/Plot at all. "Message"/"Extract" stay true single-entry actions (new `singleMessageActionEntry()`): extracting a pattern only makes sense for one specific message, so Table enables them only with **exactly one** marked row (0 or 2+ disables), and Plot never enables them — there's no "one clicked/hovered point" concept there, and none was built for this. `handleRowActionButtons()`'s disabled-state computation and `handleRowActionClick`'s `filterAfter`/`filterBefore`/`filterForMessage`/`extractMessage` cases updated to use the new resolvers instead of the old single-selection-only `currentRowActionEntry()`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 178** rewritten for the new visibility split (After/Before always visible, Message/Extract hide only on Plot); **Group 176a** updated (2+ multi-select no longer disables After/Before, only Message/Extract); new **Group 187** (187a-d) covers Table's single-row vs. inclusive-multi-row resolution, Plot's viewport resolution, and Message/Extract's stricter exactly-one-entry gating in both views. `docs/ui-and-views.md` updated in place. **3692 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Fixed: timeline minimap's "currently rendered" rect (`#minimapRenderedRangeRect`) went stale on the Table/Plot tabs** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-reported). `updateMinimapRenderedRange()` had branches for the Log view's Context/Filtered tabs only — switching to Table or Plot left the rect showing whatever the Log view last computed, and scrolling the table or zooming the plot did nothing to it. Two new branches: a **Table** branch derives the visible row range from `extractScroll.scrollTop`/`EXTRACT_ROW_HEIGHT` (rows there are a flat height, no per-row offsets — same idea `minimapRenderedSpan` uses for the Log view's flat-`ROW_HEIGHT` case, with the sticky `#extractHead`'s own height subtracted first), wired into `renderExtractTable()` and `extractScroll`'s scroll listener (rAF-batched, same pattern as `tableBody`/`highlightBody`). A **Plot** branch is built on a new shared helper, **`visiblePlotPoints()`** (top-level, next to `renderPlotChart`) — every finite-`(x,y)` point from the most recent render (`plotLastSeries`, a new snapshot alongside `plotLastRender`), restricted to `plotZoom`'s x-domain when a zoom is active; the rect's bounds come from each point's *source entry* timestamp (`extractRowsData[p.rowIndex].entry.ts`), not the plotted x value, since the X column plotted is often not time at all. Both `renderExtractTable()`/`renderPlotChart()` also now call `renderTimelineMinimap(rootId, entries)` with their own view's entries (not stale Log-view data) for the minimap's background/full-range box, plus `updateMinimapRenderedRange()` itself (`renderTimelineMinimap()` deliberately doesn't call it — see its own comment). `visiblePlotPoints()` is also used by the Plot toolbar's own filter-info point count (see next entry). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 184** — Table view scrolling moves the rect (independently computed via `minimapBarSpan`), Plot view's `plotZoom` narrows/hides it, and a Log→Table→Plot→Log walk leaves no stale span behind at any step (the regression itself). **3631 passed, 0 failed**.

- **Table/Plot toolbars show the active time filter(s) plus a row/selection or visible-point count** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested plan, "Paket B" of a multi-package delegation — parallel with a sibling "Paket C" minimap-sync package on another branch, merged just before this one). New `#tableFilterInfo`/`#plotFilterInfo` spans, right-aligned in `#tableToolbar`/`#plotToolbar` (`.toolbar-filter-info`, muted tabular-numeric text matching `#contextNavLabel`'s style), report what's currently narrowing/shown without having to check the tree/breadcrumb: any active "after"/"before"/"timerange" ancestor filter (new helper `activeTimeFilters()`, near `isTimeFilterType` — walks `getChain(state.activeId)`, names each via the existing `timeRangeFilterName()`), joined with the row count (Table: `extractRowsData.length`) or visible-point count (Plot), plus a selection count when non-zero (Table: `markedRowEntries().length`). Filled by `updateTableFilterInfo()` (end of `renderExtractTable()`) and `updatePlotFilterInfo()` (end of `renderPlotChart()`, including its early-return branches so the span stays accurate for 3D/empty/too-small-viewport states). The Plot count goes through `visiblePlotPoints()` — the shared helper Paket C's minimap-sync package owns (see previous entry); this branch originally carried a TEMP stub for it, removed during merge in favor of Paket C's real implementation. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 185** (185a Table, 185b Plot; renumbered from a colliding "184" at merge time) covers the info text with/without an active timerange filter, the row/point counts, and the Table selection count. **3622 passed, 0 failed** (3613 + 9). See `docs/ui-and-views.md`.

- **Filter-Toolbar: "Extract" and "New" row-actions, distinct "Message" icon** (this session, 2026-09-08, Paket A of a person-approved plan). Two new buttons join `VIEWBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` (`#viewBar`'s row-actions group). **"Extract"** (`extractMessage`, next to "Message", reuses `ICON_TABLE`) is a one-click version of "Message": it builds the exact same auto-extraction pattern from the current row's message column (`buildNumericExtractPattern` + `collapseNewlinesToWildcard`, unchanged) but instead of opening the filter popup for review, commits it straight to a new `filterType:"text"` node via `createFilterNode` — the same primitive `commitFilter()`'s own submit path uses — then reveals the Filtered view. New `extractMessageFilter(entry)` function, shares `openFilterForEntryColumn`'s disabled-state gate (`singleRowEnabled`) and Table/Plot hiding (`updateViewBarRowActions`). The resulting node is a plain text-filter node like any other — no special-casing, stays editable via F2/dblclick/context menu afterward. **"New"** (`newFilter`, at the end, behind a new visual separator) is a one-click `openFilterPopup()`, identical to Ctrl+F; gated only on `state.activeId` (no row needed). `buildRowActionsHtml` gained a `separator: true` entry flag rendering a `.row-action-separator` divider (new CSS, `var(--border)`) immediately before an entry. Also: **"Message"** (`filterForMessage`) gets its own icon, `ICON_MESSAGE_EXTRACT` (a speech-bubble outline with bracket accents) — it no longer shares the generic funnel `ICON_FILTER` every other filter-creation button uses. New `ICON_FILTER_PLUS` (funnel + badge-overlaid "+", same technique as `ICON_FILE_AUTO`'s circled "A") for "New". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 186** (186a-c: Extract creates a node with no popup shown and stays normally editable, New opens the popup empty in "Add filter" mode and gates on `state.activeId` alone, the Message icon no longer matches the generic funnel path; renumbered from a colliding "184" at merge time); Group 176a's `expectedActions`/disabled-state assertions and Group 178's `logOnlyActions` list extended for the two new buttons.

- **PiP toolbar: more breathing room around the View-Selector; web build: theme applied before first paint (no more FOUC)** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested, two small fixes). (1) `#tauri-pip` (`desktop/src-tauri/src/inject.js`) grew again, 36px → 40px, giving the 28px `#fhTabs` View-Selector 6px of clearance top and bottom instead of ~4px each side; `html.pip-mode #app`'s `padding-top` and `html.pip-mode #fhTabs`'s `top` (`philogg.html`, 4px → 6px) updated to match and keep it centered. (2) Web build: `initTheme()` (applying `localStorage["philogg-theme"]`/`prefers-color-scheme` via `data-theme` on `<html>`) only ran at the very end of the huge inline `<script>`, so the page briefly painted the default dark `:root` values first — worst for light/Catppuccin-Latte users. Added a tiny synchronous inline `<script>` immediately after `<body>` (before any visible markup) that reads `localStorage.getItem("philogg-theme")` (falling back to `prefers-color-scheme`) and sets `data-theme` immediately; `initTheme()` itself is unchanged and still runs later as the source of truth, correcting the rare case of a stale/unresolvable theme id (its `resolveThemeId()` is deliberately not duplicated in the early script — kept trivial on purpose). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` assumed exactly one inline `<script>` in `philogg.html` (both its precompiled-`PAGE_SCRIPT` extraction and the strip-comments round-trip check in Group 146d used the first/only `<script>...</script>` match) — both updated to target the *last* `<script>...</script>` pair (the main app script), now that there are two. `docs/desktop.md` updated for the PiP strip's new height/offsets; no dedicated theme doc section existed to update. No behavioral test added for the FOUC fix itself — jsdom parses the whole document before running any inline scripts (unlike a real browser's incremental parse), so a "did the flash not happen" assertion wouldn't be meaningful there. **3609 passed, 0 failed** (net, after accounting for pre-existing sharding flakiness unrelated to this change — see 2026-09-07 entry below).

- **Wildcard-pattern syntax simplified: `[value:TYPE]` → `[*:TYPE]`** (this session, 2026-09-08, person-requested). The two separate placeholder vocabularies for "match anything" (`[*]`) and typed extraction (`[value:float]`, `[value:int]`, ...) are unified under one `*`-prefixed family: `[*]` still means "anything", and typed placeholders (with their optional numeric conditions, e.g. `[*:float>=10]`) now use `[*:TYPE]` instead of `[value:TYPE]`. Purely a literal-string change in `EXTRACT_TOKEN_RE` (`philogg.html`) — the alternation still structurally distinguishes the bare wildcard from a typed one, only the prefix text moved from `value:` to `*:`; `compileExtractPattern`, `parseValueConditions`, `valueSatisfiesConditions`, `wildcardMatch`, and the condition grammar (`VALUE_CONDITION_*`) are untouched. No release has shipped yet, so this is a straight replacement with **no legacy `[value:...]` alias** — old patterns typed as `[value:float]` are no longer recognized as wildcard tokens. Updated everywhere the old syntax appeared: the filter popup's placeholder text/token-chip tooltips, `insertTokenAtCursor`, `buildNumericExtractPattern`'s auto-generated patterns, in-code comments, `docs/filters.md`, `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`, `docs/ui-concept-*.md`, `docs/ui-implementation-plan.md`, `PROJECT.md`, `README.md`, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`, `tools/log-simulator.html`, `homepage/index.html`, and the SVG sketches under `docs/images/`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: every `[value:...]` pattern literal across the suite updated to `[*:...]` (mechanical, no behavioral test changes).

- **Unify the Table/Plot filters: "Time range"/"Select" replace Plot's dedicated viewport buttons; two flaky-suite bugfixes** (this session, 2026-09-07, person-requested). Plot's own two dedicated `#plotToolbar` buttons (`#plotFilterTimeRangeBtn`/`#plotFilterEntriesBtn`, and the second `[data-row-actions="plot-viewbar"]` group in `#viewBar` that swapped in for them) are gone entirely. Instead, `#viewBar`'s row-actions are context-aware: Filter after/before this/for this message hide on Table/Plot (they key off a log-row selection those tabs don't have), while **"Time range"** stays visible everywhere and resolves its input per view — a 2+ log-row multi-selection in Context/Filtered (unchanged), rows with at least one marked cell in Table (a partial row counts as the whole row), or the Plot tab's own currently-visible viewport (`timeRangeActionEntries()`). The Filtered view's **"Select"** (Add to selection) action now also lives in Table's and Plot's own toolbars (`SELECT_ROW_ACTION_HTML`, one `TOOLBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` entry rendered into a new `[data-row-actions="select-action"]` span in each), resolving the same way (`selectActionEntries()`, a single marked row/viewport is enough, unlike "Time range"'s 2+) — `currentSelectionRowIds()` is now a thin wrapper around it. On Plot, both stay gated on `plot2dToolsAvailable` (something actually plotted in 2D), with the exact viewport count re-checked at click time via a toast since zoom/pan don't always go through `updateRowActionButtons()`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 123 rewritten for the unified path (idset/getPlotViewportEntries coverage kept), Group 176d/177a/178 updated for the removed buttons and the new copies/hiding, new **Group 183** (183a-c) covers Table's marked-cell resolution and Plot's viewport resolution directly. `docs/ui-and-views.md`, `docs/filters.md`, `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` updated to match (were left describing the old two-button/two-group design).

  Two bugs surfaced while getting the regression suite fully green again under `npm test`'s parallel sharding (both intermittent — invisible in a fast/unloaded single-group run, only reproducible via repeated full 8-shard runs or manually saturating all cores): (1) a **crash** — `restoreSessionFromCache`'s own `finally` (`updateMultilineMsgButton` etc.) firing after a test's `window.close()` under load, since most groups never await the boot-restore promise before tearing down their window; fixed by awaiting it (`window.__t.bootRestore`) in `withApp`'s own `finally`, once, for every test. (2) a **cross-session state leak** in Group 30e: `render()` unconditionally debounces a session-cache meta write (`schedulePersistMeta`, 400ms), and that timer is a real one — jsdom's `window.close()` does not reliably cancel it under load — so it can fire well after its own window closed, landing mid-way through a *later*, `indexedDB`-sharing nested session (the group's own "simulated reload" pattern) and writing a real "meta" record there; that flips `sessionRestoreInProgress` on for the later session's own boot, silently no-op'ing its own explicit `persistFileHistoryNow()` call. Fixed narrowly in the test itself — Group 30e's session 2 now barriers on `await T2.bootRestore;` first, same "reload" idiom every other group already uses, rather than a broad harness change (an earlier attempt to fix this by force-flushing `persistMetaNow()`/`persistFileHistoryNow()` in every `withApp` teardown was reverted — it made the contamination deterministic instead of rare, breaking Group 30e even standalone). **3613 passed, 0 failed**, confirmed clean across three consecutive full `npm test` runs (~76-79s each) plus repeated isolated 8-way-parallel reproductions, after two of those repros reliably failed pre-fix.

- **Mini-View toolbar taller + Filter-Toolbar controls vertically centered** (this session, 2026-09-07, person-requested, two visual fixes). (1) The mini window's injected strip (`#tauri-pip`, `desktop/src-tauri/src/inject.js`) grew from 30px to 36px so the 28px ViewMode switcher (`#fhTabs`, `position:fixed` into the strip's left) no longer sits flush against its top/bottom edges — `html.pip-mode #app`'s matching `padding-top` and `html.pip-mode #fhTabs`'s `top` (1px → 4px) were updated so the switcher stays vertically centered in the taller strip. (2) `#viewBar` (the Filter-Toolbar) floated its controls with a vestigial `margin-bottom:6px` left over from when the breadcrumb used to wrap *below* them in the same row — that margin made the top padding (8px) and effective bottom spacing (6px margin + 8px padding = 14px) asymmetric, so the tabs/level-pills/row-actions sat ~3px too high. Removed the bottom margin from `#fhTabs`/`#levelBar`/`#btnApplyLevelToTree`/`#viewBar .row-actions` (now `margin:0 14px 0 0` / `margin:0`), leaving the row's symmetric 8px top/bottom padding to center them. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 102's margin assertion updated (6px → 0px bottom margin). Pure CSS/JS-injection, no functional changes.

- **Picture-in-picture (PiP), replacing the abandoned popout-window approach** (this session, 2026-09-07, person-requested). The `claude/popout-window-feature-b87cwa` branch built "popout" as a second `WebviewWindow` mirroring the active view; its whole history is sync bugs (missed pushes, `JSON.stringify` dropping negative indices, blank containers, tailing never engaging) plus a window-creation deadlock on Windows (tauri-apps/wry#583), all of which trace back to *making a second copy of the app*. This replaces it with the opposite design: **no second window, no pushed state, no sync, no jump-back IPC.** The *same* `philogg.html` instance — same `state`, same DOM, same render pipeline, same real nodes — becomes the PiP, so the sync bugs are impossible by construction. **Trigger is a dedicated button, not a setting**: a diagonal `<->` window-control button (left of minimize, in the injected `#tauri-wc` controls) invokes `pip_enter`, which shrinks the one window in place to `420×320`, sets it always-on-top, and CSS-hides all chrome (`html.pip-mode`) except the active content view. The mini window gets its own slim injected strip (`#tauri-pip`, only visible in `html.pip-mode`, doubling as the drag handle): the **ViewMode switcher (`#fhTabs`) sits at its far left** (`html.pip-mode` collapses `#viewBar` and `position:fixed`-s the tabs into the strip, so the switcher stays usable), a diagonal `<->` on the right returns to the full window (`pip_exit`), and an X that does **not** close the app but ends PiP and minimizes the full window back to the taskbar (`pip_minimize`, restore-then-minimize ordering). Double-clicking an empty spot of the strip also expands back to the full window's **windowed** state (never maximized) — `interceptDragDoubleClick` grabs the drag region's second `mousedown` (`detail === 2`) ahead of Tauri's own drag script (which otherwise fires `internal_toggle_maximize`). F11, the toolbar's double-click, and the injected rectangle button are all **equivalent to the same native maximize/restore** (`window_toggle_maximize` / `internal_toggle_maximize`): a borderless *fullscreen* was tried first, but its "restore on drag" had to be hand-rolled and never felt native, so fullscreen was dropped in favour of maximize — which restores under the cursor the same way whichever trigger entered it. An interaction that jumps to another view also exits PiP first, via the page's `jumpAfterPip` helper (threaded through the six jump entry points: link-view dblclick, Context-row dblclick, extraction-row dblclick, 2D/3D plot-mark click, and the Enter reveal), awaiting `window.philogg.exitPip` so the reveal's scroll/anchor math runs against the restored viewport. `jumpToEntry` has no UI call site on main, so there was nothing to thread. Both modes remember their own position + size (`state.rs` keeps `pip_active`, plus `full_prev`/`full_was_maximized` for the full window and `pip_prev` for the mini window): `enter_pip` leaves maximize *before* capturing the full window's windowed bounds — which is what guarantees going maximized → windowed lands on the full windowed size, never the mini size — and `exit_pip` re-applies that windowed position + size first (resetting the OS's restore size so a later unmaximize/drag-out-of-maximize never lands on the mini size), then re-maximizes if that was the state at entry, while remembering the mini window's own geometry for the next enter. `commands.rs` gains `pip_enter`/`pip_exit`/`pip_minimize`; `window_minimize` is back to a plain minimize; `inject.js` adds the `<->` button and the `#tauri-pip` strip. See `docs/desktop.md` → "Picture-in-picture (PiP)". `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 182** covers the page-side half jsdom can see (`philoggSetPip` flipping `state.pipActive` + `html.pip-mode`, the `html.pip-mode` chrome-hide CSS list as a tripwire, and `jumpAfterPip` routing direct vs. through a stubbed `window.philogg.exitPip`); real window geometry and the injected buttons need `cd desktop && npm run tauri dev`.

- **Fixed: log rows (Context/Filtered) could render narrower than the view when every message was short, making the `.selected` row outline look cut off partway across the screen** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-reported with a screenshot). `#tableRows`/`#highlightRows` already clear their own inline `width` whenever the widest message needs less room than the view — falling back to their `left:0;right:0` stretch-fill — but that decision is only ever recomputed when the entry list itself changes, not on every resize/layout pass, so a stale narrower width computed once could stick around with nothing to correct it. Added `min-width:100%` (of the positioned `#tableSpacer`/`#highlightSpacer` ancestor) to both rules as a pure-CSS floor requiring no JS timing to get right: it always wins over a smaller explicit `width` (min-width clamps upward per the CSS box model), while a genuinely wider explicit width (the real "content needs a horizontal scrollbar" case) still wins over this floor since it's larger. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: extended Group 73's existing "short messages leave #tableRows filling the view" assertion with a `getComputedStyle(...).minWidth === "100%"` check on both elements. **3556 passed, 0 failed** (3554 + 2).

- **Table/Plot: dropped the "n rows · m columns" info line, moved Statistics into its own Table-only Entry-Detail-style panel, plot marks now jump to the Table row instead of Filtered; Ctrl+1-4 now cycle the View Selector positionally** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-requested). (1) `#extractInfo` (the "n rows · m columns (k ignored)" line above the extraction table) is removed entirely — its "Invalid pattern" fallback message now shows in `#extractPatternView` instead. (2) The Statistics panel — previously a shared, per-tab-toggleable bar (`#extractStatsBar`, `#statsToggleTable`/`#statsTogglePlot`) — is replaced by a standalone `#statsPanel`: same position/behavior as `#detailPanel` (the exact same collapsed/peeking pin-or-hover mechanism, `isStatsCollapsed`/`isStatsPeeking`/`toggleStatsCollapsed`, sharing the `hoverExpandDetail` Setting), but nested at the bottom of `#extractWrap`'s own flex column and shown ONLY while Table is the active sub-view — Plot lost the Statistics panel entirely. Defaults pinned open (`philogg-stats-collapsed` unset = expanded); no more separate show/hide toolbar button, the panel's own header toggle (titled "Statistics") is it. Both toolbars' now-empty Settings groups are gone: `#tableToolbar` is Actions-only again, `#plotToolbar` is back to Controls\|Actions. (3) Clicking a Plot mark (2D `#plotSvg` or 3D `#plot3dCanvas`) no longer jumps straight to the Filtered view — it now calls a new `revealInTableView(entry)` instead, landing on and selecting the corresponding row in the SAME node's Table view (that row's own double-click already goes on into Filtered from there, via the unchanged `revealInFilteredView`). (4) Ctrl+1-4 (`SHORTCUT_ACTIONS` ids renamed `viewFull`/`viewFiltered`/`viewStacked` -> `viewTab1`-`viewTab4`) no longer target fixed Full/Filtered/Stacked destinations — `jumpToViewTab(n)`/`currentViewTabsList()` now jump to whichever tab currently sits at position n in the View Selector, same order it renders in: `[Context, Filtered, Table, Plot]` in tabs layout (Table/Plot only when the active node is extraction-capable), or `[Stacked, Table, Plot]` once Stacked layout is already active — so Ctrl+4 is a no-op without Table/Plot, and Ctrl+3/4 are no-ops in Stacked layout without them either. Entering Stacked itself is no longer a Ctrl-shortcut destination (Settings -> "Context/Filtered display" is still how you get there). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 162 fully rewritten (162a-d: default pinned-open, Table-only visibility, header-toggle persistence, hover-peek); Group 159b rewritten for the Table-row jump instead of Filtered; Group 72 revised (Ctrl+3 no-op without a 3rd tab) plus new 72b (positional cycling on an extraction-capable node, in both tabs and Stacked layout); Group 177b's toolbar-grouping assertions updated for Table/Plot's Settings group removal; two other plot-click assertions (drag-to-zoom, axis-equal) and the rebindable-shortcuts-count assertion (18 -> 19) updated to match. **3554 passed, 0 failed** (3542 + 12).

- **Shortened button hover labels/tooltips across Controls, Settings, and Actions** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-requested). Most row-action labels (`TOOLBAR_ROW_ACTIONS`/`VIEWBAR_ROW_ACTIONS`) and `title` tooltips throughout the toolbars, Settings panel, and extraction table restated context a button's position/icon already makes obvious (e.g. "Filter after this (incl.)" -> "After", "Filter: time range — create a time-range filter spanning what's currently visible in the plot" -> "Time range"), so they're trimmed to the shortest unambiguous phrase — one word where that's enough. Kept unchanged: keyboard-shortcut suffixes (`(Ctrl+B)`), short functional hints (`middle-click to hide`), the float/int manual-condition examples (`[value:float>=10]`, …), and dynamic content (thread/location/method cell tooltips). Also unchanged: the right-click context-menu item labels and the keyboard-shortcut settings list, which are visible menu/list text, not hover-only tooltips. Purely string changes, no attribute names/IDs/logic touched — no test assertions reference the old literal text. **3546 passed, 0 failed** (unchanged pass count).

- **Fixed: Filter/View Toolbar hover-expand flickered or froze mid-animation at some edge positions** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-reported). Root cause: expand/collapse for `.row-action-hit`/`.tb-hit` keyed off plain `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` on each fixed-size hit-zone — but while a PRECEDING button in the row is expanding or collapsing, its growing/shrinking box keeps shoving the NEXT hit-zone back and forth under a stationary cursor, so that hit-zone's own `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` fired repeatedly off the currently-animating (shifting) geometry, visible as the state flickering between expanded/collapsed or getting stuck half-animated. Fixed by replacing the per-hit listener pair with `setupHitExpandGroups()`: hit-zones sharing a parent container are grouped, and a single `mousemove` listener on the container decides `.expanded` by testing the pointer against each hit-zone's rect — but that rect is measured and cached only while the whole group sits collapsed (never refreshed again until the group returns to fully collapsed), so the decision always uses the stable, at-rest geometry, never geometry that's mid-animation because a neighbor is resizing. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 176a and Group 180a's hover assertions rewritten to dispatch `mousemove` (with a stubbed collapsed-state `getBoundingClientRect()` on the target hit-zone) against the shared container instead of `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` directly on the hit-zone. **3546 passed, 0 failed** (unchanged pass count — same assertions, different dispatch mechanism).

- **Fixed: View Toolbar icons looked shifted right at rest** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-reported follow-up to the rectangle-expand mechanic). Root cause: `.toolbar-icon-btn:has(.tb-hit)` overrode `justify-content` to `flex-start` (needed, it seemed, for the label to sit to the right of the icon once expanded) — but this stylesheet's global `*{box-sizing:border-box}` means a 28px button's actual content box is only 26px (28px minus its 1px border on each side), so the 28px `.tb-hit` inside it always overflows by 2px somewhere. With the base rule's `justify-content:center` (what `.row-action-btn` already relies on, unchanged), that 2px overflow is split evenly and clipped symmetrically on both sides — invisible. `flex-start` instead clipped only the right edge, visibly shifting the icon off-center. Fix: dropped the `justify-content` override entirely — `width:auto` on expand leaves no slack for `justify-content` to distribute either way, so centering was never actually needed for the expand mechanic to work; removing it restores symmetric clipping at rest, same as `.row-action-btn`. Pure CSS fix, no structural/DOM changes — no new tests needed.

- **New setting: button-label visibility (Never/On hover/Always), separate for the Filter-Toolbar and the View Toolbars** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-requested — a real Settings -> Behavior setting, not a per-toolbar control). Two new selects, `#settingsFilterToolbarLabels` and `#settingsViewToolbarLabels`, each persisted to their own localStorage key (`philogg-filter-toolbar-labels`/`philogg-view-toolbar-labels`, same plain-preference wiring shape as `settingsFilterActivationView`) and applied as a class on `<body>` (`filter-toolbar-labels-never`/`-always`, `view-toolbar-labels-never`/`-always`) rather than per-button state — a single change instantly affects every button of that kind. "On hover" (the default) needs no extra CSS at all, since it's just last session's hover-expand mechanics with no override present; "Never"/"Always" add higher-specificity CSS rules that force every relevant button permanently collapsed or expanded, overriding `.expanded`/`:focus-visible` regardless of actual mouse/focus state. The Filter-Toolbar setting targets `.row-action-btn:not(.rect)` (the circle/pill row-actions in `#viewBar`); the View Toolbar setting targets `.row-action-btn.rect` and `.toolbar-icon-btn:has(.tb-hit)` together (every button across `#contextToolbar`/`#filteredToolbar`/`#tableToolbar`/`#plotToolbar`). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 181** covers the defaults, independent persistence, and independent `<body>` class toggling for both settings. **3546 passed, 0 failed** (3530 + 16).

- **View Toolbars get the same hover-expand mechanic as the Filter-Toolbar's row-actions, but rectangle instead of pill** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-requested). `#contextToolbar`/`#filteredToolbar`/`#tableToolbar`/`#plotToolbar`'s plain icon buttons (display toggles, Context's match-nav/expand-collapse, the Statistics toggles, Export as CSV, Save as image, Fullscreen) now reveal a hover label the same way `.row-action-btn` does in `#viewBar`, but expand into a rounded rectangle (the toolbar's ordinary 7px corner radius) instead of a pill — only the Filter-Toolbar's own row-actions become pills. New `makeToolbarBtnExpandable()` wraps every such button, once, near the end of the script (after every icon has already been assigned), into a `.tb-hit`/`.tb-label` pair reusing the same fixed-hit-zone `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` idea `.row-action-hit` already established, scoped in CSS via `.toolbar-icon-btn:has(.tb-hit)` so it never affects a `.toolbar-icon-btn` elsewhere that was never wrapped. `buildRowActionsHtml` gained a `shape` argument (`"circle"`, the default, for `VIEWBAR_ROW_ACTIONS`/Plot's viewport filters; `"rect"` for `TOOLBAR_ROW_ACTIONS`) so the duplicated Bookmark/Add note/Add to selection copies get a new `.row-action-btn.rect` modifier overriding the border-radius back to 7px throughout, reusing the rest of the existing circle/pill markup and JS unchanged. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 151's "icon-only, explained by a tooltip" assertion updated (every button in `#contextToolbar` now carries a non-empty, hover-revealed `.textContent`, not just `.row-action-btn` copies); new **Group 180** covers the `.tb-hit`/`.tb-label` wrapping and expand/collapse behavior across all four toolbars, and that the duplicated Actions-group copies carry `.rect` while the Filter-Toolbar's own row-actions don't. **3530 passed, 0 failed** (3514 + 16).

- **Toolbar reorganization corrections: Add to selection is an Action, Save as image is an Action, per-tab statistics visibility, reversed time-filter arrows, Controls\|Settings\|Actions order** (this session, 2026-09-05, person-requested corrections to the previous sessions' toolbar reorganization). (1) "Add to selection" moved from `VIEWBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` (the Filter-Toolbar's filter-creating row-actions) into `TOOLBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` (the log views' own Actions group, alongside Bookmark/Add note) — it opens a popup rather than creating a filter node itself, same as those two; `#viewBar`'s row-actions group is now four buttons, not five. (3) Column-statistics visibility is now a per-tab **Setting** instead of one shared collapse chevron: the old `#extractStatsToggle`/`philogg-extract-stats-collapsed` mechanism is gone, replaced by independent `#statsToggleTable` (new Settings group in `#tableToolbar`) and `#statsTogglePlot` (new Settings group in `#plotToolbar`), each persisted to its own localStorage key (`philogg-table-stats-visible`/`philogg-plot-stats-visible`) and driving the same shared `#extractStatsBar` element via `updateExtractStatsVisibility()` (called from the same three hook points as `updateViewBarRowActions()`). - **Plot tab: swap the Filter-Toolbar's generic row-actions for Plot's own viewport filters; every view toolbar's icon buttons back to full size** (this session, 2026-09-04, two person-requested follow-ups). They're now hidden entirely while on Plot, replaced by a second `#viewBar` group (`[data-row-actions="plot-viewbar"]`) holding Plot's own two viewport-based filter buttons (moved up out of `#plotToolbar`'s own now-removed Actions group), shown only on the Plot tab and only once something is actually plotted in 2D — `updateViewBarRowActions()` (called from `applyFhView()`/`renderMainView()`/`initFhView()`) picks exactly one of the two groups; `setPlot2dToolsVisible()` now drives that same 2D-availability check instead of toggling a separate Actions group/separator directly. - **Three toolbar follow-ups: hide unavailable Table/Plot tabs, move Bookmark/Note into the view toolbars, group every view toolbar as Settings\|Controls\|Actions** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). (2) Bookmark this row/Add note moved out of `#viewBar`'s row-actions group into each log view's own toolbar (`#contextToolbar`/`#filteredToolbar`) as that toolbar's new Actions group — they mutate the selection's bookmark/note state rather than creating a filter, so the Filter-Toolbar (`#viewBar`, now holding only the five actions that genuinely create a filter node) was the wrong home for them; `ROW_ACTIONS` split into `TOOLBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` (bookmark/note, rendered into `[data-row-actions="toolbar-actions"]`, duplicated into both log-view toolbars the same way the six display toggles already are) and `VIEWBAR_ROW_ACTIONS` (the remaining five, into `[data-row-actions="viewbar"]`) via a shared `buildRowActionsHtml()` helper. (3) Every view toolbar's top-level `.toolbar-group` children now carry `data-toolbar-group="settings"|"controls"|"actions"`, in that fixed order with the existing `.ctx-toolbar-sep` `|` divider between whichever groups a toolbar actually has (no label, no dangling separator): Context = Settings\|Controls\|Actions (Prev/Next-match and Expand/Collapse merged into one Controls group instead of two separately-separated ones); Filtered = Settings\|Actions; Table = Actions only; Plot = Controls\|Actions — Plot's Controls group folds Fullscreen/Save in with the (still conditionally hidden) zoom controls, and a new `#plotFilterActionsGroup`/`#plotActionsSep` pair carries the two filter-creating buttons out into their own Actions group, both toggled together via a new `setPlot2dToolsVisible()` helper (replacing three separate direct `plot2dToolsGroup.classList` call sites). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 158a and Group 18 updated for tab-hiding instead of tab-disabling; Group 151 excuses `.row-action-btn` from its "icon-only, no text content" assertion (its label text is a real DOM child, just CSS-collapsed at rest); Group 176a's `expectedActions` trimmed to the five that stay in `#viewBar`; new Group 177 covers bookmark/note's duplicated new home and every toolbar's group order/labeling. **3494 passed, 0 failed** (3477 + 17).

- **Row-action circles: combined layout-shifting growth with the circle shape** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested — third iteration of the same feature). The previous (second) iteration fixed the left-to-right overshoot bug by never resizing the circle at all, floating the expanding label over later circles purely visually — but that also undid the person's original ask that expanding should push later buttons aside. Reconciled both asks: `.row-action-hit`, a new fixed 28x28 `<span>` wrapping just the icon, is now the actual `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` target (plain JS listeners toggling an `.expanded` class on the parent button), instead of a CSS `:hover` on the button itself — the button (`.row-action-btn`) is free to grow its own `width` again on `.expanded`/`:focus-visible` (pushing later circles rightward, as asked), while expand/collapse still keys off the fixed, never-moving `.row-action-hit` circle specifically, so leaving it collapses the pill immediately regardless of how wide the button has grown — the exact same overshoot bug is avoided without giving up the layout-shifting look. `updateRowActionButtons()` also force-clears a stuck `.expanded` class if a button becomes disabled while the mouse happens to be sitting over its circle (e.g. selection changed via keyboard). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 176a extended — asserts the icon is wrapped in `.row-action-hit`, that dispatching `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` on it toggles `.expanded` correctly, and that a button disabled while `.expanded` gets it force-cleared. **3477 passed, 0 failed** (3465 + 12).

- **Fixed: row-action circles overshot their neighbor when read left-to-right** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported follow-up, with a sketch, to the circle/pill hover feature below). The previous version grew the BUTTON's own `width` on hover — a flex sibling's growth shifts every later sibling further right by the same amount, so moving the cursor rightward across the row hovered circle N, which then pushed circle N+1 out from under the arriving cursor, landing on N+2 instead. Fixed by never resizing the circle itself: `.row-action-label` is now a separately, absolutely positioned pill-shaped span sharing the circle's own `left:0`/curve (so the two shapes still read as one seamless pill, per the person's sketch) that floats OVER later circles instead of pushing them aside, with `pointer-events:none` so hovering the visually-overlapping label hit-tests straight through to whatever real, unmoved circle is physically there — leaving circle N's real box immediately un-hovers it and raises N+1's label the instant the cursor crosses into N+1's own box. `z-index:2` on `:hover`/`:focus-visible` keeps the active label painted above later-DOM circles; the label markup now sits before the `<svg>` icon in `ROW_ACTIONS_HTML` so the icon stays on top of its own (now underlying) label. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 176a's existing structural assertions (`.row-action-btn` class + `.row-action-label` text matches `title`) needed no changes — this was a pure CSS/markup-order fix with the same DOM attributes. **3465 passed, 0 failed** (unchanged pass count — no test behavior affected).

- **Row-action buttons: circle at rest, pill-with-label on hover** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). The Filter-Toolbar's seven row-action buttons (Bookmark this row/Add note/Filter after this/Filter before this/Filter for this message/Time filter from selection/Add to selection) now render as plain circles (`.row-action-btn`, `border-radius:50%` on the 28x28 box) — the same 14px radius `.level-btn`'s own rounded end-caps have — showing only the icon. Hovering (or keyboard-focusing) turns a button into a pill exactly like `.level-btn`'s shape and reveals a text label next to the icon (the button's own short description, e.g. "Filter after this (incl.)"), via a `max-width`/`opacity` CSS transition. Label and native `title` tooltip now come from one shared `ROW_ACTIONS` data array (`{action, label, svg, strokeWidth}`) that builds `ROW_ACTIONS_HTML`, instead of the title string being hand-duplicated. No JS behavior changed — click handling and `updateRowActionButtons()`'s enable/disable logic are untouched. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 176a extended — every row-action button now also asserted to carry `.row-action-btn` and a `.row-action-label` span whose text matches its `title` exactly (the hover/pill CSS itself isn't tested, per the suite's existing "jsdom has no layout engine" convention for presentation-only states). **3465 passed, 0 failed** (3444 + 21).

- **Toolbar reorganization follow-up: breadcrumb placement + a real "Filter-Toolbar"** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested refinement of the toolbar reorganization below). Two corrections: (1) `#breadcrumbBar` now sits **between the timeline minimap and `#viewBar`**, not below `#viewBar` as the previous change had it. (2) The row-actions button group (Bookmark this row/Add note/Filter after this/Filter before this/Filter for this message/Time filter from selection/Add to selection) moved OUT of being duplicated inside `#contextToolbar`/`#filteredToolbar` and into `#viewBar` itself, right of the level filter — `#viewBar` is now genuinely a "Filter-Toolbar": every filter-CREATING control (view toggle, level filter, row-actions) in one universal row, identical across all four tabs including Table/Plot, while `#contextToolbar`/`#filteredToolbar` keep only display/navigation controls (the six log-display toggles, Context's match-nav/expand-collapse). Buttons in this group stay `#viewBar`'s own 28x28 size (not the 22x22 the per-view toolbars use), matching the view toggle/level pills next to them. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 26 gets a DOM-order assertion (minimap → `#breadcrumbBar` → `#viewBar`) plus a row-actions-visible-in-`#viewBar` check on the Table tab; Group 176 rewritten for the single-copy-in-`#viewBar` architecture (was: duplicated into both log-view toolbars). **3444 passed, 0 failed** (3447 net -3, from removing the now-redundant duplicate-copy assertions).

- **Toolbar reorganization: one consistent structure across all four views** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested "Gesamtkonzept" — full follow-up to the status-strip/empty-toolbars change below). `#viewBar` is now reduced to just the view toggle + level quick-filter, identical on every tab including Table/Plot (`#levelBar` no longer hides there — it already applied via `applyLevelFilter()`, just without a visible pill row before this session). The breadcrumb moved out of `#viewBar` into its own always-reserved `#breadcrumbBar` row. The six log-display toggles (Pin bookmarks/Notes/Multiline/Columns/Text-match-highlight/Highlight-match-text) moved out of `#viewBar` into each log view's own toolbar — five of them into `#contextToolbar`, all six into `#filteredToolbar` (Pin only affects the Filtered result set, so it's not duplicated into Context) — as shared `.toggle-*` classes rather than unique ids, since each now exists twice in the DOM with one shared state (new `elAll()` helper, `[...document.querySelectorAll(sel)]`, replacing the old single-element lookups everywhere these six are read/written). A new row-actions button group — Bookmark this row, Add note, Filter after this, Filter before this, Filter for this message, Time filter from selection, Add to selection — is now permanently visible (disabled instead of hidden when not applicable) in both `#contextToolbar` and `#filteredToolbar`, built from one shared HTML fragment and wired to the exact same underlying functions the pre-existing right-click context menu already used, just fed the current selection (`state.selectedId`/`state.logMultiSelect`) instead of the right-clicked row. The Table view's toolbar gained an "Export as CSV…" button (second entry point alongside the existing right-click menu item); Plot's two filter buttons switched from text labels to the same icon-button look everything else uses. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 176 (new)** covers the row-action buttons' disabled/active state across 0/1/2+ row selections in both toolbars, that clicking one produces the same effect as its context-menu equivalent (bookmark toggle, note dialog, timerange filter creation, add-to-selection popup), Plot's icon-button conversion, and the Table export button; Groups 4/26/27/28/29/29b updated for the new structure (`.toggle-*` selectors instead of ids, `#breadcrumbBar` instead of `#breadcrumb` inside `#viewBar`, level bar staying visible on Table/Plot, the six toggles' new 22x22 size in their toolbar homes instead of 28x28). **3447 passed, 0 failed** (3395 + 52).

- **Removed the status strip; Filtered and Table views get their own toolbar row** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested, first step of a larger UI toolbar pass). `#statusStrip`/`renderStatusStrip()` ("N of M shown" above the table) is gone entirely, no replacement. Context and Plot already had a real toolbar row of their own (`#contextToolbar`, `#plotToolbar`); Filtered and Table now get the same pattern — `#filteredToolbar` inside `#tableWrap` (under `#tableHeader`, above `#tableBody`) and `#tableToolbar` inside `#extractWrap` (above `#extractScroll`, toggled by `switchExtractView()` the same way `#extractScroll`/`#plotWrap` already are). Both are just the empty row for now — content/buttons land in a later session. In Stacked layout, Context's and Filtered's toolbars are both on screen at once, since the two panels already render together there. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 175 (new)** checks `#statusStrip` is gone, `#filteredToolbar` is visible in the Filtered tab and in Stacked alongside `#contextToolbar`, and `#tableToolbar` shows only on the Table tab (hidden on Plot); Groups 4/27/28 updated to drop their now-obsolete status-strip assertions (27/28 use `.pinned-row` counts instead). **3395 passed, 0 failed** (3387 + 8).

- **Fixed: renaming an extraction table column was impossible while its file was being tailed** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported: F2/right-click "Rename column…" jumped straight back out of edit mode). Root cause: `onTailChange` (fired by `tailTick` on every new chunk of a live-tailed file) calls `render()` unconditionally, which reaches `renderExtractTable` and rebuilds `extractHead.innerHTML` from scratch — including while a rename `<input>` was focused. Detaching a focused element fires `blur` on it synchronously, and the input's own blur handler commits/cancels the rename, so any tail tick landing mid-edit ended it before the user had typed anything; a fast-growing file made this happen on essentially every keystroke. `renderExtractTable` now checks upfront whether the currently-focused element is the live rename input for `renamingColIndex` and, if so, skips rebuilding the header entirely (row data below still refreshes normally) — the input keeps its focus, caret and in-progress value until the user actually commits (Enter/blur) or cancels (Escape). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 157 extended** — 157c (new) simulates a `renderExtractTable` call landing mid-rename (standing in for a tail tick's `render()`) and asserts the same input survives with its in-progress value and focus intact, then still commits normally afterward. **3387 passed, 0 failed** (3384 + 3).

- **New context-menu action: "Time filter from selection"** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). Right-clicking with a 2+ row multi-selection (`state.logMultiSelect`) active now also offers `#ctxTimeRangeFromSelection`, alongside the existing "Filter after/before this" pair — hidden for a single selected/right-clicked row, same threshold `currentSelectionRowIds` already uses for "Add to selection". Clicking it creates one unified `"timerange"` filter node (`createFilterNode(state.activeId, "timerange", {from, to})`) whose `from`/`to` are the earliest and latest `ts` among the selected rows' entries (looked up via `entryIndex`), inclusive both ends — so all rows between them, not just the selected ones, end up in the resulting filter. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 174** (new) — the item is hidden for a single-row selection; with 3 rows multi-selected out of click order, the created node's `from`/`to` match the actual min/max ts (not first/last clicked) and the filter keeps every entry in that inclusive range; the context menu closes afterward. **3384 passed, 0 failed** (3374 + 10).

- **Table/Plot now inherits upward through narrowing/context filters, stopping at link filters** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). Previously `nodeHasExtractableWildcards(node)` — "does this node itself carry a `[value:...]`/`[*]` pattern" — was the sole gate for showing a node's Table/Plot views, so a timerange/idset/level/context/... filter created under an extraction-pattern node lost Table/Plot entirely, even though it conceptually should still show the (now further-filtered) extraction table/plot. New `findExtractionAncestor(node)` walks the parent chain for the nearest extraction-pattern ancestor, stopping (returning `null`) the moment a `"link"` filter is hit (its paired entries have a different shape, so nothing above it carries through) or the root is reached; `nodeIsExtractionView(node) = !!findExtractionAncestor(node)` is the boolean drop-in that now gates Table/Plot tab enablement, the Filtered-vs-extraction scroller pick, and remembered-view restoration, replacing `nodeHasExtractableWildcards` at those call sites (which itself is unchanged and still used directly for the tree's own `TBL` tag/icon and filter-editing UI, both about the node's *own* pattern specifically). `renderExtractTable` now resolves the pattern-bearing ancestor once and pulls `compileExtractPattern(...)`/`ignoredColumns` from it while still building rows from the *active* node's own (narrowed/extended) `getEntries(node.id)` — so a timerange filter under an extraction node shows the same columns, fewer rows. Pure live lookup, no new persisted field — see `docs/filters.md` → "Filter rules: the three movement classes, and Table/Plot upward-lookup inheritance" for the full model. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 173** (new) — a timerange filter under an extraction node inherits Table/Plot with the ancestor's pattern applied to the child's own narrowed entries; a filter under a link filter does not inherit past it; a plain narrowing chain with no extraction ancestor stays unaffected (regression baseline). **3374 passed, 0 failed** (3359 + 15).

- **CSV export: new "Include full log entry column" checkbox** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested; refined same session to carry the whole entry, not just its matched message text). `#csvExportDialog` gets a new checkbox (`#csvExportFullEntryInput`, default **off**) alongside the existing "include header row" one; when checked, `buildExtractCsv` appends one extra trailing column (header "Log entry") per row carrying that row's `entry.raw` — the entry's complete original line (timestamp, level, thread, etc.), not just the message text the extraction pattern's regex matched against — through the same `csvQuoteField` RFC4180 escaping as every other field. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 172** (new) — the checkbox defaults to unchecked; left unchecked, no extra column is exported; checked, the header gains "Log entry" and each row's appended cell carries the entry's full raw line. **3359 passed, 0 failed** (3355 + 4).

- **Desktop (Tauri): dragging a filter tree row no longer triggers the "Drop Logfiles to Load" overlay** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported). Root cause: the desktop wrapper's native drag-drop handler (`WindowEvent::DragDrop`, `windows.rs`) fires `Enter`/`Over`/`Leave` for *any* drag the webview sees — including `philogg.html`'s own in-app HTML5 drag for reparenting a filter tree row — not only a real OS file drag, so it unconditionally showed the page's `#dropOverlay` via `window.philoggDropOverlay(true)` regardless of what was being dragged. `handle_drag_drop` now only trusts `DragDropEvent::Enter`'s `paths: Vec<PathBuf>` field (empty for an in-app drag, populated for a real file drag) to decide, and remembers that verdict in a `Cell<bool>` captured by the window-event closure so the `Over`/`Leave`/`Drop` events later in the same drag (which carry no `paths` of their own) agree with it. Rust-only change (`desktop/src-tauri/src/windows.rs`); nothing in `philogg.html` or the plain-browser build is affected. No `cd tests && npm test` run — out of scope per `CLAUDE.md` (nothing here is loaded/parsed by the jsdom suite); this environment has no GTK dev headers installed for a `cargo build`/`cd desktop && npm run build`, so verify with a real desktop build before shipping.

- **"Filter for this ___" no longer loses a multi-line message's newline** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported follow-up to the dotAll fix below: even after that fix, the auto-generated pattern for a multi-line message still found nothing). Root cause: `#filterInput` is a single-line `<input type="text">`, whose value-sanitization algorithm silently strips `"\r"`/`"\n"` from anything assigned to `.value` — `openFilterForEntryColumn` built its pattern straight from the entry's real (possibly multi-line) column text, so the newline never survived into the input, and the eventually-submitted filter node no longer matched the message it was built from. New `collapseNewlinesToWildcard(pattern)` replaces each `\r\n`/`\r`/`\n` run with the bare `[*]` token before the pattern reaches `filterInput.value` — survives the input untouched, and (thanks to the dotAll fix below) still matches straight through the break. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 171** (new) — `collapseNewlinesToWildcard` unit behavior (a bare `\n` and a `\r\n` pair both collapse to one `[*]`); end-to-end through the real right-click "Filter for this message" menu action on a multi-line fixture entry (no raw newline ever reaches `#filterInput`, the generated pattern has one `[*]` per line break, and submitting it creates a `"text"` filter node that actually matches that entry). **3355 passed, 0 failed** (3349 + 6).

- **`[*]`/`[value:...]` wildcard patterns now match across an embedded newline** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported: a text filter's auto-generated/manual wildcard pattern for a multi-line message — e.g. a stack trace or a wrapped log line — never matched, because JS's `.`/`.*` doesn't match `"\n"` without the "s" (dotAll) flag). `compileExtractPattern`'s compiled `regex` now carries `"is"` instead of `"i"`; every other place that recompiles the same pattern from `regexStr` (`textFilterMatchSpec`, both `getEntriesUncached` `"text"` branches, the filter popup's `evaluateLiveMatch` live-count and `updateFilterPatternPreview`) appends `"s"` too, so matching, highlighting, and the live-match/preview UI all agree. No new UI: a plain (non-wildcard) substring search already matched across newlines fine (`String.includes`), and a wildcard's `.*` between fixed text now simply spans the line break like any other character — no separate newline token/wildcard was needed. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 170** (new) — a `[*]` pattern spanning a fixture entry's embedded newline matches via `compileExtractPattern`'s own regex, `getEntries` (as a real filter node), `wildcardMatch` (the highlighting path via `textFilterMatchSpec`), and the filter popup's live-match count. **3349 passed, 0 failed** (3341 + 8).

- **Entry detail format+highlight: each fragment block now renders on its own, left-aligned line** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). Previously a formatted `.syn-block` (`display:inline-block`) sat wherever it happened to fall inline with the surrounding message text, which could leave it indented under whatever preceded it on the same visual line. `formatMessageWithHighlight` now inserts a literal newline right before and right after each fragment's block (skipped when the message already put the fragment on its own line, or when the fragment sits at the very start/end of the message, to avoid a spurious blank line) — `#detailMessage`'s existing `white-space:pre-wrap` turns those into real line breaks, so the block's own `display:inline-block` starts flush at the new line's left edge with no CSS change needed. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 169 extended** — 169i (a newline precedes/follows each of two fragments in one message), 169j (no doubled newline when the source text already had one), 169k (no leading/trailing blank line for a fragment at the very start/end of the message). **3341 passed, 0 failed** (3336 + 5).

- **Entry detail format+highlight now defaults ON** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). Originally shipped off (opt-in); `initDetailFormatHighlightSetting` now treats an unset `localStorage["philogg-detail-format-highlight"]` key as `true` instead of `false` (an explicit choice, either way, is still read back and honored — nothing changes for anyone who already clicked the toggle once). Fixed an interaction the default-off design hadn't needed to consider: `updateDetailPanel()` previously let `detailFormatHighlightEnabled` win outright over text-match highlighting (`markFieldHtml`) whenever it was on, which was fine as an opt-in but would have silently hidden text-match marking in the entry detail for everyone once on-by-default, on every message with nothing to format (the common case). Now it only prefers the formatted output when `formatMessageWithHighlight` actually produced a fragment (a `syn-block` span present); otherwise it falls through to `markFieldHtml`/plain text exactly as before this change. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 169 extended** — 169a rewritten for the new default (toggle starts active with nothing written to `localStorage` yet, still toggles off/on and persists explicitly); 169b–e/g/h's now-redundant "click to turn on" step removed since the toggle already is on; two pre-existing tests elsewhere in the suite that relied on the old default-off behavior for entry-detail text-match marking pass unchanged now that the fallback fix is in. **3336 passed, 0 failed** (3336 + 0 net, same count as the previous session entry below — this was a same-day behavior tweak on top of it, not new coverage).

- **Entry detail format+highlight: .NET-style `ToString()` object dumps now detected too** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported follow-up to the feature below). Real-world messages like `Setting selected test procedure to 'Evaluate' with parameters 'TestProcedureParameters { Type = MyProgramRecipe, ViewerName = , MyProgramRecipeId = 4c382e97-... }'` are a common .NET `ToString()` dump shape — unquoted keys, `=` instead of `:`, blank values — and correctly failed `JSON.parse`, so the toggle left them as plain text even though a person would still want them formatted. `findEmbeddedFragments` now tries a third detector, `parseKeyValueDump`, once `isValidJson` has already rejected a `{...}` candidate: `splitTopLevelByComma` cuts the span on top-level commas only (nested `{ }`/`[ ]`/`"..."` inside a value don't get split), and every non-blank segment must fit `identifier = value` (`DUMP_KV_RE`), with at least one such segment — otherwise the candidate is left as plain text exactly as before, so a genuine code block (`if (x < y) { do(); other() }`, no `=`) or a segment that doesn't fit (`{ a = 1, just some text }`) still never misfires. `formatDumpFragmentHtml` renders the result flat (not recursively re-parsing a nested dump inside a value) reusing the existing `syn-key`/`syn-string`/`syn-punct` CSS classes — no new colors/CSS needed. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 169 extended** (169g/h) — the exact reported message now highlights (`syn-key`/`syn-string` on its `Key`/`Value` pairs, a blank value renders no stray span, surrounding text/quotes preserved); a code-block brace and a mixed-fit brace both still stay plain. **3336 passed, 0 failed** (3330 + 6).

- **Entry detail: "format + syntax highlight" toggle for embedded XML/JSON** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested). `#detailFormatToggle`, a single `.panel-toggle-btn` next to `#detailToggle` in `#detailPanelHeader`, turns on/off pretty-printing + syntax-highlighting for well-formed XML/JSON fragments embedded anywhere inside the entry detail message's free text (e.g. `Sending Control <ctrl><cmd>reset</cmd></ctrl> successful`, or `Response: {"status":"ok"} done`), leaving the surrounding text untouched — persisted as one global on/off (`localStorage["philogg-detail-format-highlight"]`), default off. Detection is two-pass, never trusting a regex alone: `findEmbeddedFragments` scans for candidate spans (a balanced `<tag>...</tag>`/`<tag/>` run, or a bracket-matched `{...}`/`[...]` span, string-aware) and validates each one — `DOMParser`/`"application/xml"` checked for a `parsererror` node, or `JSON.parse` — before ever treating it as a hit, so a stray `<`/`{` used as everyday punctuation is left as plain text. `formatXmlFragmentHtml`/`formatJsonFragmentHtml` re-walk the already-validated parsed structure into indented, `<span class="syn-*">`-wrapped HTML, `escapeHtml`-ing every real piece of text the same way the rest of the app does — only the wrapper `<span>` tags are real markup, so an unmatched/malformed fragment (however script-shaped) never becomes live DOM. New CSS custom properties `--syntax-tag`/`--syntax-attr-name`/`--syntax-attr-value`/`--syntax-string`/`--syntax-number`/`--syntax-key`/`--syntax-boolean-null`/`--syntax-punct` (`SYNTAX_COLOR_KEYS`, parallel to `THEME_COLOR_KEYS`) get fixed hardcoded defaults in `:root`/`:root[data-theme="light"]` only — every other theme (the four Catppuccin flavors, any custom/imported theme) is free to skip them entirely and inherit those defaults via the normal cascade, or to override them via an optional `syntaxHighlightColors` block in its JSON, applied the same optional way `applyTheme()` already applies `THEME_COLOR_KEYS`. `buildThemeTemplateJson()`'s downloaded template now seeds that block from the active theme's own computed colors plus a sibling `_syntaxHighlightColors_comment` hint string (JSON has no real comments); `importThemeJson()` treats the whole block, and each of its keys, as optional — never rejects a file for lacking it, unlike `THEME_COLOR_KEYS` — and drops the hint field on import rather than storing it. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 169** (new) — toggle default/persistence/gating; XML detection+highlight (tags, attribute names/values, nesting, self-closing); JSON detection+highlight (keys, string/number/boolean/null); conservative-detection cases that must stay plain text (`a < b`, `if (x < y) { ... }`, an unclosed tag, a malformed JSON span); XSS safety (an unmatched tag never becomes real DOM, a validated fragment's special characters are re-escaped not injected raw); the theme template/import round trip for the optional `syntaxHighlightColors` block, with and without it present. **3330 passed, 0 failed** (3295 + 35).

- **Link filter view: row click, Up/Down and Alt+Enter now act on the whole pair/tuple, not a single line** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported). Previously a click on a `.pair-row` did nothing at all (only the brace's own click selected the pair), Up/Down was a deliberate no-op in the Link view, and keyboard Alt+Enter wildcard-filtered whichever single real entry `state.selectedId` happened to still hold from an earlier row double-click — not the pair — because it read `entryIndex[state.selectedId]` unconditionally and a pair's synthetic id is never registered there. `buildPairBlock` now attaches the pair-selection click handler (toggle-select by index, same as before) to the whole `.pair-block` instead of only the `.pair-brace`, so clicking any row selects that row's pair. A new `moveLinkSelection(delta)` drives Up/Down through `linkSelectedPairIndex`, scrolling the target block into view via `linkBlockOffsets` and re-rendering the virtualized window; the arrow-key handler's former Link-view no-op branch now calls it instead. The global Alt+Enter handler checks for an active Link-view block selection first and, when present, calls `openFilterForEntryColumn` with the *pair* object (`linkPairsData[linkSelectedPairIndex].pair`) — byte-identical to what right-click brace → "Filter for this message" already produced, since that mechanism only ever worked because it was handed the pair object, not because of any bracket-specific code path — falling back to the previous single-entry path otherwise. Double-click on a row is untouched (`buildPairRow`'s own `dblclick` still jumps to that single line via `revealInHighlightView`). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 168** (new) — row click selects/toggles the whole block same as the brace, double-click still reveals a single entry and clears the block selection (re-selected via a row click afterward), Up/Down moves and clamps the block selection, and Alt+Enter produces the exact same wildcard pattern as right-click brace → "Filter for this message", covering both sides of the tuple. **3295 passed, 0 failed** (3280 + 15).

- **Folder watch: a file deleted outside the app now disappears from the listing** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-reported follow-up to the detail fixes below). Previously deliberately out of scope ("doesn't detect removed files"), but that left a stale entry — grayed or, worse, still rendered as a real open row — permanently in the listing once a file it pointed at was deleted on disk. `mergeScannedFiles` now drops ANY listing entry a fresh scan no longer finds, open or not, closing it first (`closeFolderFile`, same teardown as a person closing it by hand) if it was open, instead of only ever dropping unmatched *closed* entries. `folderScanTick`'s poll loop, which used to only run a merge when something NEW appeared (a pure removal changed nothing until the next unrelated new-file event), now also runs one when a previously known file goes missing from the scan. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 167** (new) — an open and a closed file both deleted outside the app vanish from `folder.files` on the next `folderScanTick`, the open one's node/entries/entryIndex fully released and no longer rendered anywhere; a subsequent no-op poll leaves the survivor untouched. **3280 passed, 0 failed** (3272 + 8).

- **Files & Filters / Folder watch: several person-reported detail fixes** (this session, 2026-09-04). 1) The tailed-file "live" indicator is a pulsing **file icon** now instead of a separate dot before it (`.tree-icon-live` replaces `.tree-live`) — the dot's own reserved space had misaligned a tailed row's label against its non-tailed siblings. 2) An opened folder-watch file's row now lines up flush with its still-closed (grayed) siblings instead of sitting extra-indented: `renderNode` takes a new `{ folderTopLevelNoChevron: true }` option (used only by `renderFolderSection` for a folder's own top-level rows) that matches the closed rows' 12px inset and skips reserving the chevron slot when the file has no filters of its own to collapse. The same session also fixed the brief "closed/grayed" flash a newly auto-opened file went through between its loading-progress row and its final open row: `renderFolderSection`'s "still loading" fallback used to require `typeof loadFraction === "number"`, which stopped matching for one render right after `loadOneFileIntoTree`'s own `finally` block clears `loadFraction` and renders — a render that lands *before* `loadFolderFile` (its caller) gets to set `rec.nodeId` on the very next line. Dropping that condition (matching by folder+name alone) closes the gap. 3) `applyFolderAutoRules`' auto-open-newest / auto-close-keep-N now act on a given file **at most once**: a new permanent `rec.autoOpenFired` flag (set the first time either rule opens a file) stops both rules from reopening a file the person has since closed by hand. Symmetrically, a new `rec.openedByAuto` flag (set by `loadFolderFile`'s new `auto` parameter, true only when an auto rule is the caller) means "auto-close — keep only N open" now only ever closes files an auto rule itself opened, never one the person opened manually — previously it would close *any* open match that fell outside the window, auto-opened or not. An auto-opened file's tree-row icon now also carries a small circled "(A)" badge (`ICON_FILE_AUTO`) and a tooltip note, so the two kinds of "why is this file open" are visually distinct. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 166** (new) — the live indicator moved onto the icon (and still goes stale correctly); an opened folder-watch row's inset/no-chevron-slot matches its closed siblings; auto-open firing once and staying closed after a manual close across a later rescan; a manually opened file surviving a keep-N window it falls outside of, with the icon/flags asserted directly. Group 164's manual-open + keep-N-enabled-later scenario updated in place — its old assertion that the manually opened oldest file gets auto-closed once keep-N is turned on no longer holds, superseded by the new "manual open protects from auto-close" behavior. **3272 passed, 0 failed** (3271 + 16, net +1 after the Group 164 update).

- **Off-main-thread log parsing via Web Worker, and concurrent multi-file loading** (this session, 2026-09-04, person-requested: typical logs run 50-100MB with 2-4 loaded at once, and JS parsing is CPU-bound — the main thread's own chunked loop only ever yields between chunks, it never runs two files' parses at once). `parseLogTextAsync` now tries a Worker first (falling back to the existing loop when `Worker` is unavailable, which is also why the regression suite — jsdom has no `Worker` — exercises the fallback path already): `buildLogParseWorkerSrc()` serializes the actual parsing functions via `Function.prototype.toString()` into a `Blob`, so the worker runs the exact same source as the main thread rather than a hand-copied mirror that could drift. Entry ids are reassigned on the main thread as each chunk streams back (`uidCounter`/`entryIndex` are both main-thread-only state a worker can't share). `loadFileDescriptors` now loads its whole batch **concurrently** (`Promise.all`) instead of one file at a time, so several large files loaded together actually parse on separate cores at once — every placeholder now flips from grayed to actively-loading together rather than in turn (see Group 68's addendum). Deliberately scoped to this: a full Rust/Tauri parallel-parsing path was considered and dropped in favor of Workers, since Workers need no Rust at all and work identically in the plain browser build and the desktop wrapper — no format-engine duplication into Rust, no divergence risk for PhiLogg's fully user-configurable log formats. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 165** (new) — concurrent multi-file loads produce correct, non-colliding entries/ids per file; the built worker source, run in a bare `vm` sandbox with no browser APIs, parses both the builtin default format and a custom regex-mode format identically to the main-thread fallback. Group 68a's "one file loads at a time" assertions updated to match the new concurrent-batch behavior. **3256 passed, 0 failed** (3236 + 20).

- **Folder watch settings, second follow-up: "newest" now uses real file mtime, not just filename order** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-reported: the keep-N sliding window still appeared to not open a new file). The previous fix (below) made "Auto-close — keep only N open" a genuine sliding window, verified with alphabetically-increasing filenames — but the underlying "which file is newest" heuristic was still pure filename order, which silently breaks for any real naming scheme where a chronologically newer file doesn't happen to sort last as a plain string (unpadded day-of-month, a rotated-log scheme where the *active* file has the smallest name, etc.) — exactly the kind of case that would look, from the outside, like "the new file just doesn't open." `scanFolderHandle` now fetches each pattern-matching file's real `file.lastModified` via one extra `getFile()` call per file — gated behind a new `folderSettingsNeedRecency()` check so a folder with no auto rules configured pays nothing extra on its 3s poll — and a new `sortFolderRecsByRecency()` (mtime when both sides have one, name order as fallback) replaces the plain-array order everywhere `applyFolderAutoRules` picks "the newest" (show-newest-M, close-keep-N's window, auto-open-newest). mtime isn't available for a natively listed (desktop wrapper) folder's files — `urlTailHandle.getFile()` returns a plain fetched `Blob`, which has no `lastModified` — so the name-order fallback still matters there. Also re-verified, this time end-to-end through the real dialog checkboxes rather than direct `folder.settings` mutation: "Show relative path" now confirmed to update an **already-open** file's shown name live in both directions (on → off → on) with no re-open needed, not just right after opening it — this was already correct from the first follow-up below, just not previously tested through the actual UI controls. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 164** (extended in place) — a `Z-old.log`/`A-new.log` mtime-vs-name-order case; the keep-N sliding window re-tested through manually-opened files + the real settings-dialog number input + the real `folderScanTick` poll (not a manual `rescanFolder()` call); "show relative path" toggled through the dialog's real checkboxes, live, in both directions, on an already-open file. **3236 passed, 0 failed** (3226 + 10).

- **Folder watch settings follow-up: "Auto-close — keep only N open" now slides its window instead of stalling, and a relative-path name now survives opening the file** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-reported, follow-up to the settings dialog below). Two bugs: (1) `applyFolderAutoRules`'s auto-close rule only ever closed excess open files — it never opened the newer file that pushed the count over N, so with 2 already open and a 3rd, newer match appearing, the new one silently stayed unopened instead of the intended "oldest closes, newest opens, the N newest stay open." Rewritten as a genuine sliding window: for a pattern with `autoCloseKeep` set, `recs.slice(-N)` (the N newest) is computed first, whichever of those aren't open yet are opened (awaited), THEN whatever is open but fell out of that window gets closed — so a new arrival correctly evicts the previous oldest. "Auto-open newest file" (no keep-N configured) is unchanged, and is now explicitly skipped for a pattern that also has `autoCloseKeep` set, since that pattern's own window already covers it. (2) `nodeDisplayName()` (the one function every name display — tree row, breadcrumb, tab — already reads through) didn't know about a folder-watch file's `relPath`, so a file opened with "Show relative path" on reverted to its bare filename the instant it was loaded, even though the still-grayed listing kept showing the full path. `nodeDisplayName` now looks up a `folderId`-tagged file node's owning folder and, when `includeSubfolders`+`showRelativePath` are both on, returns its listing record's `relPath` instead of `node.name` — display-only, exactly like the pre-existing `node.label` override for filter nodes, so the underlying `node.name` (identity, matching in `folder.files`) is untouched. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 164** (extended in place) — the sliding-window scenario (2 open, keep-2, a newer 3rd file arrives → opens, the previous oldest closes, the still-in-window one is left untouched) plus a "Show newest M" follow-on with two open files; `nodeDisplayName()` and the actual tree row both keep showing the relative path after double-clicking to open the file. **3226 passed, 0 failed** (3224 + 2 net, after rewriting 3 pre-existing assertions in place for the new fixture).

- **Folder watch: per-folder settings dialog — filename patterns (with per-pattern auto-open-newest/auto-close-keep-N/show-newest-M) and include-subfolders/show-relative-path** (this session, 2026-09-03, person-requested). A gear icon (`.folder-watch-settings`, `ICON_GEAR`) on each watched folder's header opens `#folderWatchSettingsDialog` — styled with the same `settings-page-card`/`settings-row`/`settings-switch` classes as the app-wide Settings dialog, just scoped to one folder. Configurable per folder: **filename patterns** (`folder.settings.patterns`, default one `"*"` pattern — files matching none of the configured globs, reusing the filename-rule glob engine `getCompiledGlob`, are filtered out on top of the existing fixed `.log` extension filter), each with its own **auto-open newest file** (opens+selects the pattern's alphabetically-last match as soon as it appears, tailing/auto-scroll included — same effect as a manual open, via the existing `loadFolderFile`/`loadOneFileIntoTree` path), **auto-close — keep only N open** (closes the oldest open matches beyond N via the existing `closeFolderFile`, so they stay listed grayed out, not removed), and **show M newest files** (drops *closed* listing entries older than the M newest — an open file is never dropped); and folder-wide **include subfolders** (recursive scan — `scanFolderHandle` now walks subdirectories when a handle exposes `values()` on them; a no-op under the desktop wrapper's native folder listing, which is a flat, non-recursive `read_dir`) with **show relative path** (shows `sub/App.log` instead of just `App.log` in the grayed listing once subfolders are included). All three settings persist with the folder record (IndexedDB `folders` store, `persistFolder`/`restoreWatchedFolders`) and every change re-persists + re-scans immediately (no separate Save button, same immediacy as the rest of Settings).
