  6. **`-tauri` dropped everywhere** (person-requested follow-up, same session: the app is unpublished, so there are no installs whose `settings.json` a moved config directory could orphan — which was the only thing holding this back). `desktop-tauri/` → `desktop/`, `docs/desktop-tauri.md` → `docs/desktop.md`, `tauri-release.yml` → `desktop-release.yml`; product name `PhiLogg Tauri` → **`PhiLogg`**, bundle identifier `com.kleinphilipp.philogg-tauri` → **`com.kleinphilipp.philogg`**, config directory `PhiLogg-Tauri/` → **`PhiLogg/`** (`~/.config/PhiLogg`, `~/Library/Application Support/PhiLogg`, `%APPDATA%\PhiLogg`), crate/bin/npm package `philogg-tauri` → `philogg-desktop`, release tag `tauri-<sha>` → `desktop-<sha>`, installers `PhiLogg-tauri-<sha>.<ext>` → `PhiLogg-<sha>.<ext>`. Names that describe the *framework* rather than the product are deliberately left alone: `src-tauri/`, `tauri.conf.json`, `data-tauri-drag-region`, the `#tauri-wc` window-control block `inject.js` injects, and the `tauri-plugin-*` dependencies. **Verified**: `cargo check` clean under the new crate name, `Cargo.lock` needed only its own package-name line changed (`cargo metadata` left it otherwise untouched), and the suite still reports 2785 passed, 0 failed against both the tracked and the stripped file.

- **Tauri wrapper: folder watch is listed natively — Desktop works, and so do macOS/Linux** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-reported: watching a folder like Desktop failed in the Tauri build the same way it does in Chrome, and the question was whether Tauri can simply allow it). **It can't, and no embedder can.** `philogg.html`'s folder watch went through `window.showDirectoryPicker()`, which is a *webview engine* API: Chromium refuses a directory handle for anything on its hardcoded sensitive-directory list (`ChromeFileSystemAccessPermissionContext` — Desktop and Downloads among them, hence the "this folder contains system files" error), the check runs inside the engine after the call, and there is no Tauri setting and no WebView2 browser flag that turns it off. The fix is to stop asking the webview: this wrapper now lists watched folders **in Rust**, which has no blocklist. That is `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #61, and it closes all three of its gaps at once — the blocklist, the fact that folder watch previously existed *only on Windows* (WKWebView and WebKitGTK don't implement the API at all), and the missing path on files opened from a watched folder.
  1. **Two new Rust commands, one new stand-in.** `pick_folder` runs the OS folder dialog (same Rust-driven `tauri-plugin-dialog` route as `pick_files`, so still no ACL entry — `capabilities/default.json` is unchanged), `list_folder(path, extensions)` does a non-recursive `read_dir` filtered by the extensions the *page* considers loadable and registers each match like any other file the wrapper opens. On the page side, `nativeDirHandle()` is a duck-typed stand-in for a `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` — `name`, `values()`, `queryPermission()`, `requestPermission()`, the four members the folder-watch code actually uses — so `scanFolderHandle`, `mergeScannedFiles`, `rescanFolder`, `folderScanTick` and `tryReconnectFolder` are untouched. Each listed entry *is* a `urlTailHandle` (now carrying the file's path alongside its URL), so tailing comes along for free, exactly as it does for a picked or dropped file.
  2. **Folder-watched files finally know where they are.** They were the last route in `desktop-tauri/` with no `node.localPath`, i.e. the only files there without "Open File Location"/"Copy Path". A natively listed file arrives with both halves, applied by the new `applyFolderFileLocation()` on load *and* on every rescan — the ids behind `philogg://local/<id>/…` are per-process, so a session restored from cache holds the previous run's dead URLs until the first scan re-registers them.
  3. **A dropped folder starts a watch again.** The previous session had accepted losing that (the native drag-drop handler carries paths but no directory handle); a path is now exactly what a watch wants, so the "use Open… → Folder…" notice is gone for this wrapper and stays only for one that can't list folders.
  4. **Persistence.** A stand-in holds a closure, which structured clone rejects outright — the same trap the `urlTailHandle` cache bug fell into. `persistFolder` stores the folder's **path** instead of the handle and `restoreWatchedFolders` rebuilds the stand-in from it; a rebuilt one answers `queryPermission()` with `"granted"`, so a restored native folder resumes silently and never shows the "Reconnect" button (a path, unlike a handle, needs no permission re-grant). A real `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` still travels as itself, unchanged.
  5. **`register_local_file` now dedupes by path.** It minted a fresh id per call; with a rescan every 3 s per watched file that would have grown the map without bound and handed the page a new URL for a file it was already tailing.
  **Verified**: `cargo check` clean; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` gained **Group 145** (picker delegation with no `showDirectoryPicker` present at all, the path/URL on a loaded folder file plus its context-menu items, rescan-picks-up-new-file and listing-failure, the dropped folder, and the two-window persistence round-trip proving the path is stored and the per-process URLs are refreshed). Group 143b was re-pointed at "a wrapper that *can't* list folders" and Group 139's framing corrected — no route under this wrapper is pathless any more. **Not run on a real Windows/macOS desktop**, per this wrapper's usual status caveat; the headless splash deadlock documented in `desktop-tauri/README.md` still makes a GUI run impossible from here. The Electron wrapper hits the same Chromium blocklist and is *not* touched here — it has its own escape hatch (`session.on("file-system-access-restricted")`), filed as `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #62. 2758 passed, 0 failed.

- **CI: workflow actions bumped off the deprecated Node 20 runtime** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-reported). Every workflow run was warning `Node.js 20 is deprecated. The following actions target Node.js 20 but are being forced to run on Node.js 24: actions/checkout@v4` — GitHub already runs those actions on Node 24 regardless, so the warning is about the pin being stale, not about a broken build. `actions/checkout@v4` → `@v5` and `actions/setup-node@v4` → `@v5` (the first versions declaring `runs: using: node24`) across all three workflows: `release.yml`, `desktop-release.yml`, `tauri-release.yml`. `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` in `tauri-release.yml` was checked and already declares `node24` at that tag — left alone. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` is a composite action with no JS runtime at all. While there: the `node-version:` the two desktop workflows hand `setup-node` (the toolchain that builds the wrappers, unrelated to the runtime the *actions* run on) went 20 → 22, Node 20 having reached end-of-life in April 2026. No product code touched — `philogg.html`, `tests/` and both wrappers are unchanged, so the regression suite wasn't re-run.

- **Tauri wrapper: "Open File Location"/"Copy Path" now work for picked and dropped files, and a new "Copy Path" action everywhere** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-requested — the Tauri build could only reveal files opened by double-click, and the question was whether a permission grant would close that or whether it needed extra interaction). Neither: the wrapper's `reveal_path` command already worked and its own `#[tauri::command]`s need no ACL entry at all (`capabilities/default.json` is unchanged by this). What was missing was *knowing the path* — no system webview resolves a `File` back to an OS location, which is exactly what Electron's `webUtils.getPathForFile` does and why that function has always returned `null` there. The fix routes around the limitation instead of fighting it: **the wrapper opens the files itself**, so the path is known before `philogg.html` ever sees them — generalizing the route a `.log` file-association open already took.
  1. **Three routes, one mechanism.** "Open… → File(s)…" now defers to a new `philogg.pickFiles` bridge function that runs the OS dialog in Rust (`tauri-plugin-dialog`, driven from Rust — so still nothing added to the ACL); drag-and-drop is caught by Tauri's *native* handler (`WindowEvent::DragDrop` in `windows.rs`); file associations are unchanged. All three end at `state.register_local_file(path)` and hand the page both halves — the real path and the `philogg://local/<id>/…` URL the file is served under — through a new `window.philoggLoadLocalFiles({ files, folders })`. That builds ordinary load descriptors, so the queued placeholders, the multi-file merge prompt and tailing all behave exactly as they do for a dropped `File`; `node.localPath` and `node.sourceUrl` are both set, so the context menu offers everything it does under Electron. `loadFileDescriptors` gained support for a *lazy* descriptor (`name` + `openFile()`) so every placeholder is in the tree before the first byte is fetched, and now cleans up a placeholder whose read failed before `loadOneFileIntoTree` ever ran.
  2. **The drag-drop trade, reversed.** Tauri's native handler is the only one carrying OS paths, and enabling it suppresses the HTML drop events — the earlier version took the opposite side of that trade (`disable_drag_drop_handler()`) and accepted pathless drops. Two consequences, both handled: `philogg.html` never sees a `dragenter`, so the wrapper drives its drop overlay through a new named `window.philoggDropOverlay(show)` hook rather than reaching into `#dropOverlay`; and **a dropped folder can no longer start a folder watch** (that needs a live `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` to list and rescan, which a path can't produce) — folders are reported in the same call and the page points at "Open… → Folder…" instead. Accepted with the person up front. Folder watch stays this wrapper's one pathless route — `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #61.
  3. **New "Copy Path" context-menu action**, in *both* wrappers, on exactly the same gate as "Open File Location": a known `node.localPath` goes straight to the clipboard; a `philogg://local/…` file — which the page knows only by that URL — has its path fetched back through a new `pathForLocalUrl(url)` bridge function (`path_for_local_url` in Tauri, `philogg:path-for-local-url` in Electron; the same id → path lookup `revealLocalUrl` does, handing the answer back instead of acting on it). Person-requested as the fallback that's useful even when revealing isn't — pasting a path into a terminal or a ticket.
  4. **Bugfix found on the way: every URL-opened file was silently missing from the session cache.** `persistFileNode` stashed `node.tail.handle` in the IndexedDB record; for a `?url=` deep-link or a file-association open that handle is a `urlTailHandle`, a plain object holding a closure, which structured clone rejects with a `DataCloneError` — and `cacheStoreOp` swallows the synchronous throw, so the *entire* record was quietly never written and the file vanished from "restore last session". This predates the Tauri wrapper and affected Electron too. URL-backed handles are no longer persisted (they're marked with a symbol-ish key and skipped) and are rebuilt from the already-persisted `sourceUrl` on restore — which also means those files now resume *tailing* after a reload, with no permission round-trip.
  **Verified**: the Rust side compiles clean; the page side is covered by new **Groups 141-144** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` (the pick/load route incl. tailing, the multi-file batch, the failed-read cleanup; both "Copy Path" variants and its absence in a plain build; the overlay hook and the dropped-folder report; the cache round-trip, confirmed to fail without the fix). Group 139's framing was re-pointed at the case that *is* still pathless there (folder watch) rather than left describing a route that no longer exists. **Not yet run on Windows** — two things to check there: that the internal filter-node drag-and-drop in the tree still reparents now that Tauri's native drop handler is on, and the native dialog/drop themselves. The drop half is a separate commit from the picker half so it can be reverted on its own. 2724 passed, 0 failed.

- **Tauri wrapper: two real-Windows bugfixes (window unmovable, empty window on last-file-close)** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-tested on Windows after setting the branch as a temporary default to trigger the tauri-release.yml build). Both root causes were places where a Tauri webview needs an explicit Rust round-trip that Electron gets for free.
  1. **The window couldn't be moved** — clicking empty toolbar space did nothing detectable at all. `data-tauri-drag-region` (which `inject.js` stamps onto `#toolbar`) is read by a script Tauri auto-injects into every window, which calls the core `start_dragging` command — and that command is deliberately *not* part of `core:window`'s default permission set (unlike `internal_toggle_maximize`, which is). `desktop-tauri/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json` now grants `core:window:allow-start-dragging` explicitly.
  2. **Closing the last open file** (`quitOnLastFileClose`) **left an empty, permanently-open dark window**, recoverable only via the tray's Quit. `philogg.html` calls the plain DOM `window.close()`, which Electron's `BrowserWindow` intercepts and turns into a real window close — a Tauri webview has no such interception, so the webview engine tore down the *page* (visibly "closing" the app) without ever notifying the Rust side, and `WindowEvent::CloseRequested` (and the close-to-tray decision behind it) never fired. `inject.js` now overrides `window.close` to `invoke("window_close")`, routing it through the same Rust-side path the injected title-bar close button already used.
  Verified: a temporary `visible(true)` + `xdotool` drag under Xvfb moved the window from (0,0) to (150,90); an `eval("window.close()")` smoke test confirmed the window hides (`is_visible() == false`) instead of staying open. `docs/desktop-tauri.md` and `desktop-tauri/README.md` → "Status" updated. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` unaffected (Rust/injected-JS only) — 2685 passed, 0 failed.

- **Second desktop wrapper: Tauri, running in parallel with Electron** (this session, 2026-09-01, person-requested). Asked for a Tauri wrapper alongside the existing Electron one — same feature set, same visuals (rounded corners, shortcuts, buttons in the current theme) — so it can be tested against it and, if it holds up, eventually replace it. New `desktop-tauri/` (Tauri v2 + Rust; `desktop/` is untouched and stays the supported build). Both install side by side: own identifier `com.kleinphilipp.philogg-tauri`, own product name, own config directory `PhiLogg-Tauri/` (a deliberate sibling of Electron's `PhiLogg/`, so comparing the two can't have one clobber the other's settings). `philogg.html` is not modified — same rule as `desktop/`.
  1. **Feature parity in one pass**, each mirroring `desktop/main.js`: the custom `philogg://` scheme (registered *asynchronously*, since a served log is re-read on every tail tick and a sync handler would block the webview thread), `.log` file associations and CLI-argument opening, single instance with the running window reused via `window.philoggLoadUrl`, splash window, tray (Open / Open Config Folder / Clear Cache / Quit), close-to-tray, `settings.json` mirroring, "Open File Location", the system font list, F11 fullscreen, frameless window with rounded corners.
  2. **Window chrome is real DOM, not a native overlay.** Electron gets minimize/maximize/close from Chromium's Window Controls Overlay; Tauri has no equivalent, so `inject.js` appends a three-button block into `philogg.html`'s own `.toolbar-right`, styled purely from the page's existing theme variables. That removes Electron's whole `watchTheme()` accent-polling loop (the buttons *are* themed elements, so nothing can go stale) along with `TITLEBAR_HEIGHT`'s 49px and the `titlebar-area-*` padding reservation (the buttons occupy real layout space). macOS keeps native traffic lights via `TitleBarStyle::Overlay`. The drag region needed rebuilding too: `-webkit-app-region` is inherited by children, `data-tauri-drag-region` is not, so the attribute is stamped on `#toolbar` and every descendant outside a control, re-stamped by a `MutationObserver`.
  3. **A `fetch` shim, because Tauri doesn't expose a real custom scheme everywhere.** What it registers as `philogg` is served as `philogg://localhost/…` on macOS/Linux and `http://philogg.localhost/…` on Windows — neither is the `philogg://local/…` shape `isDesktopLocalUrl()` matches to decide a URL is a tail-able local file. Rather than change `philogg.html`, the page is handed the canonical URL (keeping tail-follow, filename derivation and the context menu working unmodified) and `window.fetch` rewrites that one prefix to whatever the platform actually serves; the protocol handler accepts both shapes.
  4. **One injected script instead of preload + `dom-ready`.** Tauri's `initialization_script` runs before any page script *and* re-runs on every navigation, so the settings hydration, the bridge, the CSS and the DOM work all live in one file that survives the "Clear Cache" reload with no re-injection hook. The settings *read* half got simpler than Electron's synchronous-IPC preload: the values are known on the Rust side at window-creation time, so they are baked in as a JSON literal. Hydration is once per *process* (a nonce in `sessionStorage`), not per load, so a reload can't revert a setting changed since startup. The *write* half keeps Electron's ~1x/second diffed poll. Close-to-tray also became synchronous — the flag is already mirrored into Rust state and seeded from `settings.json`, so no `executeJavaScript()` round-trip at close time.
  5. **One accepted gap**: `getPathForFile` always returns `null` there. No system webview can resolve a `File` back to an OS path, and Tauri's own path-carrying drag-drop event can only be enabled by suppressing the HTML drop events `philogg.html` needs to receive files at all — so dropping files keeps working and "Open File Location" is simply not offered for picker/drop-loaded files. File-association opens still reveal, through `revealLocalUrl`. Two smaller ones: macOS font names are approximated from font filenames (Linux `fc-list` and Windows PowerShell are exact), and the maximize button doesn't swap its glyph.
  6. **Release**: new `.github/workflows/tauri-release.yml`, structurally a copy of `desktop-release.yml` (manual dispatch, per-OS checkboxes turned into the matrix by the same `jq` step, `prepare` job creating tag `tauri-<sha>`, `fail-fast: false`, 5-attempt upload retry) rather than an extra dimension inside it — the Electron release path has to keep working. Linux builds on `ubuntu-22.04` for glibc reasons and installs the WebKitGTK dev packages; the upload step renames bundles to `PhiLogg-tauri-<sha>.<ext>` since Tauri has no `artifactName` template.
  **Verified end to end on Linux** (WebKitGTK under a headless X server): the page loads through the custom scheme, a `.log` passed on the command line is fetched and then re-fetched on every tail tick, `settings.json` is written, and a second launch with another `.log` exits immediately and routes its file into the running window. **Not yet run on Windows or macOS** — the window chrome is the part most likely to need adjustment there, which is what the wrapper is for; the macOS-only code paths aren't even compile-checked from this environment. New docs `docs/desktop-tauri.md` (written as a delta against `docs/desktop.md`) and `desktop-tauri/README.md`; `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #60 is the "does Tauri replace Electron" decision this defers. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 139** (a wrapper whose `getPathForFile` only ever returns `null` — exercising the *load* path Group 109 only ever stubbed) and **Group 140** (the `#toolbar` structure/50px-height contract both wrappers' injected chrome is keyed off, a tripwire for a rename that would silently break window chrome nothing else tests). 2685 passed, 0 failed.

- **Context view follow-up: matches full width, revealed context indented (not dimmed), binary gap fold with icon-button navigation** (this session, 2026-08-30, person-provided sketch). Feedback on the rework directly above: the visual didn't match the intent yet — a hand-drawn sketch specified full-width, unindented matches; context rows indented but shown at full opacity, not greyed out; two small fold-back icons (top and bottom of a revealed block) sitting in the indent the fold itself creates; a single indented, lightly dimmed placeholder row (an ellipsis icon plus a line count) standing in for a closed gap. Alongside that: the nav chip's buttons were too small/custom and repeated the filter's name for no reason, its position wasn't configurable, and Stacked layout should always start fully expanded.
  1. **Gaps are now binary** — shut, or fully revealed. The earlier partial/step-reveal control (⌃/⌄ chevrons peeling off 25 lines at a time, `CONTEXT_EXPAND_STEP`) is gone entirely; `contextExpansions` changed from a `Map<gapStart, {before, after}>` to a plain `Set<gapStart>`. Nothing needed it once revealed rows stopped competing for readability with the matches around them.
  2. **Revealed context rows are indented, not dimmed.** `.ctx-context` dropped its `opacity:.5` (and the `:hover`/`.selected` exceptions that undid it) for `padding-left:24px` — full readability, no fight with the `.lvl-error`/`.lvl-warn` row tinting the opacity approach was flattening. Applies whether or not the row also carries a highlight-rule colour, since the "never dim a coloured row" special case is now moot (nothing dims at all).
  3. **An open gap costs no extra row.** Previously every gap — open or shut — occupied a `.ctx-gap` strip row (the open ones as their own "collapse" control). Now only a **closed** gap gets a row (`.ctx-gap-placeholder`, indented like a context row, lightly dimmed to read as a stand-in rather than a real line, ellipsis icon plus "*N* lines", the whole row clickable). An **open** run instead gets two small fold-back icons overlaid inside its own first and last row's indent zone (`.ctx-fold-btn.ctx-fold-top`/`.ctx-fold-bottom`, `stopPropagation`'d so clicking one doesn't also select the row underneath it) — collapsible from wherever in the block you're currently reading. A single-row run shows one icon, not two overlapping ones. `contextStrips` now tracks only closed-gap placeholder positions; a new `contextFoldMarks` (index → `{gap, top, bottom}`) tracks the fold icons, contributing no row height at all — `buildContextRowOffsets` itself needed no change, since its contract was always "however many entries are in `contextStrips`", and there are now fewer of those than before in the common (partly-open) case.
  4. **Nav chip buttons resized and the filter name dropped.** `‹`/`›` (and the expand/collapse-all button) were small custom text buttons (9px font, "‹"/"›"/"⌄"/"⌃") with the active filter's name inline. All three are now `ICON_CARET_LEFT`/`RIGHT`/`UP`/`DOWN` icon buttons styled with the exact same `.panel-toggle-btn` class every other small panel-corner control uses (`#sidebarToggle`, `#detailToggle`) — matching an existing element's size literally, rather than inventing a new one. The filter name is gone from the label (it already sits in the breadcrumb right next to the chip); what's left is a bare "*n* / *m*" position readout. New plain outline icons `ICON_CARET_LEFT/RIGHT/UP/DOWN` and `ICON_ELLIPSIS` — deliberately not the existing `ICON_CHEVRON_LEFT/RIGHT/UP/DOWN`, which carry an extra rail/bar specific to "collapse this panel toward that edge of the screen" and would have been the wrong shape for match-navigation or fold buttons. The chip now also hides entirely on a file node (nothing to navigate or fold there) instead of showing an unrelated "whole file · *N* lines" readout.
  5. **New setting: nav chip corner.** Settings → Behavior → "Match navigation position" (`contextNavCorner`, `philogg-context-nav-corner`, one of `top-right` [default, unchanged from before] / `top-left` / `bottom-right` / `bottom-left`), applied via one of four `.corner-*` CSS modifier classes (`applyContextNavCorner()`).
  6. **Stacked layout always seeds fully expanded.** `buildContextView`'s one-time-per-node seeding step now seeds every gap open whenever `fhLayout === "stacked"`, regardless of `contextInitialExpansion` (which continues to govern the Tabs layout's Context tab only). It is a seed like any other, not a standing override: manually shutting a gap in Stacked survives a flip to Tabs and back. Accepted, documented edge case left alone rather than solved: a node already seeded while in Tabs layout (e.g. collapsed, the default) keeps that seed the first time Stacked is opened on it — switching layout alone never re-seeds, only a genuine node change does.
  `docs/ui-and-views.md`: "Context view" section's gap-model/row-treatment/nav-chip/initial-state paragraphs updated in place for all of the above. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 138** rewritten for the binary model (step-reveal assertions replaced; new coverage for indentation vs. dimming, the placeholder row, top/bottom fold icons including the single-row-run case, the nav chip's icon buttons and file-node hiding, the corner setting, and Stacked's one-time expanded seed surviving a layout flip) — two of its own DOM assertions had to stop assuming every revealed row of a 29-line gap becomes a real DOM node, since `#highlightBody`'s clientHeight is stubbed to a fixed 400px in this suite regardless of model size, same virtualization-window reality every other row-count assertion in this file already accounts for. 2631 passed, 0 failed.

- **The "Full" view becomes the "Context" view: the active filter's own result with the log around it, collapsed into expandable gaps** (this session, 2026-08-30, person-reported). Feedback: *"der Full View ist insofern verwirrend, das Nutzern nicht klar ist, dass sie sich noch immer auf einem Filter Node befinden und eben nicht über Full auf den File Node wechseln."*
  **Diagnosis first.** `renderHighlightView()` set `currentHighlightViewEntries = state.nodes[rootId].entries` — the root file's raw entry array, with no reference to `state.activeId` at all. The view was therefore *pixel-identical* on a filter node and on a file node: there was no wrong signal to correct, there was no signal. Two things compounded it — "Full" and "Filtered" are an opposition, so the label itself implies the filter is off, and switching tabs kept the view's own scroll position, so nothing tied what you saw to what you had been looking at.
  **The rework**, per the agreed framing (the view is *your result, seen in context* — its original purpose was "den Kontext der Filterergebnisse sehen, ohne im Tree umherzuspringen"):
  1. **The view now renders the active node's own result** (`buildContextView`): the matches, with everything the filter rejected collapsed into **expandable gap strips** between them — the GitHub-diff expander people already know (`⌃` reveals 25 rows at the top of the hidden run, the middle button reveals all of it, `⌄` reveals 25 at the bottom; a fully open gap's strip becomes its collapse control). A gap carries `before`/`after` so the still-hidden remainder is always one contiguous run, i.e. one strip per gap however it was opened. `currentHighlightViewEntries` stays a flat array of real entries, so the ~15 places that consume it (minimap, selection, Shift-click ranges, arrow-key nav, `captureViewAnchor`) needed no changes.
  2. **What counts as a match**: `getEntries(state.activeId)`, deliberately not `applyLevelFilter()`'d and not unioned with pinned bookmarks — person-stated rule: *"egal was für eine Art Filter vorausging: der Output sind die Ergebnisse, alles andere ist Kontext"*; the level quick-filter hides result rows from the Filtered view's *display*, they never leave the result set. That keeps the earlier "level filters must not narrow the whole-file view" requirement true rather than contradicting it.
  3. **On a file node nothing is marked and nothing is collapsed** (`contextActive === false`) — the view renders the plain whole file exactly as before, which is what keeps its other, older purpose (tracking several coloured highlight rules across a whole file) available.
  4. **Match rows reuse the `context`/`countContext` filter's existing `.ctx-bracket`** (plus `ctx-start`/`ctx-end` caps) rather than inventing a new marker — people already read that bracket as "the filter produced this". It gets its own gutter lane on `#highlightRows` (`ctx-active`) instead of sharing `.hl-markers`' slot, since here *every* match is bracketed and the Filtered view's rare, documented overlap would become constant. Revealed context rows recede to `opacity:.5` — opacity only, never a colour change, so the `.lvl-error`/`.lvl-warn` tinting survives; hover/selection restores full strength, and a row carrying a highlight-rule colour is never dimmed.
  5. **Renamed to "Context"** (tabs: Context / Filtered / Stacked; internals stay `highlight*`, same compromise the earlier "Full" rename made). "Context" and "Filtered" describe a containment relation instead of two filter strengths, which is what made the old pair misreadable in the first place.
  6. **Nav chip `#contextNav`** replaces the Context panel's Stacked-only `.fh-panel-badge` and is shown in tabs layout too: `‹`/`›` (previous/next match), the active node's name, "*n* / *m* matches", and expand-all/collapse-all. It is the view's textual answer to "am I still on a filter?". **`Ctrl+↓`/`Ctrl+↑`** (`nextMatch`/`prevMatch`, rebindable) do the same from the keyboard, walking real matches and skipping revealed context rows; their check sits ahead of both arrow-key blocks, which match on `ev.key` alone and would otherwise swallow the chord.
  7. **Jumping in is unchanged in shape** — double-click, `Enter`, link-view pair rows all still route through `revealInHighlightView` — but it now opens the gap the target is hidden in first, which is a correctness requirement rather than a preference (a target inside a collapsed gap has no row to scroll to). `moveHighlightSelection` gained the matching courtesy: a selection with no row here continues from its **would-be** position (`contextWouldBeIndex`, binary search over the root order), the same idea the Filtered view's temp anchor applies.
  8. **New setting** — Settings → Behavior → "Context view starts out": `collapsed` (default — the view then opens *as* the filter result, the strongest available "you are still on a filter" signal), `expanded` (the pre-rework whole-file view, still collapsible per gap), `aroundJump` (collapsed, but a jump also opens the gaps on either side of the target).
  9. **Virtualization**: the Context view always uses the offsets path now (`buildContextRowOffsets`), since gap strips make row tops variable regardless of the multiline/notes toggles — each strip's height folded into the row it follows, the leading strip into `offsets[0]`, so `findOffsetIndex`/`topVisibleEntryId`/`captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor` keep working on it unchanged (`minimapRenderedSpan` re-keyed from `needsRowOffsets()` to the array's own presence for the same reason). Collapsed, this is *cheaper* than before: the array is sized by the match count, not the file. Opening/closing a gap goes through `captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor` so the reading position never jumps.
  10. **Text-filter match marking now runs in this view too** (`textMatchHighlightInRows`, setting relabelled "Show matches in log rows"). Not in the original scope, added because Stacked shows the same entry in both panes at once and marking it in only one read as two different rows. *(Superseded on merge into main: the highlight-rule feature's tested design — Group 137 asserts the Full/Context view never marks the active filter path's own matches — takes precedence, so the merged Context view is filter-path-independent and shows only highlight-rule marks. See `docs/filters.md` → "Highlight-rule match text".)*
  11. **`toggleBookmark` rebuilds the Context view too** (found while reviewing the diff, not reported). It deliberately repaints row DOM directly instead of calling `render()` — a bookmark toggle must never yank the reading position — which was fine while this view was the raw file, but it now holds the *active node's* result: toggling a bookmark while standing on the auto "Bookmarks" node changes which rows are matches and where the gaps fall. Rebuilt in place there now, through `captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor` so the reading position still holds. Covered by Group 138(l).
  All new state (`contextGaps`/`contextExpansions`/`contextStrips`/…) is module-level and session-only, deliberately **not** on the node — that keeps it out of `cloneSubtree`/`snapshotSubtree`/`serializeFilterBranch`/`serializeFilterTreeForCache` entirely. Expansions survive re-renders of the same node and are dropped on any active-node change.
  `docs/ui-and-views.md`: the "Highlight view (Filter/Highlight split)" section rewritten in place as "Context view (Context/Filtered split)"; the auto-reveal and level-filter passages updated to the new semantics (one verbatim duplicate "Group 61c" paragraph dropped while editing). `docs/filters.md`/`docs/persistence-and-sync.md`/`docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`/`PROJECT.md`/`README.md`/`homepage/index.html`/`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` (#15, #54): "Full" wording and stale rationale updated; the minimap opt-out's own label/hint now say what it actually does, since the reason it was added ("the Full view always covers the whole log") no longer holds. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 138** (11 areas: the model, gap coverage, rendering, the chip, offsets/spacer agreement, expand/collapse, partial expansion, expand-all/collapse-all, the level-filter rule, jump-into-a-gap, match navigation, expansion reset). Groups 4/61a/61e's "Full stays the whole file" assertions rewritten in place — 61e also had to move onto a filter node to keep saying anything at all, since on the file node it stood on, "the level filter didn't narrow it" is now vacuous; Groups 99/110a picked their non-matching row out of that view by index, which only worked while it listed every row of the file; Group 98's Settings subsection count and Group 114a's shortcut-row count bumped. 2619 passed, 0 failed.

- **Highlight-rule match text: a coloured filter now underlines the substring it matched, in its own colour, in both views and the entry detail** (this session, 2026-08-30, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #20, entry removed). Until now a highlight rule only tinted a row's left-edge gutter — *that* row matched, without saying *where*. The inline match-text marking that already existed for the active filter path (2026-08-23) is now also driven by highlight rules.
  - **Which nodes**: every `filterType: "text"` node carrying a `highlightColor` anywhere under the active root file (`getHighlightMatchNodes`) — `computeHighlightMap`'s own node set narrowed to the one type that has a substring to mark. Deliberately not scoped to the active filter path the way the existing marking is: a highlight rule is a standing, file-wide marker, which is why its gutter markers already showed in both views.
  - **How it paints**: a 2px underline stripe in the rule's own colour, in a single lane whose depth never changes. Where several rules cover the same characters the lane alternates between their colours in 5px slices, so a rule's line runs dead straight through the overlap and the overlap costs no vertical room. Drawn as a `background-image` inside the mark's own box (`left bottom`, `100% 2px`), never as a shadow beneath it. **A stripe rather than a second background colour is what makes a priority rule unnecessary** — one character can carry the filter path's background `<mark>` *and* any number of rule colours at once, so neither has to win. Person-chosen from three offered options for the overlap case, over "highlight wins" and "filter mark wins", and from three again for the overlap *rendering* (below), over parallel per-rule lanes and a vertically split lane. `markCombinedHtml` cuts the text at every boundary either range list contributes and asks both lists per segment; the two lists are never merged (that would destroy the per-rule colour), and with no rule ranges at all it delegates straight to the pre-existing `markRangesHtml`, leaving that hot path byte-for-byte what it was. Rule colours are `escapeHtml`'d into the inline `style` — `node.highlightColor` can carry arbitrary text from a hand-edited or imported filter file.
  - **Where**: Filtered view, **Full view** and entry detail (person-chosen: consistent with the gutter markers already showing in both views). The Full view had no match marking at all before — and it still never marks the *filter path's* matches, only rules: that view is filter-path-independent by design, so the active filter's matches would be marking text in rows it makes no claim about. `buildMatchMarkContext`/`markFieldHtml` are the shared once-per-render bundle and per-field entry point all three surfaces now use.
  - **Control surface**: one view-bar toggle, `#btnHighlightMatchText` (default on, `localStorage["philogg-highlight-match-text"]`), next to `#btnTextMatchHighlight`. Person-chosen over the backlog entry's own two alternatives — a per-node toggle in the colour picker (would have meant a new filter-node field threaded through every persistence carrier, to answer a question already answered by whether the filter carries a colour) and a Settings checkbox. No Settings rows of its own for the same reason. The two buttons are fully independent.
  - **Bug fixed on the way in: `textFilterMatchSpec` ignored `node.isRegex`.** It fell through to the plain-substring path and searched the regex *source* as a literal, which is almost never present in the log — so a regex filter that matched rows perfectly well silently marked nothing inside them, ever since regex filters shipped. It now branches on `isRegex` first, in the same precedence `getEntries`'s own `"text"` branch uses, and `findMatchRanges` gained a regex branch (all occurrences, zero-width matches skipped so a pattern like `a*` can't pepper the field with empty marks). This fixes the pre-existing filter-path marking too, not just the new highlight-rule path.
  - **Two person-reported defects in the first cut of this, fixed the same session before it left the branch.** (a) *The stripes were invisible in both log views.* They were drawn with `padding-bottom` + an inset `box-shadow`, i.e. **below** the text's line box — but `.row-grid` is `align-items:center`, so every log-row cell is exactly as tall as its own line box, and `.col-time`/`.col-msg`/… are `overflow:hidden`: everything under the text was clipped away. Making the rows taller would not have been the fix (inline padding doesn't grow a line box anyway); the lane had to move *inside* the mark's own box, which is why it is a `background-image` now. (b) *An overlapped line stepped down mid-sentence.* Each stripe's depth came from the rule count of the **segment** it sat in, so the same rule was drawn at 2px next to a word and at 4px under it — in the reported screenshot, the red "Target Virtual" underline dropped a step wherever the purple "Target" rule joined it. Depth is now a property of the single shared lane and never of the segment; overlap is encoded horizontally instead. Every mark from the segmenting path also gained `.mark-seg` (no side padding, no rounded corners), since one rule's underline is routinely cut into several marks by another rule's boundary and the pieces have to join seamlessly.
  - **A third person-reported defect, found via a pixel comparison of screenshots with the button on vs. off: toggling it shifted the layout of the unrelated, pre-existing active-filter marking.** `mark.text-match-mark` (the filter-path's own background mark, unchanged since the 2026-08-23 session that introduced it) carried `padding:0 1px` — 2px of layout-affecting side padding on an inline element, unlike the purely-cosmetic background/box-shadow the rest of this marking uses. Whether a filter match rendered through the new `.mark-seg` path (padding zeroed, needed for seamless multi-rule joins) or the old plain `markRangesHtml` fallback depended entirely on whether a coloured highlight rule *also* matched the same field — so the SAME filter match was 2px narrower with the button on than off, reflowing every character after it, nowhere near any actual highlight rule. Fixed at the root rather than patched around: `mark.text-match-mark` now carries `padding:0` unconditionally, on every path — marking a substring never changes its layout width again, regardless of which feature is on.
  Runtime-only, like the marking it extends: `highlightColor` already existed and already travels through every persistence carrier, so no "Known gotchas" carrier needed touching. See `docs/filters.md` ("Highlight-rule match text", and the corrected `textFilterMatchSpec`/`findMatchRanges` bullets) and `docs/ui-and-views.md` ("Highlight view"). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 137**. 2618 passed, 0 failed.

- **Full code-review pass over the self-contained filter architecture: five defects fixed, documentation re-synced to the current state** (this session, 2026-08-30). Requested as a review of the whole baked-condition rework now that it has settled, looking for bugs and performance problems and checking that the docs still describe what is actually implemented. All five defects were found by reading the baked model against its persistence carriers and its hot paths, not from a person report, and each was reproduced in jsdom before being fixed.
  1. **`node.formatId` was dropped by the delete-file undo round trip.** `snapshotSubtree`'s file branch copied `entries`/`cacheKey`/`merged`/`tail` but not `formatId`, and `restoreSubtree` didn't restore it. Removing a file parsed under a custom format and undoing left the node with no `formatId`, which silently reverted it to the builtin default in three places: the level bar's button list (`formatLevels`), tail parsing of newly appended lines (`appendTailText`), and the session cache's reparse on the next reload (`persistFileNode` wrote `formatId: null`). Both carriers now thread `formatId` (and `folderId`, which is unreachable today only because `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo` routes folder-owned files to `closeFolderFile` first).
  2. **`addRowsToSelectionFilter` mutated `node.value` in place.** `cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree` and `captureNodeFields` all copy `value` by reference — harmless for the primitive shapes, aliasing for the array/object ones — so pushing into an `idset` selection filter's array also grew every copy/pasted duplicate of that node, and retroactively rewrote the undo stack's own "before" capture. It now replaces the array wholesale (`node.value = node.value.concat(added)`), the same immutable-value convention `"timerange"` already followed. The convention is now written down as a gotcha in `CLAUDE.md`/`PROJECT.md` rather than left implicit.
  3. **An `and`/`or`/`link` node with no baked sides threw.** A filter file or library preset written before the `bakedA`/`bakedB` model materializes as a combiner with neither side, and `bakeNodeCondition`/`materializeBakedAsNode` did a bare `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(undefined))` on it — so combining such a node threw `"undefined" is not valid JSON`, and Unpack threw on reading `undefined.filterType`. Both now go through `cloneBaked`/`hasBakedSides`; Combine and Unpack refuse the node instead (and the Unpack menu entry is withheld for it), and `importFilterJson`'s validation rejects it up front rather than creating a filter that silently matches nothing.
  4. **AND/OR was offered over a `link` node.** A link's result is synthetic pair entries whose ids never appear in a plain entry pool, so `AND(link, anything)` was always empty and `OR(link, anything)` type-mixed — reachable straight from the bulk-action menu, with no indication anything was wrong. New `canBeCombined` predicate: `describeBulkActions` now offers only "Link…" when either selected node is a link, and `createAndOrNode` refuses such a side. Chaining onto a link is unaffected — that goes through `createLinkNode`, whose evaluator does understand pair entries.
  5. **Performance: `buildOrderIndexMap` rebuilt an O(file size) Map on every render.** `getVisibleEntries()` calls it on every `renderTable()` while "pin bookmarks into the Filtered view" is on, and each link recompute calls it again per nested baked hop — every call allocating a fresh `entry.id -> index` Map over the whole root file. It is now memoized on the file node (`file._orderIndexMap`) with a self-validating key (the entries array's identity and length), so rotation and tail append re-derive it automatically and no invalidation call sites are needed; `mergeFiles`' final in-place sort — the one reorder that key can't see — drops it explicitly via `invalidateOrderIndexMap`.
  **Documentation re-synced to the current state, with the superseded history dropped rather than layered on top** (per the request: describe how things are now, with reasons, not how they were two iterations ago). `PROJECT.md`: Unpack's description still claimed it wires the two materialized siblings into a *fresh* combiner, which the previous session had already removed — corrected, along with the "corrected this session" framing, the stale "3D scatter not built" claim in the docs index, and the line count. `docs/filters.md`: dropped the `linkedId`/`inputA`/`inputB` history from the baked-model section and the Unpack section, and documented what may be a combiner side. `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`, `docs/ui-and-views.md`, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` (#58): remaining `inputA`/`inputB` field references corrected to `bakedA`/`bakedB`. `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`: the export-scope section now states why the question is general instead of narrating the removed and/or/link-specific dialog. `docs/testing-and-limitations.md`: the "Known limitations" list was mostly resolved items carrying full root-cause narratives duplicated from this changelog — rewritten as a current-state list (the 3D scatter plot ships, so its entry is gone; the two new limitations from (3) and (4) are recorded). `CLAUDE.md`: line count and the new `value`-immutability gotcha. Stale `squash`-box and `inputA`/`inputB` wording removed from code comments too.
  `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 136** covering all five fixes, with a positive control on the Unpack menu assertion. Group 130's selection-filter assertions had to stop holding a node object across an undo — `restoreSubtree` rebuilds the node, and those assertions only passed because the `value` aliasing fixed in (2) made the stale reference observe the live node's mutations. 2575 passed, 0 failed.

- **Three person-reported follow-up fixes to the previous session's baked-condition redesign** (this session, 2026-08-29).
  1. **The "Save filter" export-scope prompt (`#exportScopeDialog`) was stuck permanently visible in the main window.** It had no CSS at all — not even a `.hidden{display:none}` rule, let alone the fixed-overlay positioning every other dialog gets — so it rendered as an ordinary inline block wherever it fell in the document, regardless of the `hidden` class being toggled on it. Gave it the same `position:fixed; inset:0; z-index:75` modal-overlay treatment (and matching `.hidden{display:none}`) as `#contextDialog`/`#timeRangeDialog`/etc.
  2. **"Save to library…" silently failed to persist anything.** Direct consequence of (1): `saveFilterToLibrary` awaits `openExportScopePrompt()`'s promise, which only resolves when the person clicks "Just this filter"/"Include ancestor chain" — with the dialog's CSS missing, that prompt was never actually visible/reachable as a modal, so the click never happened and the promise hung forever, `cacheStoreOp("filterLibrary", ...)` never ran, and "Apply from library…" always found the store empty. Fixed by the same CSS fix — no logic change was needed in `saveFilterToLibrary`/`listFilterLibrary`/`applyFilterFromLibrary` themselves.
  3. **Unpack additionally created a copy of itself.** `unpackAndOrLinkNode` materialized `node.bakedA`/`node.bakedB` into two visible sibling filters, then ALSO built a fresh `and`/`or`/`link` combiner wired to those two siblings, next to the original node — leaving the original and the fresh combiner sitting side by side, functionally identical, a duplicate of itself. First fix attempt restored an old `deleteNode(nodeId)` call to delete the original once the fresh combiner replaced it, but that's unnecessary churn: the original node's own `bakedA`/`bakedB` already work fine once its two inputs are visible again, so there's nothing to replace at all. Simplified per person feedback: Unpack now ONLY materializes the two sibling filters next to the original — no fresh combiner is created, the original node (id, `bakedA`/`bakedB`, result) is left completely untouched.
  See `docs/filters.md` ("Self-contained AND/OR/LINK: `bakedA`/`bakedB`, placement, and Unpack", "Export/library: 'just this filter' vs. 'with ancestor chain'") and `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` ("Reusable filter library") updated in place. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 128's Unpack assertions updated for the original node staying untouched with no fresh combiner; Group 135 extended with `getComputedStyle` regression guards for `#exportScopeDialog`'s hidden/shown CSS. 2554 passed, 0 failed.

- **Correction of the previous session's AND/OR/LINK redesign: `bakedA`/`bakedB` baked conditions, replacing the still-live `inputA`/`inputB` lookup** (this session, 2026-08-29). The session below shipped `node.inputA`/`node.inputB` as a fix for the old `linkedId` model — but it was still a LIVE id lookup, just renamed: `getEntries(node.inputA)`/`getEntries(node.inputB)` resolved `state.nodes[...]` on every recompute. Person-reported repro: create filter A ("Some") and filter B ("Entry"), combine with AND, delete filter A — the AND filter's results disappeared along with it. Exactly the bug the whole redesign was supposed to fix, just moved to new field names. Base rule, now explicit: *"Filter kennen keinen Bezug zu anderen Filtern. Sie werden nur durch Verkettung auf den Output von anderen Filtern angewandt und erzeugen daraus eigenen Output."*
  - **Core data model**: `and`/`or`/`link` nodes now carry `node.bakedA`/`node.bakedB` — a flat, self-contained COPY of each side's own single condition (`filterType`, `value`, and whichever of `caseSensitive`/`columns`/`isRegex`/`contextBefore`/`contextAfter`/`countBefore`/`countAfter`/`linkDirection`/`linkN`/`linkOrderEnforced`/`linkExclusive`/`bakedA`/`bakedB` apply — see `bakeNodeCondition`), never a node id, never an ancestor chain. `getEntries()`'s `and`/`or`/`link` branch is no longer special: it reads its own input entries the ORDINARY way (`getEntries(node.parentId)`, exactly like every other filter type), then evaluates `bakedA`/`bakedB` as predicates over that pool via a new standalone evaluator, `getEntriesFromBaked`, which mirrors `getEntriesUncached`'s own per-filterType branches (same `textFilterMatches`/`wildcardMatch`/`compileRegexFilter`/etc.) and recurses into a nested baked `link` condition for multi-hop chains. Deleting, renaming, moving, or replacing the original source node(s) now has **zero effect** — verified with the exact user repro (combine "Some" AND "Entry", delete "Some", verify unchanged, delete "Entry", verify still unchanged) and an ancestor-independence case (a filter A nested under an unrelated ancestor C combines using only A's own condition, never C's restriction).
  - **No cycle guard needed at all any more** (even the "hand-edited file" guard from the previous session is gone) — `bakedA`/`bakedB` hold plain data, not node-id references, so there's nothing left to corrupt into a cycle except normal recursion into a nested baked link, bounded by ordinary data-structure depth.
  - **Unpack is now purely additive**: materializes `node.bakedA`/`node.bakedB` into two new, real, visible sibling filter nodes, wired via a FRESH combiner near the original — the original `and`/`or`/`link` node is left completely untouched (previously it was deleted and replaced).
  - **Export/library generalized to "just this filter" vs. "include ancestor chain", for EVERY filter type** — the previous session's `and`/`or`/`link`-specific "Include source filters used by combined nodes?" dialog is removed entirely (it was also the cause of a reported bug: the dialog could appear at app startup instead of only during an actual save/export action). `serializeFilterBranch(nodeId, includeAncestors)` now offers this choice for any single filter node; the imported branch always attaches under whatever node is currently active/selected at import time, never hardcoded to FILE.
  - See `PROJECT.md` ("Core data model"), `docs/filters.md` ("Self-contained AND/OR/LINK: `bakedA`/`bakedB`, placement, and Unpack", "Export/library: 'just this filter' vs. 'with ancestor chain'" — both rewritten), and `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` ("Filter save / load", "Reusable filter library", updated in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 128 rewritten (baked-condition model, the exact user repro, ancestor-independence, additive Unpack, all four persistence carriers); Group 7/16/20/21/22 updated for `bakedA`/`bakedB`; Group 11 rewritten for the general "just this filter"/"with ancestor chain" scope; new Group 135 (general export-scope option for an ordinary filter type, non-FILE import placement, no-startup-dialog regression guard). 2553 passed, 0 failed.

- **AND/OR/LINK redesign: self-contained `inputA`/`inputB` inputs, replacing the `linkedId`/squash-box model** (this session, 2026-08-29). Supersedes and removes the entire previous, never-merged squash-box session below ("Detach copy", `linkedId` dependency highlighting, cross-file reconstruction on move) — none of that work had reached `main`, so nothing here is a migration; old fields/formats are simply deleted, no backward-compat shims.
  - **Core data model**: `and`/`or`/`link` nodes now carry two fixed, explicit input references, `node.inputA`/`node.inputB` (ids of other filter nodes), read directly by `getEntries()`. `node.parentId` is now purely tree display position for these three types — it has zero bearing on the result. `createAndOrNode`/`createLinkNode` always place the new node as a fresh top-level child of the two inputs' shared root file; from there it moves anywhere like any other node, changing only `parentId`.
  - **No cycle guard needed for ordinary moves any more** — `wouldCreateLinkedCycle`/`subtreeHasLinkedCycle` are gone, since `parentId` no longer interacts with the node's own logic. `getEntries()` instead guards against a genuine (only reachable via a hand-edited file) `inputA`/`inputB` cycle with a "currently resolving" set, degrading to an empty result rather than recursing forever.
  - **Cross-file copy/cut/paste/drag no longer needs special-casing** — `importBranchAt` and the cross-file-detection branch in `pasteClipboard`/`moveFilterNodeWithUndo` are gone; ordinary `cloneSubtree`/`moveNode` already work correctly regardless of file boundaries once inputs are self-contained id references.
  - **New "Unpack" context-menu action** (any `and`/`or`/`link` node): replaces it in place with its two inputs (cloned in as real, visible siblings) plus a fresh combiner explicitly wired to reference them, at the same tree position — a no-op on the filter's own result, meant as a stand-in for inline input-editing (not built this pass — see FEATURE_BACKLOG.md).
  - **New export/library prompt**: saving an `and`/`or`/`link` node (or a subtree containing one) to a `.json` file or the reusable library now asks "Include source filters used by combined nodes?" — off (default) exports only the node's own chain, with unresolved inputs marked `inputAMissing`/`inputBMissing` at export time and `node.inputBroken` at import time (surfaced with a visible highlight, never silently dropped); on additionally pulls in each input's own ancestor chain as extra roots.
  - **Removed entirely** (grew out of the abandoned squash-box design): `detachFilterChain` ("Detach copy"), `node.hiddenInTree`/`node.squashGroup`/`node.squashExpanded` and every function reading/writing them (`renderSquashGroupBox`, `renderNodeOrGroup`, `moveSquashGroupWithUndo`, `toggleSquashGroupExpanded`, `squashGroupTopLevelMembers`, `squashGroupDisplayId`), the `"deleteGroup"`/`"moveGroup"` undo/redo kinds, `computeLinkedDependencyHighlight` and the `.dep-target`/`.dep-user` tree-row classes, and the `.tree-squash-box`/`.tree-squash-divider` CSS.
  - **Kept from the previous (never-merged) session**, since they're independent tree-UI improvements: tree row indentation (`8 + depth*18`), generic collapse/expand for any node with real children, the Settings → Behavior "Tree row type indicator" setting, the row-count percentage bar's removal, extract nodes' highlight-colour swatch, the tree context menu's "Info" submenu, and the `textFilterMatches()` wildcard-as-text bugfix (matches `e.message`, not `e.raw`).
  - See `PROJECT.md` ("Core data model"), `docs/filters.md` ("Self-contained AND/OR/LINK", "Export and library: including source filters" — both new, replacing "Squash / Detach copy", "Cross-file paste/move...", "linkedId dependency highlight"), and `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` ("Filter save / load", "Reusable filter library", both updated in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 128 rewritten (self-contained inputs, placement, Unpack, all four persistence carriers, cycle guard); Group 7 rewritten (no more cycle-guard/cross-file-reconstruction coverage — a plain reparent everywhere now); Group 11 rewritten (export prompt's two paths, `inputBroken` surfaced case); Groups 129-131 removed (squash/detach/linkedId-highlight-only coverage); Group 132 trimmed of squash-box-specific assertions; several other groups (16, 20, 21, 22, 108) updated for the new placement/field names. 2544 passed, 0 failed.

- **Squash box redesign (final iteration, same session, person-refined after trying the header-row version live) + a person-reported Save/Extract matching bugfix.**
  1. **The squash box's header is now the real display row, rendered a second time — not a read-only mirror.** After trying the previous "read-only header row above the box" shape, the person asked for the header to be *identical to* the target row inside, "so both are selected when you pick either." `renderSquashGroupBox` now always renders the display node's own row first (fully interactive, unchanged), and — only once `squashExpanded` — appends a divider plus the private chain, which naturally re-renders that same display row a second time at the bottom of its own chain. Both instances share one node id, so selecting the node marks BOTH rendered rows `.active` together (the intentional "double selection... wie wir das vorher schon mal hatten"). The `.tree-squash-box` background is now **always present, even while collapsed** (previously it only appeared once expanded), so a detached filter visually reads as "logic inside" from the start; expanding just reveals the chain below the header inside the same box. The old `buildSquashGroupHeaderRow`/`.tree-squash-header` (non-selectable mirror row) is gone.
  2. **Bugfix: `filterType: "text"` (Save) with a wildcard/extract-token pattern matched nothing on a link-derived pair entry, while `filterType: "extract"` with the identical pattern worked fine** (person-reported, found while toggling Save↔Extract on a detached extraction+link chain). Root cause: a link pair's `raw` field is a non-contiguous concatenation of both source lines' full raw text (metadata in between), unlike its clean `message` join — `getEntries()`'s `"extract"` branch already matched against `message`, but `textFilterMatches()`'s wildcard-as-text branch defaulted to `raw`. Fixed to match `message` there too.
  See "Tree row layout" in `docs/ui-and-views.md`, "Squash / Detach copy" → "Manual expand/collapse" in `docs/filters.md` (both rewritten), and the new note under "Link filter: nearest-neighbor pairing" in `docs/filters.md` for the bugfix. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 130/132 rewritten for the always-visible box + double-rendered display row, new **Group 134** for the Save/Extract matching fix. 2601 passed, 0 failed.

Hidden behind a new **"Info" submenu trigger** at the top instead — hover or click opens `#treeCtxInfoMenu`, a small floating popup next to the trigger showing the full name, same shape as the log-row context menu's own "Add to selection" submenu (`openTreeCtxInfoMenu`/`closeTreeCtxInfoMenu`).
  2. **Collapsing a detached filter's expanded box was contra-intuitive** — the toggle sat on the display node's own row, now the *deepest* entry inside the box, much further down than where the person expanded from. A **read-only header row** now stays at the group's original position (`renderSquashGroupExpanded`/`buildSquashGroupHeaderRow`), showing the display node's swatch/icon/label/count (swatch still functional — edits the real node's colour) but itself not selectable/draggable/context-menu-able, separated from the box below by a thin divider. The display node's own row, now inside the box, loses its squash-toggle chevron once expanded (reverts to the plain real-children rule) — only the header collapses it back.
