- **Three follow-up fixes to the tree row layout rework, same session, person-reported from a screenshot.**
  1. **"Icon" indicator mode still showed the TXT/LNK/... badge too** (only "Type abbreviation" mode looked right). The separate `.tree-type-tag` badge is gone outright now in both modes — the type is stated exactly once, always at the icon's own position (glyph in "Icon" mode, abbreviation text in "Type abbreviation" mode).
  2. **The row-count percentage bar looked identical on every row** — a filter typically keeps well under 10% of its parent's rows, and a plain linear `fraction*100` width puts every realistic value within a pixel or two of "empty" at this bar's size. `.tree-pct-fill`'s width now uses `Math.sqrt(fraction)*100` with a small minimum for any nonzero fraction, so everyday differences (0.4% vs. 13% vs. 100%) actually read apart at a glance; the tooltip still shows the real percentage.
  3. **Expanding a "Detach copy" node's box left the display row floating on top, showing only its ancestors below** — not what "show the whole state" meant. `renderNodeOrGroup` (new — the one caller-side decision point every "render this child" call site now goes through) detects a `hiddenInTree` child whose group is expanded and replaces its WHOLE slot with the box (built once per group, not once per top-level member) instead of `renderNode` rendering a separate row for it; the display node itself now renders INSIDE the box too (`forceVisible`), as the last real row in its own chain, so the box is the group's one complete, non-duplicated picture. `flattenTreeIds()` mirrors the same box-detection so arrow-key nav still reaches every member. Rows inside the box stay non-draggable, and moving/deleting ANY group member (not just the display) now correctly operates on the whole group rather than risking an in-place reparent of just that one member.
  See "Tree row layout" in `docs/ui-and-views.md` and "Manual expand/collapse" in `docs/filters.md` (both rewritten). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 132 extended (badge absent in both modes, the box containing the display node exactly once, a hidden member's row non-draggable and its move operating on the whole group, the pct-bar's non-linear scale), Group 94's stale `.tree-type-tag` assertion fixed. 2582 passed, 0 failed.

- **Tree row layout rework** (this session, person-reported via screenshot: files indented too far right/wasted left-edge space; a child filter's colour swatch visually sat to the LEFT of its parent file's own icon instead of clearly nested under it; extract nodes had no swatch at all so their indentation was unclear; the squash-group expand chevron shifted the icon and unpacked flat rows instead of something more consistent; the icon and the TXT/LNK/... type badge said the same thing twice).
  - **Indentation**: base/step changed from `12 + depth*16` to `8 + depth*18`, and file rows no longer reserve the swatch's width at all (they never had a colour to show) — a child filter's swatch/chevron/icon now clearly sits to the right of its file's own icon instead of visually overlapping it.
  - **Extract nodes get a highlight-colour swatch too** (still just sets/persists `node.highlightColor` like any other filter — the Highlight view itself still has no dedicated panel for it, this is purely for tree-indentation clarity) — including a detached/squashed extraction, and the colour survives toggling Extract↔Save (it was already preserved structurally; only the swatch UI was missing).
  - **Generic collapse/expand for ANY node with real children**, not just squash groups — a chevron slot (`.tree-chevron-slot`) is now ALWAYS present at a fixed width so the icon never shifts depending on whether a particular row happens to have one. New `node.collapsed` (display-only, not persisted).
  - **A squash group's expand no longer unpacks into flat sibling rows** — it opens a small inline `.tree-squash-box` directly below the display row instead (visually set apart with its own background, same idea as a folder section), containing the private chain as its own nested tree (`renderSquashGroupBox`, `renderNode`'s new `forceVisible` param). `hiddenInTree` nodes are transparent in the MAIN tree unconditionally now (no `squashExpanded` exception) — the box is a separate, self-contained rendering context.
  - **New Settings → Behavior "Tree row type indicator"** (`icon` default / `type`): picks whether the icon slot shows the glyph or the `TXT`/`LNK`/... abbreviation (fixed-width, sized to the longest possible tag) — in `type` mode the separate badge is skipped so the type is stated exactly once.
  - See "Tree row layout" in `docs/ui-and-views.md` and the "Squash / Detach copy" section's "Manual expand/collapse" in `docs/filters.md` (rewritten for the box). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 132**, plus Group 17's extract-swatch assertion flipped to match the new behavior. 2576 passed, 0 failed.

- **Bugfix: a "Detach copy" node could not be dragged/Cut-pasted anywhere at all** (this session, person-reported — *"detached Filter kann ich nicht verschieben"*). The previous session's fix flatly blocked any same-file move of a squash group's visible node, reasoning that reparenting it in place would decouple it from its own private hidden ancestor chain — true, but the fix was wrong: the actual risk is reparenting the *display* node itself, not moving the group at all. `moveFilterNodeWithUndo` now detects this case and calls a new `moveSquashGroupWithUndo`, which moves every **top-level member** of the group (the ones already sitting directly under the file) to the drop target together, as one compound `"moveGroup"` undo/redo action — the display node's own `parentId` never changes, only where the group's top-level roots are filed. Dragging, Cut+Paste, and the context menu's "Cut" (re-enabled — it had been dropped for the same now-obsolete reason) all work on a detached filter exactly like on any other node. See "Squash / Detach copy" in `docs/filters.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 128's own move-related assertion rewritten to verify a real reparent (drag onto a different target moves the group, undo restores it) instead of asserting the old refusal. 2552 passed, 0 failed.

- **Three follow-ups to "Detach copy", this session, person-requested.**
  1. **`linkedId` dependency highlight.** Selecting an `and`/`or`/`link` node now highlights its `linkedId` target elsewhere in the tree (`.dep-target`), and selecting a node that's pointed at BY another node's `linkedId` highlights that other node back (`.dep-user`) — `computeLinkedDependencyHighlight()`, once per `renderTree()`. Neither class ever appears on a squash-group's display node, since it no longer has a live cross-tree dependency once detached.
  2. **Cross-file Copy/Cut/drag now reconstructs instead of leaving `linkedId` pointing into the original file.** `pasteClipboard`/`moveFilterNodeWithUndo` (the shared choke point Cut+Paste and real drag-and-drop both funnel through) compare source/destination root files; same-file behavior is unchanged, but a cross-file target now routes through `importBranchAt` (the Save/Load rebuild step, without the JSON round trip) instead of `cloneSubtree`/a plain reparent. A squash group can now move across files too — cross-file reconstruction never reassigns `parentId` in place, so the same-file "would silently detach the visible node from its hidden chain" risk doesn't apply. `moveFilterNodeWithUndo` now returns the id the node ends up under (still `nodeId` for a same-file move, a fresh id for cross-file) — both call sites updated to use it instead of assuming the original id survived.
  3. **A squashed group now stays collapsed through Save/Load JSON / the reusable library** (previously it silently unpacked back to plain visible nodes on import). `serializeFilterBranch` emits `squashGroupRef` (the ref number of the group's one visible member — `squashGroup` is an opaque tag, not a node, so it can't ride the `linkedRef` scheme directly) alongside `hiddenInTree`/`squashExpanded`; `materializeSerializedRoots` resolves it in a third deferred pass, reusing the freshly materialized display member's own id as the group's new tag. New manual **expand/collapse chevron** (`toggleSquashGroupExpanded`) on every squash-group row lets a person see the hidden chain on demand — flips `squashExpanded` on every member of the group at once, purely a display preference (no undo entry, no cache invalidation).

  See "Squash / Detach copy", "Cross-file paste/move reconstructs...", and "linkedId dependency highlight" in `docs/filters.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 129** (cross-file copy/cut/drag reconstruction, same-file behavior unaffected), **Group 130** (squash stays collapsed through Save/Load, expand/collapse chevron, session-cache round trip), **Group 131** (the dependency highlight both directions); Group 7's cross-file-shaped cycle-guard scenario updated to reflect the new reconstruct-instead-of-block behavior, with a genuinely same-file fixture added alongside it to keep testing the original same-file cycle guard. 2548 passed, 0 failed.

- **Two "Detach copy" bugfixes, this session, person-reported.** (1) The detached node was rendered **twice**, both rows showing `.active` together regardless of which was clicked (functionally the same node, since both DOM rows shared one id): `materializeSerializedRoots` already attaches each top-level root to `file.children` itself (a plain `push`); `detachFilterChain` was ALSO unshifting them onto `file.children` afterward, listing each id twice. Fixed by dropping the redundant unshift — placement now matches Load/library-apply's own (plain append, not unshifted-to-top). (2) The right-click context menu's `.ctx-meta` name header had no `max-width`/wrapping, so a long filter name (a `link`/detach-copy node's name concatenates both its source names) forced `#treeContextMenu` — and the page along with it — far wider than the viewport. `.ctx-meta` now wraps (`white-space:normal; word-break:break-word; overflow-wrap:anywhere`) and `#contextMenu`/`#treeContextMenu`/`#openMenu` gained a `max-width:280px` cap alongside their existing `min-width`. See "Squash / Detach copy" in `docs/filters.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 128 extended — no id appears twice in `file.children` after a detach, and the visible node's row exists exactly once in the DOM. 2507 passed, 0 failed.

- **"Detach copy" — collapses a filter node's whole dependency chain into one self-contained row** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested "squash": *"ich möchte einen Filter... in einen neuen Filter packen, der diese ganze Logik enthält... Hab nur noch einen Eintrag, der mir genau das liefert."*). Grew out of a design discussion about `and`/`or`/`link` nodes' live, ID-based references (`linkedId`) — cloning one via Ctrl+C/Paste (`cloneSubtree`) deliberately keeps `linkedId` pointing at the ORIGINAL target even across files, which is correct for an in-tree copy but surprising when the copy is meant to stand alone. New tree context-menu action, "Detach copy (break references)" (`detachFilterChain`), clones a node's whole *needed* chain — its own ancestors plus any `linkedId` targets' ancestors, the exact same set `serializeFilterBranch` already computes for Save/Load — into a brand-new, self-contained set of nodes, then hides every node but the originally-clicked one (`node.hiddenInTree`) so the whole private chain reads as exactly one tree row; `renderNode`/`flattenTreeIds` treat a `hiddenInTree` node as transparent (no row, but its children still render/navigate at the same depth), and the breadcrumb/filter-popup target chain filter it out too so nothing gives away the hidden structure. Reused `materializeSerializedRoots`, newly factored out of `importFilterJson`, rather than duplicating its rebuild logic — Save/Load, the reusable library, and this same-file action all now share one "rebuild a serialized branch with fresh ids, wire up linkedRef" implementation. All cloned nodes (the visible one included) share one fresh `squashGroup` id: `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo` deletes the whole private chain together when the visible node is deleted (new `"deleteGroup"` undo/redo kind, restoring every member with its original id/flags on undo) instead of leaving hidden orphans behind, and `moveFilterNodeWithUndo` refuses to reparent the visible node (its own `parentId` is required by its hidden ancestor chain — dragging or Cut+Paste would otherwise silently decouple it). `squashGroup`/`hiddenInTree` are threaded through `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` and the session cache (`serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters`, carried verbatim — no remapping needed since a group never spans files); deliberately **not** through `cloneSubtree` (a Copy of the visible row becomes a plain, unflagged node — same "copied away from its real parent" limitation a link-derived extraction already has) nor through `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson` (exporting a squashed node re-expands it to fully visible nodes on import — an accepted simplification, not corruption). See "Squash / Detach copy" in `docs/filters.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 128** — clone independence (fresh ids, `linkedId` repointed at a fresh clone, original chain untouched), `squashGroup`/`hiddenInTree` tagging, single-row tree rendering and tree-nav passthrough, move refusal, group delete + undo restoring every member with its flags, and the session-cache round trip. 2504 passed, 0 failed.

- **"Restore filters" ghost preview gained a Cancel button** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested — *"füge der 'restore Filter' noch ein cancel Button hinzu. damit man das weg bekommt ohne einen neuen Filter anlegen zu müssen"*). Previously the only way to dismiss an unwanted filter-history ghost preview was to build a real filter yourself (`node.children.length === 0 && node.filterHistoryMatch` is the preview's whole visibility condition — see "File filter history" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`). `renderFilterHistoryPreview` now renders both buttons side by side (`.tree-ghost-actions`): **Restore filters** unchanged, and a new **Cancel** that just sets `fileNode.filterHistoryMatch = null` and re-renders — a plain, session-local dismissal (deliberately does NOT set `filterHistoryTouched`, so the underlying stored history record is untouched and the same match can resurface if this content is opened again later). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 30a extended — Cancel removes the ghost preview without creating any filter, and the match can be re-seeded and Restored afterward, proving Cancel didn't disturb the stored record.

- **Session restore now shows every restored file as a grayed placeholder immediately** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested — *"wenn Dateien beim neu laden der Anwendung wiederhergestellt werden, blende sie schon ausgegraut ein. aktuell erscheinen sie erst nachdem die vorherige Datei geladen wurde."*). `restoreSessionFromCache` used to `await addFile(...)` one file at a time, so a session with several large files looked like they trickled in sequentially with no visibility into what was still queued. Now mirrors `loadFileDescriptors`' own multi-file-open shape: a first pass creates a `createQueuedFileNode` placeholder for every restored file, in order, with one `render()`; a second pass activates and parses each one in turn, via a new `existingNode` 5th parameter on `addFile` (mirroring the one `loadOneFileIntoTree` already had). Required a matching fix to `activateQueuedFileNode`, which unconditionally set `state.activeId` — now guarded by `sessionRestoreInProgress`, the same guard `createFileNode` already carries, so routing restore through it doesn't reintroduce the old activeId-thrashing bug through a new call path. See "Session cache" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 127** — two files restored across a simulated reload; polls for both root nodes existing together (well before either could plausibly have finished parsing), confirms the second is still queued/not-yet-loaded while the first is actively parsing, then confirms both finish with correct entry counts.

- **The load-tick real-time throttle (200ms) was removed again — back to updating on every tick** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-reported follow-up to the entry directly below — *"nehme die 200ms wieder raus bzw. nutze was vorher eingestellt war. Das scheint keinen merklichen Unterschied zu machen und sieht so etwas 'stockend' aus. das weglassen der anderen UI Updates hat schon genug gebracht."*). The throttle turned out not to matter — the level bar's live counts and the filter-subtree walk (removed in the entry below) were what actually cost anything — and made the progress bar/counter visibly stutter. `scheduleLoadRender` no longer checks `performance.now()`/a min-interval at all; it does its (already cheap, single-row-only) per-tick work unconditionally again, same as before that throttle existed. See "How it got here" under "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 49/50 both had their `performance.now` mocks removed (no longer needed — the cache invalidation/row update now happens deterministically on every tick again, not racing a throttle window); Group 50's "further tick inside the throttle window doesn't update" assertion was replaced with "a further tick DOES update, matching the data exactly." 2473 passed, 0 failed (full suite, covering all three entries in this session's final round).


- **Load-tick visualization stripped down to just the progress bar/counter, and given a reduced update rate** (this session, 2026-08-28, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below — person-reported: *"Ich sehe noch immer eine deutliche Beschleunigung wenn ich auf eine andere Datei wechsle, als diejenige die gerade geladen wird. Liegt das daran, dass die Zähler für die Level (Warning, Error, etc) noch mitzählen? Nehme (außer dem Ladebalken selbst) (und vielleicht dem Zähler daneben) alle Visualisierungen des Ladevorgangs raus. Und auch Ladebalken etc. darf eine etwas reduzierte Updaterate bekommen."*). Confirmed: the level bar's per-tick live counts (`updateLevelBarCounts`) were exactly the remaining cost — switching to a different, already-loaded file mid-load skipped them, staying on the loading one didn't. `updateLevelBarCounts` had no other caller left and was deleted outright as dead code (not just unhooked from the tick path); the filter-subtree walk the row-update function used to do (giving a filter created mid-load its own live-growing row count) was dropped too — `updateLoadRowLiveData` is renamed `updateLoadRowProgress` and now touches only the loading file's OWN row. `scheduleLoadRender`'s remaining per-tick work (the row update + a cache-invalidation call so a mid-load click doesn't see a stale count) is now also time-throttled to at most once per `LOAD_TICK_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (200ms) of real time, rather than running on literally every chunk/byte tick. Net effect: during a load, only the loading file's own tree row (progress fill + entry count) updates at all, and at a reduced rate — the level bar, any filter created under the loading file, and the Filtered/Full/minimap view all stay frozen at their last real-render value until the load finishes or an unrelated action triggers a real render. See "Current behavior"/"How it got here" under "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` (rewritten in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 50 rewritten with a mocked `performance.now` to deterministically test the throttle (a tick past the window updates the row, a tick well inside it doesn't); Group 52 rewritten to assert the level bar/filter-row counts do NOT auto-update anymore (flipped from the previous "...updates live" assertions) while still proving neither is ever blocked or torn down mid-load; Group 49 gained the same `performance.now` mock so its own explicit-render assertions aren't racing the new throttle. 2461 passed, 0 failed.

- **The live tailing-style view update during a file's own load was removed outright**

- **The live tailing-style view update during a file's own load was removed outright** (this session, 2026-08-28, same-day follow-up to the two entries directly below — *"Baue die Visualisierung einfach aus. Der Ladebalken genügt mir beim Laden."*). After two rounds of performance tuning (throttling, then skipping the view's most expensive per-tick scans) still left a large/fast load measurably slower with the live view on than off, and real decoupling (parsing off the main thread via a Web Worker) turned out not to be realistic for this codebase's single-file/no-build-tooling shape, the person's own preference was simpler than further tuning: drop the live view during loading entirely and keep just the progress bar. `scheduleLoadRender` is now just two direct DOM writes per tick — the loading file's own tree-row progress fill/count (`updateLoadRowLiveData`, including any filter already created under it) and the level bar's counts (`updateLevelBarCounts`) — and never a `render()` of any kind, active view or a file loading in the background alike. The Filtered/Full/minimap view for a loading file only reflects it again once the load's natural completion runs a real, full render (`flushLoadRender`), or an unrelated action (a click, a filter edit, switching away and back) calls `render()` anyway. The throttle (`LOAD_RENDER_MIN_INTERVAL_MS`), the rAF-coalescing, the per-tick main-view render function (`renderLoadTickMainView`), and the flag that made `renderTable()` skip its two expensive scans during a load tick were all removed as dead code rather than left unused. See "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md` (rewritten to lead with current behavior, with the whole same-session arc summarized underneath instead of stacked as further "Fix N" entries). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Groups 50/51 rewritten in place to assert the new invariant (no `render()` at all during any load tick, regardless of active/background); Group 52 unaffected (never depended on the removed live-view path); Groups 127/128 (the throttle and the scan-skipping) deleted outright, since the mechanisms they tested no longer exist. 2459 passed, 0 failed.


- **Follow-up: load ticks now skip the two remaining O(entries-so-far) full-file scans entirely, not just throttled** (this session, 2026-08-28, same-day person-reported follow-up to the entry directly below — *"Das ist noch immer merkbar langsamer. Siehst du noch eine Möglichkeit hier stark zu optimieren? Lässt es sich hier was entkoppeln... Falls das nicht möglich ist würde ich die Visualisierung beim Laden lieber ausschalten."*). The 150ms throttle below caps how OFTEN a load tick's full render runs, but each one still cost the same, since `renderTable()`'s own work (rebuilding the timeline minimap's bucket counts, rescanning every visible entry for the longest message line) is O(entries-so-far) regardless of call frequency. True decoupling (parsing off the main thread via a Web Worker) isn't realistic for this codebase's single-file/no-build-tooling shape, and the person's own stated fallback was to turn the live visualization off outright if that's not possible — instead, `renderMainView`/`renderTable` gained an `isLoadTick` flag (set only from `renderLoadTickMainView`) that skips `renderTimelineMinimap`/`computeMaxMessageWidth` entirely during a load tick; both catch up to the true state at the load's natural completion. The tree row's own live count/progress fill and the (already virtualized, cheap) visible table rows are unaffected — only the minimap and the message column's width stop updating live during the load itself, as the accepted tradeoff for the load no longer paying an O(n²) cost. See "Fix 5" under "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 128** — a direct `isLoadTick=true` call skips both functions, a normal render still runs both, and an end-to-end mid-parse load tick (real `scheduleLoadRender`/rAF path) skips both while the load's completion runs them again. 2475 passed, 0 failed.

- **Bugfix: the load-tick tailing visualization was measurably slowing down loading itself** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-reported — *"Die Visualisierung des Ladevorgangs... scheint das Laden deutlich zu verlangsamen. Wenn ich eine andere als die gerade ladende Datei auswähle, läuft der Ladevorgang viel schneller."*). Root cause: `scheduleLoadRender`'s existing rAF-coalescing (at most one `renderLoadTickMainView()` per animation frame while the loading file is the active view — see "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`) wasn't enough, because `renderTable()`'s own per-frame work is itself O(entries-so-far) — it rebuilds the timeline minimap's bucket counts and rescans every entry for the widest message line on every call — and entries-so-far grows on every tick, so running that full pass every ~16ms turned an otherwise-fast load into O(n²) total work. This is exactly why switching to a different, already-loaded file mid-load felt faster: that path (`loadRenderRootIsActive`) skips the expensive full render entirely. Fixed with a real-time minimum gap between actual full renders, `LOAD_RENDER_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (150ms), checked inside the rAF callback itself — a tick landing before the gap has elapsed skips the full render for that frame (the tree row's own live progress/count still updates every tick, unthrottled) and the next tick tries again; the true start/end of a load (`flushLoadRender`) is untouched and always renders immediately. See "Fix 4" under "File loading" in `docs/persistence-and-sync.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 127** — `performance.now` and `requestAnimationFrame` both mocked to prove ticks landing inside the throttle window are skipped, a tick past the window runs a full render again, and the file still finishes loading correctly despite the throttle. 2467 passed, 0 failed.

- **Plot config now persists per extraction node** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested — *"Die Plots sollen ihren Zustand behalten... Auch wenn ich einen Extraction Knoten kopiere, möchte ich das er den gleichen Plot erzeugt."*). Previously the whole plot definition (chart type, axes, color-by, axis-equal, ranges) lived in one shared global reset to defaults on every node switch — including a switch back to the SAME node, so leaving the Plot tab and returning showed a blank plot again, and copying an extraction node never carried its plot along. `plotConfig` now lives on the node itself (`node.plotConfig`, extract-only, created lazily), swapped in by the renamed `loadPlotConfigForNode()` on every genuine node switch — the zoom/pan/rotation viewport still resets (that's "what you're looking at right now," not the plot's definition), but the actual plot setup doesn't. Threaded through all four persistence carriers per CLAUDE.md's gotcha: `cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`, `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters` — the two import paths run incoming values through a new `sanitizePlotConfig()` rather than trusting them directly, since a filter file or cache entry could be hand-edited or foreign. See "Plot config persists per extraction node" in `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 126** — leave/return reproduces the same plot, copy reproduces the same plot as an independent deep copy, undo restores it, save/load and session-cache round trips carry it, and `sanitizePlotConfig` rejects a corrupt/foreign value.

- **3D scatter plot: manual per-axis ranges** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested same-day follow-up to the 3D scatter plot below). X/Y/Z each get an "auto unless overridden" number-input pair (`#plotXMin`/`#plotXMax`/`#plotYMin`/`#plotYMax`/`#plotZMin`/`#plotZMax`), analogous to 2D's own X/Y range inputs — in fact reusing the same `plotConfig.xMin`/`xMax`/`yMin`/`yMax` fields (plus new `zMin`/`zMax`), since 2D and 3D never show their controls at the same time. `renderPlot3D()` uses the override (when set) as the extent an axis is centered/scaled from, same shape as 2D's `xRawLo`/`xRawHi`; a manual range isn't a point filter (3D has no clip-path), just changes that axis's world-scale, which also feeds into `axisEqual3d`'s "tightest scale wins" comparison. See "3D scatter" in `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 125** extended — range controls present, the scale math for a manual X/Z override against the existing asymmetric-range dataset, and clearing an override back to auto.

- **3D scatter plot** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested — previously explicitly deferred, see `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`'s old "lower priority" note). A new "3D" chart type in the Plot tab: X/Y/Z axis column selects plus an optional color-by column (blue→yellow→red gradient, same `plotColorScale` as 2D scatter). Hand-rolled — no 3D library in this codebase — a simple orthographic projection (`renderPlot3D()`/`project3dPoint()` in `philogg.html`) drawn on a new `<canvas id="plot3dCanvas">` sibling of `#plotSvg` (shown/hidden together depending on chart type). **Equal axis scale** is a select rather than a single checkbox — Off (each axis auto-fit), All equal (X=Y=Z), or any one pair equal with the third auto — since a 3D plot has three axes rather than two, unlike 2D's binary axis-equal toggle. **Interaction**: left-drag rotates (yaw/pitch), middle-click-drag or shift-drag pans, mouse wheel zooms, and clicking a point jumps to its log entry — same `jumpToFullLog` the 2D scatter's marks already use, plus a "Reset view" button. The 2D `#plotZoomBar` (zoom %, "Filter from plot view") is hidden for this chart type, since rotate/zoom/pan happen directly on the canvas instead. See "3D scatter" in `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 125** — axis/color-by/axis-equal-mode controls, the per-axis scale math for all four axis-equal modes, rotate/zoom/pan mouse interaction, point-click-to-log-entry (and its empty-space/drag-suppression no-ops), and Reset view.

- **Plot view: zoom/filter buttons moved from an overlay on the chart into the toolbar above it** (this session, 2026-08-28, person-requested). `#plotZoomBar` (zoom −/level/+/Reset, then "Filter: time range"/"Filter: these entries") was previously an absolutely-positioned overlay in the top-right corner of `#plotChartArea`, covering chart content. It now lives in the extraction toolbar's second row (`.extract-toolbar-row2`), to the left of the Table/Plot view tabs, laid out as two clearly separated `.plot-zoom-bar-group`s (zoom controls | filter buttons) plus a further separator before the view tabs. No functional/behavioral change — same elements, same listeners, same show/hide logic, just relocated in the DOM and restyled from `position:absolute` to a normal flex flow. See "Plot zoom / pan / hover tooltip" in `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`; no regression-suite change needed (layout-only, no `getElementById`/behavior touched).

- **Three person-reported bugfixes** (this session, 2026-08-28).
  1. **Plot "Filter: time range"/"Filter: these entries" used only the X viewport, ignoring Y.** `getPlotViewportEntries()` (`philogg.html`) destructured only `xDomainMin`/`xDomainMax` off `plotLastRender`, so a row panned/zoomed OUT of view on the Y axis alone — in range on X, clipped away on Y — was still counted as "currently plotted" and included in the created filter, along with rows whose Y value for the plotted column(s) wasn't even finite (the NaN-drop `renderPlotChart`'s own `series` construction applies, which `getPlotViewportEntries` didn't reuse). Now also checks `yDomainMin`/`yDomainMax` against every selected Y column, keeping a row only if at least one plotted Y value is finite and in range. See "Plot tab" in `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md`.
  2. **Minimap drag-to-select silently stopped working.** The drag's start position was anchored as a raw `minimapWidth`-unit x value at mousedown (`minimapDragStartX`). A re-render mid-gesture — a tail tick, a level-bar toggle, a window resize, anything that calls `renderTimelineMinimap()` — can change `minimapTMin`/`minimapTMax` (and, less often, `minimapWidth`) out from under an in-progress drag; re-evaluating that same stored x against the new time range at mouseup no longer pointed at the entry actually dragged from, in the worst case flipping the from/to order and silently failing the `toTs > fromTs` guard so no filter was created at all — same class of bug CLAUDE.md's own "hot loop rebuild" gotcha already documents for the tree/rows. The anchor is now captured as a real timestamp (`minimapDragStartTs`) at mousedown instead; every pixel position the gesture needs (overlay, drag threshold, final range) is re-derived from it via `minimapTsToX`/`minimapXToTs` against whatever is currently live, so a mid-drag re-render can no longer desync it. See Group 31 in the regression suite (extended with a tail-tick-mid-drag case).
  3. **A new "Add to selection" filter was named by its initial entry count, which went stale.** `createSelectionFilterNode` named a new selection via `idSetFilterName(ids, "selection")` (e.g. `"3 entries (selection)"`) — a one-time snapshot of `ids.length` at creation time; `addRowsToSelectionFilter` deliberately never rewrites `node.name` afterward (a person may have renamed it), so growing a selection from 3 to 30 entries kept the stale "3 entries" label forever. New selections are now named as a plain ordinal via `nextSelectionFilterName()` — "Selection 1", "Selection 2", ... (counted across every `selectionFilter` node in the tree) — immune to how many rows get added later. Plot-view-created `idset` filters (no `selectionFilter` flag) are unaffected and keep `idSetFilterName`'s count-based name. See Group 124 in the regression suite.

- **"Add to selection" — redesign of "Filter from selection"'s placement/UI** (this session, 2026-08-28). Same-day follow-up superseding the entry directly below: the person reviewed the initial "Filter from selection" and asked for a redesign, on the reasoning that a selection is independent of whatever filters sit above it in the tree — so it should never nest under the currently active filter node. Selection filters (still `"idset"` nodes, now marked `node.selectionFilter === true`) are now always created TOP-LEVEL directly under the file, alongside the auto-managed "Bookmarks" node (`syncBookmarksFilterNode`'s own `root.children.unshift` placement, reused). The single "Filter from selection (N rows)" menu item is replaced by an **"Add to selection"** submenu (`#ctxAddToSelection` / `#addToSelectionMenu`) listing every existing top-level selection filter by name — clicking one ADDS the current row(s) to its `value` array, deduped — plus, below a separator, "Create new selection filter". Also now works from a SINGLE selected/right-clicked row, not just a 2+ row multi-selection. Kept deliberately different from Bookmarks in three ways: deletable (not `locked`), no "always show"/pin toggle (that control, `#btnPinBookmarks`, stays hard-wired to `state.bookmarks` specifically), and membership never touches `state.bookmarks` at all. `selectionFilter`, being a genuinely new field, is threaded through and verified explicitly across all four persistence carriers per `CLAUDE.md`'s gotcha (`cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`, `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters`). See "Add to selection" in `docs/filters.md` (rewritten in place — the old "Filter from selection" description is gone). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: **Group 124** rewritten in place (single-row add, top-level placement regardless of active node, deletability, no bookmark side-effect, the submenu's existing-selection list + add-to-existing + dedup, plot-view `idset` nodes staying excluded from the submenu, and the `selectionFilter` field's carrier round trips). 2399 passed, 0 failed.

- **"Filter from selection"** (this session, 2026-08-27; superseded the very next day by "Add to selection" above — kept here as the accurate historical record of what actually shipped that day). Turns the log view's existing Ctrl/Shift-click multi-selection (`state.logMultiSelect`, the same set `copyLogSelectionToClipboard`/Ctrl+C already reads) into a filter matching EXACTLY those entries, via a new "Filter from selection (N rows)" item in the log row right-click menu (`#ctxFilterFromSelection`), shown only when 2+ rows are multi-selected. Reuses the `"idset"` `filterType` (an explicit array of entry ids) introduced alongside "Create a filter from the plot view" below, rather than a second, parallel entry-set filter type — the two producers just differ in how they name the ids handed to `createFilterNode(parentId, "idset", ids)`. Creates the node as a new child of the currently active filter node and reveals it. No new persistence-carrier code needed (rides the existing generic `node.value` field, same as `idset`'s other producer). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: original **Group 124** (menu-item visibility gated on selection size, the created node's type/value/`getEntries` result, tree placement + active-node reveal, and a copy/paste round trip) — since replaced, see above. 2382 passed, 0 failed.

- **Regex filter type, and "Create a filter from the plot view"** (this session, 2026-08-27, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #16 and #11, both retired). Two independent Road-to-1.0 items.
  1. **Regex filter type** (#16, per the person's stated refinement — a toggle at the top of the Filter Dialog, not a separate filter type): `#filterRegexCheckbox` ("Interpret input as regex"), first row of `#filterPopup`. When checked, the input is compiled as a real `RegExp` (`compileRegexFilter`, new) instead of the wildcard-token parser — a new `node.isRegex` boolean flag on the SAME `"text"` `filterType` (no new filter type; `getEntries`'s `"text"` branch, `textFilterMatches`, and `evaluateLiveMatch` all check it first, before the existing wildcard/plain-substring paths). An invalid regex degrades gracefully everywhere: `getEntries` returns an empty result, the popup shows an inline `--level-error` "Invalid regex" state (mirroring the existing "Invalid pattern" state) and refuses to submit — never a thrown exception. Case-sensitivity and column-restriction stay fully available and visible in regex mode (they're generic "text"-filter options, orthogonal to how the pattern is compiled); only the wildcard-token-specific UI — the `[float]`/`[int]`/… insert chips (`#filterTokenChips`) and the live pattern preview (a wildcard-only concept, capture-group highlighting) — hides while regex mode is on, and the Extract button stays disabled (a raw regex has no `[value:...]` placeholder vocabulary to build columns from). Threaded through every persistence carrier per CLAUDE.md's "Known gotchas": `cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`, the field-edit-undo `captureNodeFields`/`applyNodeFields` carrier (a 4th carrier alongside the three CLAUDE.md names explicitly, since in-place filter edits use it too), `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters` — same 6-touch-point pattern `caseSensitive`/`columns` already established, `isRegex` just rides alongside each one.
  2. **"Create a filter from the plot view"** (#11, resolving its own stated open question by implementing BOTH options rather than picking one): two new toolbar buttons in the Plot tab's `#plotZoomBar` (next to the existing zoom controls) — **"Filter: time range"** (`#plotFilterTimeRangeBtn`) creates a `"timerange"` filter (the existing, generic time-filter node type — see "Time filters" in `docs/filters.md`) spanning the min/max timestamp of whatever's currently visible in the plot; **"Filter: these entries"** (`#plotFilterEntriesBtn`) creates a new `"idset"` filter matching EXACTLY those entries. "Currently visible" means the Plot tab's own zoomed/panned viewport (`plotLastRender`'s `xDomainMin`/`xDomainMax` — the home/un-zoomed view when no zoom is active, not always "the whole extraction"), read via a new `getPlotViewportEntries()` that derives each row's on-screen X position the exact same way `renderPlotChart` itself does (bar: row-index slot center; line/scatter: the X column's own parsed value) so it can never disagree with what's actually drawn. **`idset`** (new `filterType`, added to `FILTER_TYPES`): `node.value` is a plain array of entry ids, matched via a `Set` in a new `getEntries` branch — the same "second, pre-computed, non-parent-derived source of truth" shape `"timerange"`/`"bookmarks"` already use, chosen (over, say, reusing `"bookmarks"` or inventing an ad-hoc selection mechanism) because it's generic enough for the exact-entry-set case explicitly asked for in the backlog item's part (b), without dragging in `state.bookmarks`' own global, singleton semantics. Needed **no new persistence-carrier code**: like `"timerange"`, its value rides the existing generic `value` field every carrier already copies/serializes for every filter type — verified directly (not just assumed) via a real `cloneSubtree` copy/paste round trip in the new test group. An empty viewport (nothing currently visible — a zoomed-out-of-data-range or gap case) shows a toast and creates nothing, rather than a broken empty filter. See "Filter popup" in `docs/filters.md` and "Plot zoom/pan" in `docs/extraction-and-plotting.md` (both updated in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new **Group 122** (regex — `getEntries` semantics including case-sensitivity/column-restriction/invalid-pattern, the popup's UI toggle hiding wildcard-only elements while keeping the rest, live-match/inline-error states, submit-blocks-on-invalid, and all persistence carriers) and **Group 123** (`idset`'s `getEntries` branch + copy/paste round trip, both new plot-toolbar buttons under a home view and a real zoom, and the empty-viewport no-op case). 2373 passed, 0 failed.

- **Bugfix: the minimap's selection pin now hides once nothing on screen is actually marked** (this session, 2026-08-27, person-reported, follow-up to the range-box fixes below). `minimapMarkedEntries` used to resolve the selection pin via a plain `entryIndex` lookup, which finds ANY real entry ever selected regardless of whether it's a member of what's currently on screen — so the pin kept pointing at a stale position after e.g. a faded temp anchor's row was removed from the Filtered table, or after switching to a pane/root file that no longer contains the selected entry (even though `state.selectedId`/`state.tempAnchor` still reference it internally for bookkeeping). It now resolves against `currentHighlightViewEntries` while the Full tab is on screen and `currentViewEntries` while the Filtered tab is (both, in Stacked layout), with no `entryIndex` fallback. `scheduleTempAnchorFade` now calls `updateMinimapSelectionMarkers()` itself the instant a faded anchor's row is actually removed (both the animated-collapse branch and the "scrolled out of the rendered window" early return), and `showFhTab`/`applyFhView`'s Stacked branch call it on every tab switch too. See `docs/ui-and-views.md` and Group 121 in the regression suite.

- **Bugfix: the temporary anchor no longer skews EITHER of the timeline minimap's range boxes** (this session, 2026-08-27, person-reported, two rounds). The temp anchor (a foreign entry stitched into the Filtered table for context by `spliceTempAnchor`) was never a real member of the active filter's own result, but both of the minimap's boxes were letting it in anyway. (a) `renderMainView()` fed `renderTimelineMinimap` the POST-splice `currentViewEntries` list, so an anchor sitting far outside the filter's real time span made `updateMinimapFullRange`'s box (`#minimapFullRangeRect`) balloon out to cover it — fixed by capturing `minimapViewEntries` from `getVisibleEntries()` **before** `spliceTempAnchor` runs. (b) That fix alone left the *second* box, `#minimapRenderedRangeRect` (`updateMinimapRenderedRange`/`minimapRenderedSpan`, "what's actually rendered right now"), still stretching to the anchor — reported again by the same person, since that box legitimately sources from `currentViewEntries` (the anchor row really is a rendered DOM row, unlike (a)'s pure bucketing pass). Fixed by having `minimapRenderedSpan` walk its computed start/end index inward past any `_tempAnchor`-flagged row to the nearest real one before converting to a pixel span (returning `null`/hidden in the edge case where only the anchor row is actually in view). See `docs/ui-and-views.md`'s "temporary anchor" section and Group 120 in the regression suite.

- **Electron: F11 and the native window-control fullscreen button are now fully equivalent** (this session, 2026-08-27, person-reported). `F11` previously toggled a custom bounds-resize hack on Windows/Linux (`toggleWindowedFullscreen`, kept separate from real `setFullScreen()` specifically to keep the Window Controls Overlay's minimize/maximize/close buttons visible — real OS fullscreen has no "titlebar area" for Chromium to paint them into), while the native window controls' own maximize/restore button always used real `win.setFullScreen()`. Two different, non-interoperable toggles meant F11 couldn't exit fullscreen entered via the window control and vice versa. `main.js`'s `watchFullscreenToggle` now just calls `win.setFullScreen(!win.isFullScreen())` on F11 on every platform (macOS already did this) — same code path the native control drives, so either trigger can enter or exit what the other started. Trade-off accepted deliberately: the Window Controls Overlay buttons now disappear in fullscreen on Windows/Linux (matching how real OS fullscreen always looks), same as they already do when triggered via the window control. `windowedFullscreenBounds`/`isWindowedFullscreen`/`toggleWindowedFullscreen` and the now-unused `screen` import removed. See `desktop/README.md`'s "Frameless window" section. No regression-suite coverage — `desktop/` has no test harness (jsdom can't drive a real `BrowserWindow`).

- **Timeline minimap: the two range-indicator boxes and click-to-jump now all know which fh pane (Filtered/Full) is actually on screen** (this session, 2026-08-27, FEATURE_BACKLOG.md #8 + two same-day person-feedback follow-ups). Previously all three mechanisms — the "covered timespan" box, the "currently rendered" box, and click-to-jump — always resolved against the Filtered view (`currentViewEntries`/`tableBody`), even while the Full tab was the one actually visible, so interacting with the minimap silently acted on an invisible pane.
  1. **Settings → Behavior → "Hide minimap's covered-timespan box in Full view"** (new toggle, default **off** — an opt-out of existing behavior, not a bugfix): when on, `#minimapFullRangeRect` (`updateMinimapFullRange`) hides itself whenever the Full tab is the one showing (`fhLayout === "tabs" && fhActiveTab === "highlight"`); left alone in Stacked layout, where the Filtered pane sits alongside it and the box stays meaningful. Persisted via `hideMinimapFullRangeInFullView`/`philogg-hide-minimap-full-range-in-full-view` in `localStorage`, same plain-flag mechanism as the existing hover-expand settings. `showFhTab`/`applyFhView` now call `updateMinimapFullRange()` on every tab/layout switch so the effect is immediate, without waiting on an unrelated scroll or render.
  2. **Same-day clarification (person feedback): the OTHER box, `#minimapRenderedRangeRect` (`updateMinimapRenderedRange`), must NOT hide in the Full view — it must instead reflect the Full view's OWN scroll position**, not the (then-invisible) Filtered view's. The function now branches on the same `fhLayout`/`fhActiveTab` check: Full tab active → `highlightBody.scrollTop`/`clientHeight` over `currentHighlightViewEntries`, not gated by `minimapInteractive` (a Filtered-view-only concept — no column sort exists in the Full view); otherwise → the existing `tableBody`/`currentViewEntries` path, unchanged. Both branches share a new `minimapRenderedSpan(entries, scrollEl, rowOffsets)` helper for the index/pixel math. `renderHighlightView()` now calls `updateMinimapRenderedRange()` on every one of its return paths, `#highlightBody`'s scroll listener calls it rAF-batched (twin of `#tableBody`'s own listener), and `showFhTab`/`applyFhView` call it alongside Box #1 on every tab switch. **Edge case**: `renderHighlightView()` runs even while the Link view is on screen (to keep the Full view's data current in the background — see `renderMainView`), which used to be exactly what kept this box hidden there by simply never being called; now that it *is* called, an explicit guard was added at the very top of `updateMinimapRenderedRange()` (`if (linkWrap.style.display !== "none") { ...hidden... }`) so the Link view — which has no row virtualization at all — still never shows a "rendered subset" box, regardless of which fh tab was last active. The window-resize and sidebar-resize drag handlers' own `updateMinimapRenderedRange()` calls lost their old `tableWrap.style.display !== "none"` gate for the same reason — that gate would have wrongly skipped Box #2 whenever the Full tab was on screen.
  3. **Second same-day clarification (further person feedback): the minimap's click-to-jump must jump the pane that's actually visible, not always the Filtered view.** `#timelineMinimapSvg`'s `click` listener now uses the identical `fhLayout`/`fhActiveTab` branch: Full tab active → resolves the nearest entry in `currentHighlightViewEntries` and calls `selectHighlightEntry(id, { scroll: true, center: true, index })` (not gated on `minimapInteractive`, same reasoning as (2)); otherwise → the existing `currentViewEntries`/`selectEntry` path, still gated on `minimapInteractive`. Drag-select (creates a `"timerange"` filter) and the right-click time-range dialog are unaffected — both always operate on the Filtered view/tree regardless of which tab is showing, since neither has a Full-view equivalent.

  See "Timeline minimap" in `docs/filters.md` for the full mechanism. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new Group 119 (a: the Settings toggle hides/shows Box #1 correctly across Filtered/Full/Stacked, persists, and re-evaluates immediately on tab switch; b: Box #2 tracks the Full view's own scroll, ignores the Filtered view's, isn't blocked by an active column sort, and stays hidden for the Link view regardless of last active tab; c: click-to-jump resolves against the Filtered view, the Full view, and Stacked, selecting via the right function in each case). Group 98's Settings-Behavior subsection-count/order assertions updated for the new "Timeline minimap" subsection. 2323 passed, 0 failed.

- **Cross-view jumps (double-click, extraction-table jump) preserve the selected entry's on-screen position instead of centering it** (this session, 2026-08-27, person-requested). The tree-click/Alt+Arrow path already kept the selected row at the same screen pixel offset across a view change (`captureViewAnchor`/`restoreViewAnchor`, see below), clamping to the top when there aren't enough rows above to keep the exact offset. Double-click (`revealInHighlightView`) and the extraction table/plot-mark double-click (`jumpToFullLog`) bypassed that mechanism entirely and always hard-centered the target row — `scrollToIndex`/`scrollToHighlightIndex` gained a new `opts.offset` mode (lands the row at a given pixel distance from the viewport top, clamped to 0 the same way `restoreViewAnchor` already relies on) and both jump functions now capture the source row's actual on-screen offset (`captureRowScreenOffset`, via `getBoundingClientRect`) and carry it through `state.scrollTargetOffset`/`state.highlightScrollTargetOffset` to the render that consumes it, falling back to centering only when the source row wasn't on screen to begin with (nothing to preserve). Bookmark-panel jumps (`jumpToEntry`) and the timeline minimap/plot-mark clicks still center — none of them jump from a row with a meaningful "current screen position". See "Cross-view jumps preserve screen position" in `docs/ui-and-views.md`. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: new Group 118 (a: double-click preserves a known on-screen offset; b: not enough rows above clamps to the top instead of centering; c: same for `jumpToFullLog` from the Extraction table). 2291 passed, 0 failed.

- **Custom log level names per format** (this session, 2026-08-26, follow-up to #47 after the person tried the fixed-five version and wanted their own vocabulary). A `LogFormat.levels` list is no longer restricted to `ALL_LEVELS`: it may contain arbitrary names (`NOTICE`, `CRITICAL`, `FATAL`, `VERBOSE`, …) alongside/instead of the five that own a theme color. `levelBucket(level, formatId)` became **format-aware** — it first tries an exact, case-insensitive match against the owning format's own list (that's what makes a custom name its own named bucket), then falls through the unchanged prefix cascade for the five known buckets, then `OTHER`; unrecognized free text deliberately still collapses into `OTHER` rather than inventing dynamic buckets, so counts and the level bar stay bounded by configuration. The `formatId` comes off the entry, already stamped at both parse sites by the earlier entry below. Every call site was threaded through (`entryComparator`, `getEntries`'s `"level"` branch, `getLevelCounts`, `applyLevelFilter`, both log-row renderers, the detail panel, the pair rows, the minimap), and `getLevelCounts()` keys off each entry's computed bucket rather than any fixed name list, so custom buckets are counted for free. Colors: a rotating categorical palette `--level-custom-1` … `--level-custom-6` per theme block (six distinct hues each, in the spirit of `COLUMN_PALETTE`, plus one `--level-custom-on`; `-soft` shades derived with `color-mix`), deliberately **not** added to `THEME_COLOR_KEYS` so previously-exported custom themes stay importable and simply inherit them. `customLevelSlot()` assigns a slot by the name's position among that format's *custom* names (wrapping after six), so a format always paints the same level the same color; `levelClass(level, formatId)` returns `lvl-custom-N`, with matching CSS alongside every existing `lvl-*` consumer (`.level-btn`/`.active`, `.col-bar`, `.level-badge`, `.log-row`/`:hover`, `.pair-row-bar`, `.minimap-lvl-*`). `levelSortRank()` places custom buckets between `TRACE` and `OTHER` by slot (`OTHER`'s rank moved to `5 + CUSTOM_LEVEL_SLOTS`), and the minimap now keeps the bucket *name* per column alongside the rank, since a rank no longer identifies a bucket by itself. Editor: the "Log levels" list keeps the five fixed checkbox rows and gains a name field + **Add** button (Enter works) below it; a custom row is always checked, reorders with the same ▲/▼, previews its palette color live, and carries a ✕ delete control instead of a checkbox-off (it isn't part of a fixed set to toggle back on). Rejected with an inline error: blank/whitespace-only input, the reserved `OTHER`, and case-insensitive duplicates of any row already listed. The "at least one level" save guard is unchanged, and existing formats (builtin default included) render and behave exactly as before. Tests: new **Group 117**, Group 116 untouched (2225 passed, 0 failed). See `docs/ui-and-views.md` → "Level bar" → "Per-format log levels".

A `LogFormat` now carries its own ordered `levels` array — any subset of the fixed colored bucket set `ALL_LEVELS` (`ERROR/WARN/INFO/DEBUG/TRACE`), in any order — edited in the Format Manager's format editor as a checkbox + ▲/▼ list (same idiom as the filename-rule list). `OTHER` is never listed: it stays the implicit catch-all, and `levelBucket()`'s prefix cascade is untouched, so what a raw level string buckets into is unchanged; only which buckets get a quick-filter button, and in which order, is now format-driven. A format without the field (every builtin, everything created before this) falls back to the old fixed `ERROR, WARN, INFO, DEBUG` — no visible change for anyone who doesn't touch the setting. With several files open under different formats, `activeLevelOrder()` renders the union of their lists, concatenated in tree order and de-duplicated, and `canonicalLevelOrder()` re-sorts a level filter node's `value` by that same order (names outside it, e.g. `OTHER` from an imported filter, are kept and appended, not dropped). Saving a format with no level checked is refused; Reset on the builtin default restores its original four. - **Desktop-only extension of the UI font picker: every OS-installed font, via `font-list`** (this session, 2026-08-26, person-requested follow-up to the UI font family setting below — *"is it possible to add a broader selection based on what the system is capable of, only when the Electron Wrapper is used"*, citing Fira Code/Iosevka). The plain `philogg.html` build still can't enumerate installed fonts (no permission-prompt UI to gate the browser's Local Font Access API, and adding one would break the "no dependencies" static-file rule) — but the desktop wrapper is a Node process with no such restriction, so it can shell out to the OS directly. Added a new `window.philogg.listSystemFonts()` bridge (`desktop/preload.js`) backed by a `philogg:list-system-fonts` IPC handler in `desktop/main.js` that calls the `font-list` npm package (`getFonts()`, wraps `fc-list`/PowerShell/`system_profiler` per platform, no permission dialog needed since it's local Node, not the browser) and caches the result for the process's lifetime; any failure (headless CI, missing binary) just resolves to `[]`. `philogg.html`'s `initUiFont()` now calls this (when present) after applying the curated default, appending every returned name as an `<optgroup>` under `#settingsUiFontSelect` — deduped against the curated stacks' own leading font name (so e.g. "Arial" isn't offered twice) — with each option's value prefixed `sys:` and its stack computed on the fly as `"<name>",<default fallback stack>` (`fontStackForId()`), rather than requiring every system font to be a hardcoded `UI_FONT_OPTIONS` entry. Selecting one persists the `sys:`-prefixed id to `philogg-ui-font` exactly like any curated choice. `desktop/package.json` gained `font-list` as a real dependency (desktop-only, doesn't touch `philogg.html`'s own zero-dependency rule). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 113 (new, two sub-groups): (a) confirms nothing changes without `window.philogg` (plain build); (b) stubs `listSystemFonts` (via a new `opts.philogg` hook in the shared `withApp()` test helper, applied in `beforeParse` so it's present before the inline script's own `initUiFont()` runs) returning `["Fira Code", "Iosevka", "Arial"]`, and asserts the optgroup appears, "Fira Code" is offered and selectable, "Arial" is NOT duplicated, and the resulting `--font-ui`/localStorage value are correct. - **Electron settings + cache now live in the OS well-known config directory** (this session, 2026-08-26, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #33, retired; person-requested, plan agreed before implementation). `desktop/main.js` gained `app.setName("PhiLogg")` so `app.getPath("userData")` resolves consistently to `~/.config/PhiLogg` (Linux) / `~/Library/Application Support/PhiLogg` (macOS) / `%APPDATA%\PhiLogg` (Windows) in both dev and packaged runs — the IndexedDB cache already lives there automatically once that path is right, no other change needed for it. Settings (`philogg.html`'s `philogg-*` `localStorage` keys) are mirrored into a new plain `settings.json` in the same directory: `desktop/preload.js` hydrates `localStorage` from it (real `setItem` calls, not a method override) before `philogg.html`'s own script runs — the only point early enough for the keys it reads once at top-level parse — while `main.js`'s new `watchSettings()` polls `localStorage` (~1s, same shape as the existing theme-overlay watcher) and writes changes back, with a best-effort flush on window close. The tray (previously only built lazily once "close to tray" fired) is now always created, so its context menu can offer "Open Config Folder" and "Clear Cache" (`session.clearStorageData` + reload) as the reachable UI for both files. `philogg.html` itself is untouched throughout — everything lives in `desktop/`. Not verified live (no display server in this environment, same standing limitation as the rest of `desktop/`) — reasoning checked against Electron's own `app.getPath`/`contextIsolation`/`session.clearStorageData` docs, not guessed. See "Desktop wrapper" → "Persistent settings + cache location" above and `desktop/README.md` → "Persistent data (settings + cache)" for the full writeup. No `philogg.html` changes, so the regression suite wasn't affected by this entry itself — see "Merged `main`'s font-picker work into this branch" below for the post-merge full-suite count.
- **Three person-reported follow-up bugfixes to the previous session's Settings work** (this session, 2026-08-26). All three confirmed with a real Chromium (Playwright) session, not just jsdom, before fixing. **(1) Log text size didn't trigger relayout.** `ROW_HEIGHT`/`EXTRACT_ROW_HEIGHT`/`LINK_PAIR_ROW_HEIGHT` (the fixed-pixel row heights the main log table, extraction table, and link/pair views enforce via inline `style.height`) were all `const`, while their rows' actual font-size already grows with `--log-text-scale` (see "Virtualized row rendering" and the extraction-table/link-view sections) — a larger Log text size grew the text right out of a still-base-size row, overflowing/clipping every cell (verified: a real row's rendered height stuck at 28px while `getComputedStyle` showed its font-size visibly past that). Fixed by making all three `let` (paired with a never-mutated `*_BASE` constant) and rescaling them proportionally to `logTextScale` inside `applyLogTextScale()`, followed by a real `render()` call so whichever view is on screen (`renderTable`/`renderHighlightView`/`renderExtractTable`/`renderLinkView`, all dispatched from `renderMainView`) redraws immediately with the new heights — `renderTable()`'s own unconditional `computeMaxMessageWidth()` call picks up the wider message-column measurement in the same pass, no separate cache invalidation needed. `LINK_DELTA_HEIGHT` and the multiline-message/note per-line heights were checked and left alone — their CSS font-sizes are fixed px, not tied to `--log-text-scale`, so nothing there is actually affected. **(2) "On open, scroll log to: End" fired before a large file finished loading.** `renderTable()` consumed and deleted the one-shot `_openScrollToEnd` flag on the very first render with any non-empty `currentViewEntries` — which, for a large file, is the first `PARSE_CHUNK_LINES` parse chunk, not the true end (see "File loading" above for the incremental-append parse). Fixed to mirror how Tailing already keeps trailing a growing bottom: the flag now only gets *deleted* once `rootNode.loadFraction` is no longer a number (`loadOneFileIntoTree`'s `finally` deletes it exactly when reading+parsing both finish) — every render before that still scrolls to the current bottom, so the view keeps trailing the growing file through every intermediate tick and settles at the TRUE end once loading actually completes. Composes naturally with Tailing (which only engages its own poll-driven follow after the load loop finishes, by which point this flag is already gone) — verified both Tailing off + open-to-End and Tailing on + open-to-End. **(3) Loading a second file left the first file's tree row still visually highlighted** (person screenshots, dark theme, drag-and-drop). Root cause: `createFileNode`/`activateQueuedFileNode` set `state.activeId` on every new file load but never touched `state.multiSelect` — and any ordinary (non-Ctrl) tree-row click already sets `state.multiSelect = new Set([id])` for that row (`renderNode`'s click handler), not just a Ctrl-click multi-select gesture. If the person had at any point clicked the first file's tree row (e.g. plain navigation, exactly what "having a log entry selected" correlated with in their repro), that id stayed in `state.multiSelect` forever — the second file's row correctly got `.active` (own `--accent-soft` background), but the first file's row ALSO kept rendering `.multi-selected` (the SAME `--accent-soft` background, via a completely different CSS rule/state field), looking identical to two files being highlighted at once. Not the drag-over CSS class initially suspected (row-level `dragover`/`drop` handlers are gated on an in-tree filter-node drag in progress, `draggedNodeId`, and never fire for an external OS file drop). Fixed by clearing `state.multiSelect` alongside `state.activeId` in both `createFileNode` and `activateQueuedFileNode` (the actual path a real drag-and-drop load takes, via `loadFileDescriptors`) — a fresh file load now starts with a clean tree selection, same as a real click on its own row would. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 112 (new, three sub-groups): (a) asserts a rendered row's `style.height` scales proportionally with Log text size and resets cleanly, plus `EXTRACT_ROW_HEIGHT`/`LINK_PAIR_ROW_HEIGHT` (now exposed via the `T` bridge) doing the same; (b) simulates a still-loading file (`loadFraction` a number) across several `renderTable()` ticks with a growing stubbed `scrollHeight`, asserting the flag stays held and the view keeps re-trailing the new bottom until `loadFraction` is deleted, then that the one-shot flag doesn't re-fire afterward; (c) reproduces the exact click-then-load-second-file scenario and asserts exactly one tree row carries `.active`/`.multi-selected` afterward. - **Icon swaps, Escape closes Settings, file-selection clarity bugfix, "On open, scroll log to" setting, Font size split into UI scale / Log text size, UI font family setting** (this session, 2026-08-26, person-requested batch). **Icons**: `#btnSettings` now uses a gear/cog SVG instead of the old sun icon; the shared `ICON_PIN` constant (`#btnPinBookmarks`, the sidebar peek toggle, the detail-panel peek toggle) is now a plain thumbtack (circle head + needle) instead of the old map-pin/teardrop outline. **Escape**: the global `Escape` keydown handler now also calls `closeSettingsDialog()`, alongside the popups it already closed. **File-selection clarity bugfix** (person-reported, German: *"Klare Darstellung in welcher Datei man ist, besonders beim neu laden -> sieht aus als wäre die alte und die aktuelle Version selektiert. Hintergrundselektion wird nicht geupdated."*). Root cause: `createFileNode` unconditionally set `state.activeId = node.id` on every call, including each of the N calls `restoreSessionFromCache` makes (once per restored file, via `addFile`) while restoring a session at boot — the tree's `.tree-row.active` background highlight visibly thrashed through every restored file in sequence (verified with a mocked `render()` call-count/activeId trace: with 3 files restored and the persisted-active one NOT the last, the highlight bounced through all 3 before `restoreSessionFromCache`'s own final `s.active` correction snapped it back) before landing on the actually-persisted active file — never two rows genuinely active at once (`renderTree()` always rebuilds the whole tree from scratch, so exactly one row ever carries `.active` on any single render), but a real, visible flicker across the wrong files first. Fixed by gating that assignment on `!sessionRestoreInProgress` (the flag `restoreSessionFromCache` already sets for its whole run) — restore now leaves `state.activeId` untouched until the loop's own single, correct, final assignment; a new `else` branch there also falls back to the first restored file when no active node was persisted at all (previously would have silently stayed on whatever a leftover `createFileNode` call happened to set, now explicit). **"On open, scroll log to" setting** (Settings → Behavior, `#settingsOpenScrollPosition`, `philogg-open-scroll-position`, default `"start"` = unchanged existing behavior): `"end"` sets a one-shot `_openScrollToEnd` flag on a root file node at creation (`createFileNode`/`createQueuedFileNode`), consumed by `renderTable()`'s "no scroll anchor to restore" fallback the first time that file's `currentViewEntries` is actually non-empty — checked *before* `restoreViewAnchor` rather than after: a just-created file's own `getVisibleEntries()` call (from its zero-entries creation render) and its post-parse completion render read the exact same underlying `node.entries` array by reference, so a naive anchor check after `restoreViewAnchor` would find a coincidental "self-match" (the file's own first entry, captured before parsing and matched again after) and silently keep the scroll at the top instead — the flag now short-circuits ahead of that. Since `state.tailFollow` already defaults `true`, landing at the bottom means a live-tailed file continues following from there with no extra wiring. **Font size split** (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #42+44): the old single "Font size" row/`fontScale` (whole-page CSS `zoom`, `philogg-font-scale`) is now "UI scale" — same mechanism, same storage key (no migration needed, still just the whole-UI zoom) — plus a new, independent "Log text size" (`logTextScale`, `philogg-log-text-scale`, defaults to 100 = no extra change on top of UI scale, so upgrading users see no visual difference until they touch either slider). Log text size does NOT reuse `zoom` — nesting a second `zoom` under the log content specifically would throw off `measureMsgWidth`'s column-width math (that function's own comment already documents `offsetWidth` being zoom-invariant only relative to the single root-level zoom it was verified against). Instead it drives a new `--log-text-scale` CSS custom property (`:root`, default `1`), multiplied via `calc()` into the font-size of the actual log/text content rules only — `.col-time`/`.col-delta`/`.level-badge`/`.col-thread`/`.col-loc`/`.col-msg` (main table, reused by the Full/Highlight view's row renderer), `#detailMessage` (entry detail), `#extractTable` (extraction table), `.pair-row-time`/`.pair-row-msg` (Link view) — toolbars/panels/buttons are untouched by it, only by UI scale. **UI font family setting** (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #46, Settings → Appearance, `#settingsUiFontSelect`, `philogg-ui-font`, default `"default"`): a curated list of system-installed font stacks only (`UI_FONT_OPTIONS` — system default, Arial/Helvetica, Georgia, Verdana, Monospace via `var(--font-mono)`) — no `@font-face`, no remote fetches, per the "no dependencies" rule — applied by overriding the `--font-ui` custom property `body` (and most UI chrome) already keys off, same live-apply pattern as the theme setting. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 111 (new, six sub-groups a–f) covers all of the above: icon shape identity, Escape closing Settings, the restore-thrash fix (mocked render-call trace + final single-`.active`-row assertion) and its no-persisted-active fallback, both scroll-position options plus the one-shot-flag/no-re-trigger-on-later-switch case, UI scale vs. Log text size updating fully independently (including which CSS var/zoom each one leaves untouched), and the UI font select (options exist, none reference anything external, selecting one updates `--font-ui`). GROUP 79's row-count assertion updated in place (Appearance's Theme+Font-size card now holds 5 rows: Theme, UI font, Accent color, UI scale, Log text size). 2098 passed, 0 failed.

A module-level `isQuitting` flag, set in `app.on("before-quit", ...)`, distinguishes an actual quit from the hide-to-tray interception. `focusWindow()` now also un-hides (not just un-minimizes) the window, so reopening a file or clicking the tray icon while parked in the tray feels instant rather than like a fresh relaunch. The node is `locked: true`: colourable/styleable like any other filter node (its own highlight swatch works normally), but `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo`/`moveFilterNodeWithUndo`/`renderNode`'s drag wiring all refuse to touch it, and the tree context menu offers only "Copy" for it — only `syncBookmarksFilterNode` itself ever adds/removes it. See "Bookmarks"/"Notes" above for the full mechanism. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`: Group 15 rewritten (old panel assertions replaced with node-creation/removal/locked/matching coverage), Group 104 (new, Notes), Group 20/21 (session cache/export persistence) and Group 29b (toolbar button shape) updated for the new `#btnNotes` button and the auto node's extra tree child. 1968 passed, 0 failed.
