(3) `box-shadow:inset 0 -2px 0 0 var(--accent)` instead (draws strictly inside the box, sidestepping the box-model issue) — screenshotted as showing no line at all. (4) Person re-clarified via devtools screenshots what "the line" should actually be: back at the TOP after all — flush with `#timelineMinimapDragRect`'s own top edge (which JS already pins to `timelineMinimapSvg.offsetTop`/`offsetHeight`, so it reads as "inside the SVG's own top edge" despite being a DOM sibling of `<svg>`, not a child — kept that way on purpose, see the comment above it, so a mid-drag `renderTimelineMinimap()` rebuild from a tail tick can't wipe the in-progress selection; genuinely nesting it inside `<svg>` would reopen that exact bug). Landed on the ORIGINAL styling, `border:1px solid var(--accent)` on all four sides — the one shape confirmed visible across every screenshot — net effect of this whole round-trip is "no functional change from before this session," now with the reasoning for keeping it documented inline. Pure CSS, no jsdom coverage possible (same "jsdom blind spot" as other pure-positioning fixes, see `tests/README.md`). #17, middle-click (`auxclick`, button 1) on a filter-tree row or a breadcrumb chip now deletes that node via the same `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo` path as its ✕ button/context-menu Remove — `mousedown` on button 1 is `preventDefault`'d first to suppress the browser's autoscroll-icon cursor. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 100 (new). #36 (Electron only), "show minimize/close controls in F11 fullscreen too": `desktop/main.js`'s F11 handler no longer calls real `setFullScreen()` on Windows/Linux — Chromium only paints the Window Controls Overlay (the native-look minimize/close buttons `titleBarOverlay` draws) inside a titlebar area, which doesn't exist in genuine OS fullscreen, so the buttons vanished. Replaced with a manual "windowed fullscreen" (`toggleWindowedFullscreen`): resize the window to exactly its display's bounds via `screen.getDisplayMatching()`, tracked per-window in a `WeakMap` for restore-on-toggle-back. A window that exactly fills its display gets the same square-corners/no-frame treatment from the OS compositor a maximized window does (see `ROUNDED_CORNERS` below), and since `titleBarStyle`/`titleBarOverlay` are never touched, the overlay buttons keep rendering exactly as they do while merely maximized — reusing that existing visualization instead of building a second custom control set. macOS keeps real `setFullScreen()` unchanged (native fullscreen already reveals the traffic lights on a mouse move to the top edge). Not verified live (no display server in this environment, same standing limitation as the rest of `desktop/`) — behavior derived from Electron's own `titleBarOverlay`/WCO docs. #9, "'Newest' button only for trailing files": first pass wrongly reported this as already implemented, on the strength of `activeTailedRoot()` gating on `root.tail` being set — person-corrected same day (*"zu Beginn werden ja aktuell alle Dateien als trailing betrachtet"*): `node.tail` is set for any file opened via a real `FileSystemFileHandle` (the common case — `showOpenFilePicker`/drag-drop both hand one over) and is never cleared once the file stops growing, so that gate really only excluded the rare legacy `<input type=file>` fallback path, not static files in general. Actually fixed by routing `activeTailedRoot()` through `isTailLive()`/`TAIL_LIVE_MS` (the same staleness window Group 63a already established for the tree row's live dot) instead of the bare existence check, and having `tailTick()`'s liveness-only pass (no bytes read, just time passing) call `updateTailJumpBtn()` in addition to its existing `renderTree()`, so the button catches the staleness flip on its own rather than waiting for some unrelated render. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 101 (new): button hides once a tailed file goes quiet past the staleness window, reappears on a genuine new write, stays available for a just-opened handle that hasn't grown yet (`lastGrowth == null` counts as live, matching the tree dot), and stays hidden for a file with no `node.tail` at all.

- **Removed: breadcrumb "hover to reveal children" flyout** (this session, 2026-08-25, person-requested: *"Entferne die Breadcrumb Hover Navigation. Diese ist mit der letzten Änderung überflüssig geworden."*). Superseded by Alt+Arrow tree navigation (see the entry directly below) — holding Alt and using the arrow keys reaches the same "navigate into a child filter without opening the sidebar" goal without a mouse, at which point the older hover flyout was pure redundant surface area. Deleted outright rather than left dead: both Settings → Behavior toggles (`#settingsHoverChildNav`/`-Multistep`), `#childNavFlyoutHost` and its CSS (`.child-nav-flyout`/`.child-nav-item`/`.child-nav-caret`/`.child-nav-empty-note`/`.hover-open`), and every supporting function (`openChildNavLevel`, `closeChildNavFlyouts`, `renderChildNavItem`, `positionChildNavPanel`, `cancelChildNavClose`/`scheduleChildNavClose`, `childNavPanels`) — `renderBreadcrumb()`'s per-chip click handler (jump to an ancestor) and the Escape handler's now-shorter close list are otherwise unaffected. `buildLevelNodeLabelContent`/`nodeIconHTML`/`nodeDisplayName`'s own comments trimmed of their "and the flyout too" mentions, since there's no longer a second call site to keep in sync with. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 92 deleted entirely (its own feature no longer exists); Group 95's now-dangling `renderChildNavItem` assertion removed; Group 98's card-membership check dropped the two removed setting ids. See "Header / toolbar layout" above (the writeup itself was removed, replaced with a short pointer here) and "Tree interactions" → the Alt+Arrow entry. 1900 passed, 0 failed.

- **Follow-up: split the sidebar peek into two independent mechanisms — Ctrl+0's persists until focus leaves the panel, Alt's is a plain hold-to-peek toggle** (this session, 2026-08-25, same-day person-requested refinement of the entry directly below). The single `sidebarForcedPeek` flag from the previous entry closed on releasing WHICHEVER modifier opened it — which read wrong for Ctrl+0, since releasing Ctrl happens immediately after typing that shortcut, closing the panel before anyone could actually look at it. Split into two: `sidebarForcedPeek` (Ctrl+0) now stays open until focus genuinely leaves the panel — picking a filter (`Enter` while the tree has focus), switching Full/Filtered/Stacked view, or clicking outside the sidebar — and is no longer tied to Ctrl's own release at all; a new `sidebarAltPeek` (Alt, held) is a plain toggle open for exactly as long as Alt is physically down, tracked via dedicated `keydown`/`keyup` listeners (`altKeyPhysicallyDown`) so even holding Alt alone (no arrow) peeks the panel open. Alt+Enter's own dialog can open while Alt is still down, so closing `sidebarAltPeek` is deferred (`maybeCloseSidebarAltPeek`) until BOTH Alt is up and that dialog has actually closed, checked from both places. See "Tree interactions" above (extended in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 99 extended in place (not a new group). 1929 passed, 0 failed.

- **Follow-up: Alt+Arrow instead of Ctrl+Arrow for tree navigation, releasing the modifier closes a forced sidebar peek** (this session, 2026-08-25, two same-day person-requested follow-ups to the entry directly below). Ctrl+Arrow's tree-forwarding was renamed to **Alt+Arrow** — Ctrl+Arrow is standard OS/app behavior for jumps within a document (e.g. "next page") and needed to stay free for that; Alt+Enter (extraction) already used Alt, and Alt+Arrow doesn't collide with anything else in the app. Separately, releasing whichever modifier forced a sidebar peek open now closes it back up again — a new document-level `keyup` listener calls `clearSidebarForcedPeek()` on `"Control"`/`"Meta"`/`"Alt"`, safe unconditionally since a forced peek only ever exists because the sidebar WAS collapsed to begin with, so closing it on release just restores the pre-peek state (a peek opened by mouse hover isn't affected — `sidebarForcedPeek` is only ever true for a keyboard-forced one). See "Tree interactions" above (extended in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 99 extended in place (not a new group), new `fireKeyup` test helper alongside the existing `fireKeydown`. 1923 passed, 0 failed.
- **Alt+Arrow tree-navigation forwarding + Ctrl+0 sidebar peek, Alt+Enter extraction, temporary anchor on a no-match filter switch** (this session, 2026-08-25, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #18, person-requested). Retires backlog item 18. Person-requested design: a first attempt this same session built a centered, keyboard-navigable popup over the breadcrumb's own child-nav flyout for cross-view filter navigation — after trying it live, reverted (branch reset to before it existed, not layered with revert commits, so there's no trace of it in the history) in favor of this simpler approach, which reuses the existing "Files & Filters" tree/sidebar instead of introducing a new picker UI (a same-day follow-up then moved the tree-forwarding key from its initial Ctrl+Arrow to Alt+Arrow — see the entry directly above). Three things landed: **(1)** `Alt+Arrow` (any direction) forwards straight to `moveTreeSelection` without moving keyboard focus off the current Log view (`state.focusRegion` untouched), and `Ctrl+0` — unchanged otherwise — now also force-peeks a collapsed sidebar open (`setSidebarForcedPeek`/`clearSidebarForcedPeek`, reusing the pre-existing hover-peek `.peeking` overlay but keeping it open until the person switches Full/Filtered/Stacked view or clicks outside the sidebar). **(2)** `Alt+Enter` opens "Filter for this message" for the selected row directly (`openFilterForEntryColumn`, shared with the right-click menu item). **(3)** Switching the active filter (tree click or Alt+Arrow) while the selected row doesn't match it shows that row at its would-be position as a temporary anchor (`state.tempAnchor`) instead of losing it — new Settings → Behavior three-way mode (Off/Persistent/Fade, its fade-duration row a −/value/+ stepper matching Font Size, shown only for Fade) in the "Filter tree" card. The pre-existing breadcrumb hover-flyout feature (see "Header / toolbar layout" below) is completely unaffected — this session never touches it. See "Tree interactions" above for the full mechanism. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 99 (new). 1921 passed, 0 failed.
- **`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`: seven new items added, 43–49** (this session, 2026-08-24, person-supplied as a list from an external note-taking app, no code changes). Appended to the end of their respective existing groups rather than renumbering, per the file's own policy: #43 (create a filter from the visible plot area) → Navigation & reading; #44 (extend match-text highlighting to Highlights) → Filter tree workflow; #45 (show minimize/close controls in `F11` fullscreen) → Desktop wrapper; #46 (`Ctrl+0` expands Files & Filters panel if collapsed), #47 (font size and UI size independently configurable, companion to existing #41), #48 (theme follows OS light/dark state), #49 (setting to change UI font family, must not break "no dependencies") → Settings & UI polish. Docs-only, `philogg.html` untouched — no regression-suite impact, full suite not re-run for this entry (no code changed).
- **`F11` fullscreen toggle + rounded window corners for the Electron desktop wrapper** (this session, 2026-08-24, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` items 31/34, person-requested). Both scoped entirely to `desktop/main.js`: F11 is caught via a per-window `before-input-event` listener (no app menu exists to hang a menu accelerator on) and toggles `win.setFullScreen()`; rounded corners are requested explicitly via the `BrowserWindow` constructor's own `roundedCorners: true` option (Electron's default already — made explicit here). The "except when fullscreen" half of item 34 needed no code: a window that exactly fills the screen is squared off automatically by the OS compositor on both macOS and Windows, so it falls out for free once the window can go fullscreen at all (item 31). See "Desktop wrapper" → "Frameless window" above for the full writeup, including the per-platform caveats (Windows pre-11-Build-22000 stays square either way; Linux is desktop-environment/compositor-dependent, outside this app's control). Not verified live — no display server in this environment, same standing limitation as the rest of `desktop/`; reasoning checked against Electron's own `BrowserWindow` option docs, not guessed. Doesn't touch `philogg.html` — no new regression-suite coverage (jsdom suite doesn't reach Electron's main process, same as every other `desktop/main.js`-only change in this changelog); full suite re-run clean as a sanity check, 1881 passed, 0 failed.
- **`README.md` intro/format section rewritten** (this session, 2026-08-24, person-requested: remove references to LogViewPlus, stop framing log4net as *the* supported format when it's actually one freely configurable option with log4net only as the zero-config fallback, and replace the "local, single-file, offline-capable" intro — which reads as if "single-file" meant "single log file" — with a clearer intro plus an explicit list of the deployment options (open the file directly / self-host and serve over `http(s)`, e.g. for CI deep-links / the Electron desktop wrapper) and each one's actual benefit. All three remaining `LogViewPlus` mentions in `README.md` (intro, and the format-manager feature bullet) removed; "The log format it reads" section reordered to lead with configurability (`Settings → Format Manager`) before describing the log4net-style pattern as the no-configuration fallback. Docs-only change, `philogg.html` untouched — no regression-suite impact, full suite not re-run for this entry (no code changed).
- **`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` renumbered top-to-bottom, sequentially** (this session, 2026-08-23, person-requested alongside the fix directly below — "the numbering... currently some are used multiple times, just number the items top to bottom"). The previous reorg's "leave retired numbers in place" policy had left the file's IDs jumping around non-sequentially (1–7, then 38–40, then 8–9, 11, 13–14, …) as items were completed and dropped — resequenced every remaining item 1..42 in its current top-to-bottom order, content otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged (verified via a set diff against the pre-renumbering file: exactly one item removed — see below — nothing else added, dropped, or reworded). Deliberately did **not** chase down historical numeric references to already-completed items in `philogg.html`/`PROJECT.md` comments (e.g. `FEATURE_BACKLOG #10`/`#12`/`#13`/`#25`) — those point at features long since implemented and removed from the backlog itself, predate this renumbering, and were already identified as intentionally-left-alone "historical, dated references" by the prior reorg's own changelog entry; they don't correspond to any current backlog entry either way. The file's own header note about leaving retired numbers going forward is unchanged — this was a one-time cleanup pass, not a policy change.
- **Bugfix: Electron window control buttons (fullscreen/minimize/close) had the wrong background color** (this session, 2026-08-23, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item, person-reported — "ideally Electron would keep the button backgrounds transparent and the buttons themselves would pick up the active theme's highlight color"). Root cause: `OVERLAY_COLORS` in `desktop/main.js` hardcoded exactly two `{color, symbolColor}` pairs, picked by whether `data-theme` was `"light"` or anything else — which only ever matched the original Dark/Light built-ins. Every Catppuccin flavor (each with its own `--bg-panel`, none of them `#151924`/`#ffffff`) and any custom imported theme got an overlay background that didn't match the toolbar underneath it; the per-theme accent re-pick (`applyAccentChoice`, changes `--accent` without changing `data-theme` at all) wasn't tracked either, since the poll only ever compared theme id, not the actual color. Fixed exactly as the backlog suggested: `watchTheme()` now sets the overlay's `color` to a fully-transparent `#00000000` unconditionally (Electron's documented `#RRGGBBAA` mechanism for a transparent Window Controls Overlay, landed well before the `^43.0.0` this project pins — see electron/electron#33567/#38693) instead of trying to hardcode a matching background per theme at all, and reads the renderer's own live `getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue("--accent")` for `symbolColor` (polled the same ~1x/second way `data-theme` was) instead of a fixed light/dark text-secondary guess — so the buttons themselves pick up the active theme's actual highlight color, exactly as requested, and the background always matches whatever `#toolbar` paints underneath it for ANY theme, built-in or custom, with no per-theme color table to keep in sync. `createWindow`'s initial (pre-`dom-ready`) overlay uses the same transparent background with the default (dark) theme's `--accent` as a placeholder `symbolColor` until the first poll resolves, same pattern the old code already used for its initial paint. Scoped entirely to `desktop/main.js` (no `philogg.html` changes) — no new regression-suite coverage (the jsdom suite loads `philogg.html` only, not Electron's main process, same as every other `desktop/main.js`-only fix in this changelog); full suite re-run clean as a sanity check, 1881 passed, 0 failed. Not independently re-verified live (no display server in this environment, same standing limitation noted throughout "Desktop wrapper" below) — reasoning checked against Electron's own WCO transparent-overlay docs/issue history, not guessed; a known, still-open upstream Electron limitation (electron/electron#48193) means the native hover-highlight color behind the symbol may not itself follow the accent color even once this ships — outside this fix's control.
- **Settings "Behavior" section split into subsections** (this session, 2026-08-23, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #30, "Settings cleanup — more internal structure/subdivisions within the existing sections... which today are each just a flat list of rows"). Of the three sections, Appearance and Log Formats already used `.settings-subsection-title` + a second `.settings-card` (Appearance's "Custom themes", Log Formats' "Filename rules" — part of the 2026-08-22 Settings dialog redesign); Behavior was the one still a single flat card of 9 unrelated rows. Split into a standalone general row (quit-on-last-close, un-headed, same pattern as Appearance's own first card), then three headed subsections: "Hover-to-expand panels" (`#settingsHoverExpandSidebar`/`-Detail`, `#settingsHoverChildNav`/`-Multistep`), "Filter tree" (`#settingsLevelFilterTreeMode`), and "Text-match highlighting" (`#settingsTextMatchHighlightScope`/`-Rows`/`-Detail`). Pure markup regrouping — no ids, CSS, or JS wiring changed (the section-nav `IntersectionObserver` only watches top-level `.settings-section` elements, unaffected by cards moving within one). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 98 (new): asserts the 3 subsection titles and 4 cards exist in the right order and that each row's id resolves inside its intended card, plus a smoke-toggle that the relocated quit-on-close switch still fires its listener. 1881 passed, 0 failed.
- **Live tail auto-follow now applies to the Highlight/Full view too, and scrolling/jumping to the bottom re-engages follow the same as the jump button** (this session, 2026-08-23, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #25, person-requested: *"Live trailing auf allen Views ermöglichen. ein scrollen/springen (zb über Default 'ans letzte Zeile springen' shortcut) soll den gleichen Effekt haben wie der jump Button..."*). `state.tailFollow` is now one shared flag driving both `#tableBody` (Filtered) and `#highlightBody` (Highlight/Full) — previously the Highlight view stayed deliberately static while only the Filtered view followed tailed growth. `onTailChange` now sets a `pendingHighlightScroll` twin of the existing `pendingTableScroll`, consumed by `renderHighlightView()` the same way `renderTable()` already consumed its own (scroll to bottom on follow, otherwise leave the position exactly where it was — never reset to top). A second floating "Newest" button, `#tailJumpBtnHighlight` (`.tail-jump-btn` shared styling with the original `#tailJumpBtn`), sits over the Highlight panel; both call the same `jumpToTailedNewest()`. New `activeTailedRoot()`/`isTailedHighlightViewActive()` helpers alongside the existing `isTailedLogViewActive()` (now Filtered-pane-specific only); `applySelection` (shared by `selectEntry`/`selectHighlightEntry`) disengages follow on a row click in **either** view now, checking both. Second half of the request: a manual scroll/jump that lands back within reach of the bottom of a tailed view — not just clicking the jump button — now re-engages follow too (`maybeReengageTailFollow()`, called from both `#tableBody`'s and `#highlightBody`'s scroll listeners once `distFromBottom` drops back under the same `ROW_HEIGHT*1.5` threshold that triggers disengage on the way out). `showFhTab`/`applyFhView` also resync a panel that was hidden (and so couldn't actually scroll — `scrollHeight` collapses while `display:none`) to the bottom when it becomes visible again, if follow is still active. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 97 (new): tail growth following both views, disengage-on-scroll-away and re-engage-on-scroll-back for both views, a row click in the Highlight view disengaging follow, and the new button's jump+re-engage behavior. 1865 passed, 0 failed.

- **Rename / label filter nodes** (this session, 2026-08-23, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #12, person-requested with two specific decisions up front: `F2` for rename, and a new shortcut proposed for the Edit dialog it displaced — chose `Ctrl+E`, matching the existing `Ctrl+F`/`Ctrl+C`/`Ctrl+X`/`Ctrl+V`/`Ctrl+W`/`Ctrl+B`/`Ctrl+J`/`Ctrl+Z`/`Ctrl+Y` mnemonic-letter shortcut family). `F2`/right-click "Rename…" sets a `node.label` shown instead of the raw auto-derived `node.name` everywhere a node's name is displayed to a person (tree row, breadcrumb, child-nav flyout, context menu header, Link dialog, save-file/save-to-library default names) — `node.name` itself stays untouched, so Edit (now `Ctrl+E`, freeing `F2`) and anything reading the actual pattern is unaffected. Inline edit-in-place in the tree row itself (`renderRenameInput` swaps `.tree-label` for a text input; Enter/Escape/blur), undoable through the existing generic edit-undo machinery, and specifically overrides `"level"` nodes' own per-word coloring once labeled. See "Tree interactions" above for the full mechanism and every touched call site; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 96 (new), Groups 18/32/46 updated in place for the `F2`→`Ctrl+E` shortcut move (their own Edit-dialog coverage, not new behavior). 1849 passed, 0 failed.

- **"level" node label coloring: dropped the gradient, each word its own solid color** (this session, 2026-08-23, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below, person-requested: *"ohne Farbgradient, jedes Wort in der eigenen Farbe"*). The entry directly below's `background-clip:text` gradient for a combined node (e.g. "ERROR, INFO" blending red into blue) is gone — `buildLevelNodeLabelContent(node)` now builds the label as real DOM nodes, one `<span>` per level name with its own plain `style.color`, comma-separated with plain text nodes, so "ERROR, INFO" reads as a solid red word and a solid blue word instead of a blend. Built via `createElement`/`textContent`, not an HTML string, so a corrupt/hand-edited import can't inject markup through a `"level"` node's `value` array (an unrecognized entry just falls back to the default text color). See "Level bar: filter-tree mode" below (extended in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 95 assertions rewritten in place for the new per-word shape (not a new group). 1812 passed, 0 failed.
- **"level" filter-tree node labels colored by log level** (this session, 2026-08-23, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below, person-requested: *"schreibe die Einträge im Files & Filter Tree in der Farbe des Log Levels. auch kombiniert wenn mehrere Level an sind."*). A `"level"` node's tree-row label (`renderNode`) and its breadcrumb child-nav flyout counterpart (`renderChildNavItem`) are now tinted in the log level's own theme color: one active level gets a plain solid `var(--level-error|warn|info|debug)` color, several get a left-to-right gradient across them (in the node's own `value` array order) via `background-image:linear-gradient(...)` + `background-clip:text` + `color:transparent`. New shared helper `applyLevelNodeLabelColor`. Every other filter type's label is untouched. See "Level bar: filter-tree mode" below (extended in place). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 95 (new). 1811 passed, 0 failed. **Superseded same session** by the entry directly above (gradient replaced with per-word solid colors) — `applyLevelNodeLabelColor` no longer exists, renamed/reshaped into `buildLevelNodeLabelContent`.
- **Level bar can write into the filter tree** (this session, 2026-08-23, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #10, previously marked "(maybe)" — clarified via two person-decided questions before implementing: trigger mechanism and how multiple active levels are represented). New Settings → Behavior setting, `#settingsLevelFilterTreeMode` — **"auto" (default)**: clicking a level button upserts a real `filterType:"level"` tree node (`value` = array of active level names) at the current position, edited in place while the active node already is one, deleted (undoably) once every level is toggled back off; **"explicit"**: the level bar keeps its exact original tree-independent `state.levelFilter` behavior, plus a new `#btnApplyLevelToTree` ("Add to tree") button, shown only in this mode, that pushes the current selection into the tree on demand. Switching modes clears `state.levelFilter` so it can't silently bleed into the other one. A `"level"` node's array `value` rides the existing generic `value` field through every persistence carrier untouched — only `FILTER_TYPES` needed the new type added. See "Level bar: filter-tree mode" below for the full mechanism. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 94 (new); eight older groups (4/13/33/52/61a/61b/61d/61e) that click the level bar only incidentally now pin `levelFilterTreeMode = "explicit"` first, since "auto" becoming the default would otherwise silently change what those clicks do. 1804 passed, 0 failed.

- **Text-filter match highlighting** (this session, 2026-08-23, person-requested alternative implementation of `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` #13's "why is this row here" popup idea — that entry is deliberately left unchanged, this is a separate feature, not a replacement, German: *"wenn ein Textfilter in der Filterkette aktiv ist, dann möchte ich in dem Filterview... Textabschnitte in den Messages durch eine Markierung hervorheben, wo dieser Match zutrifft"*). Marks the exact substring an active `text` filter node matched, inline in the Filter view's rows and/or the entry-detail panel — see "Text-filter match highlighting" above for the full mechanism. Three-state master setting (Settings → Behavior, `#settingsTextMatchHighlightMode`): off / only the active filter ("last", **default**, no fallback to a text-filter ancestor) / any filter in the active chain ("any"). Two independent "where" toggles (`#settingsTextMatchHighlightRows`/`-Detail`, both default ON), only shown while the master isn't off. Covers wildcard/value-token text filters too (numeric conditions re-checked per occurrence), and highlights within whichever column a target-column-restricted filter actually applies to, not just the message. - **Breadcrumb "hover the current entry to reveal its children"** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested, mockup attached: *"Wenn ich über den aktuellen File/Filter Eintrag des aktuellen Pfads hover, möchte ich die Child Knoten als nächste Elemente auswählen können um den Baum so tiefer nach unten zu navigieren"*). Two new Settings → Behavior toggles: `#settingsHoverChildNav` (master on/off, default ON) and `#settingsHoverChildNavMultistep` (default OFF — off shows only the immediate children; on lets hovering a child cascade a further flyout for *its* children, arbitrarily deep, "wenn On kann man auch über die Childs hovern um dann deren Childs zu sehen und so weiter"). - **Bugfix, round 2: empty gap below the header in the collapsed detail panel** (this session, 2026-08-22, same-day person-reported follow-up screenshot to the width fix directly below: "Im Collapsed state sehe ich noch einen Abschnitt... ohne Inhalt den ich dort nicht erwarte. Das unterste Element sollte die 'Entry Detail' Zeile selbst sein."). Same root cause family as the entry below (the `#detailPanelInner` refactor), different property: the original `#detailPanel` rule declared `min-height:80px` immediately followed, later in the SAME rule, by `min-height:0` — last declaration of a property wins the cascade at equal specificity, so the 80px never actually applied; it was effectively dead weight, presumably a leftover floor for the manual drag-resizer from before that second declaration was added. Moving `min-height:0` onto the new `#detailPanelInner` (alongside the OTHER properties that belonged there) left `#detailPanel`'s own rule with only the now-live `min-height:80px` — and `min-height` clamps the *used* height upward regardless of where the smaller height comes from, so `toggleDetailCollapsed`'s `style.height="34px"` got silently clamped to 80px, leaving a real ~46px empty strip below the header instead of the header being the panel's actual bottom edge. `getComputedStyle(...).height` confirmed 80px via a live Playwright run before the fix, 34px after — jsdom's existing assertions never caught this because they check `detailPanel.style.height === "34px"` (the inline style string) not the resolved/rendered height, exactly the "no layout engine" gap "Testing approach" already documents. Fixed by restoring `min-height:0` on `#detailPanel`'s own rule, after `min-height:80px`, matching the original source order exactly. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 91 extended with a text-order regression guard (checks `min-height:0` appears AFTER `min-height:80px` in the raw rule text, not just that both are present — order is the whole bug). 1706 passed, 0 failed.
- **Bugfix: Entry Detail bar stopped reaching the right edge in EVERY state, not just while peeking** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-reported via screenshots of the pinned/collapsed/hovered states — "Pinned und Collapsed sehen falsch aus. Die Darstellung ist nach rechts beschränkt und geht nicht mehr bis zum Bildrand"). Root-caused via a real Playwright screenshot + `getComputedStyle` diff (jsdom's tests all stayed green throughout — no layout engine to catch this, see "Testing approach"). The `#detailPanelInner` refactor directly below removed `flex-direction:column` from `#detailPanel`'s own CSS (moved the whole `display:flex; flex-direction:column` declaration onto the new wrapper) — but `#detailPanel`'s `display` is ALSO set directly as an inline style from `renderMainView`/`renderTable` (`"flex"`/`"none"`, gating on whether any file is loaded — pre-existing, unrelated to this feature). An inline style only overrides the specific properties it sets, so with the CSS `display:flex` gone but the inline one still firing, `#detailPanel` stayed flex but silently fell back to `flex-direction`'s initial value, **row** — turning `#detailPanelInner` into a row-flex item that shrinks to its content's width instead of stretching to fill, in every state (pinned, collapsed, peeking alike), not only while hovering (peeking merely made it more visible since the peek overlay's OWN rule doesn't touch width, so the same shrink happened there too, just easier to miss against a floating box). Fixed by putting `display:flex; flex-direction:column;` back on `#detailPanel`'s own CSS rule — confirmed via `git show origin/main:philogg.html` that this exact declaration was there before this session touched the file at all, so it's a straight revert-and-reapply, not a new decision. Also resolves the "unexpected element visible below" the person noted in the collapsed screenshot — that was `#detailPanel`'s own full-width background showing past the narrow shrunk-to-content `#detailPanelInner`, not a real extra DOM element (`#detailPanel` is genuinely the last child of `#content`, confirmed via the same repro). `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 91 extended with a stylesheet-text guard for `#detailPanel`'s `flex-direction:column` (the same "assert the CSS text, not a jsdom-unreachable layout result" pattern Group 90 uses for its own pure-CSS fixes) — the width regression itself has no jsdom-observable signature, verified live instead. 1705 passed, 0 failed.
- **Follow-up: Entry Detail's hover-peek now looks like the expanded panel, not a message floating over a still-collapsed header; hover setting split per-panel** (this session, 2026-08-22, same-day person-reported follow-up to the entry directly below, German: *"Bei Entry Detail wird aktuell beim Hover der Message Inhalt oberhalb der 'Entry Detail' Zeile eingeblendet. Stattdessen sollte die Message wie im ausgeklappten Zustand darunter stehen, während die Entry Detail Zeile Log Level, Timestamp etc zeigt... Und mit dem 'anpinnen' button dort, wo sonst der collapse/expand button sitzt."* / *"Ich möchte 'Open collapsed views on hover' in den Settings für 'Files & Filter' und für 'Entry Detail' getrennt ein- und ausschalten können."*). Two fixes. **(1)** The first cut anchored only `#detailBody` above the still-34px header (which kept showing its collapsed truncated summary), so peeking looked like a message floating over a header that still read "collapsed" — not the expanded look. Root cause: unlike the sidebar, where `#sidebarNormalContent` already wraps header+content as one peekable unit, the detail panel's header and body were direct siblings with no such wrapper, so only the body could be repositioned as a unit. Fixed by introducing `#detailPanelInner` (wraps `#detailPanelHeader` + `#detailBody`, carries the flex-column layout moved off `#detailPanel` itself) and anchoring THAT as the peek overlay (`bottom:0` of the collapsed 34px `#detailPanel`, no `top`, growing upward) — header on top with the full field set, message below it, matching the expanded panel exactly, pin button right where the collapse/expand chevron always sits (`#detailToggle` didn't move — it was always inside the header, which is now correctly part of what peeks). `updateDetailPanel()` now branches on `isDetailCollapsed() && !isDetailPeeking()`, not just `isDetailCollapsed()`, so peeking shows the full level/time/thread/location/method fields instead of the one-line summary; the `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` handlers call it directly so the header updates the instant peek state changes. No drag handle either way — `#detailResizer`'s visibility was already tied to `isDetailCollapsed()` alone, which stays true throughout a peek. **(2)** The single "Open collapsed views on hover" setting became two independent ones, `#settingsHoverExpandSidebar`/`#settingsHoverExpandDetail` (`hoverExpandSidebar`/`hoverExpandDetail`, `philogg-hover-expand-sidebar`/`-detail` in `localStorage`, both still default ON) — person wanted to gate each panel's hover behavior separately rather than one combined switch. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 91 rewritten (not a new group — same feature surface, now testing the corrected structure). 1704 passed, 0 failed.
- **Entry Detail gets the sidebar's hover-peek behavior; both peeks are now content-sized and capped at 50%; new "Open collapsed views on hover" setting** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested, German: *"Entry Detail soll im eingeklappten Zustand das gleiche Hover verhalten bekommen wie Files & Filters... nur eben nach oben ausklappen"*). Three parts. **(1)** `#detailPanel` gained the exact same hover-peek mechanism the sidebar already had (see "Collapsible sidebar/detail panel" below): `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` while collapsed toggle a `.peeking` class, which reveals `#detailBody` — the SAME element/markup shown when expanded, no separate peek-only render — as a `position:absolute` overlay. It expands *upward* rather than rightward: `#detailBody` is anchored `bottom:100%` of the still-34px `#detailPanel` (which gained `position:relative` + `overflow:visible` while collapsed, mirroring `#sidebar`'s own), so it grows up from just above the header without pushing `#fhSplit`/`#extractWrap` down. The header's chevron swaps to the pin icon while peeking (`updateDetailToggleIcon()`, new, mirrors `updateSidebarToggleIcon()`); clicking it while peeking pins the panel fully open via the same `toggleDetailCollapsed()` used everywhere else. **(2)** Both peeks changed from a fixed manual-resize size to content-sized-but-capped: the sidebar's overlay was `width:270px` (whatever the last manual width happened to be) — now `width:max-content; max-width:50vw`, so a short tree shrinks the overlay and a long one is clamped at half the application's width (ellipsis on `.tree-label` already handled overflow at any width, unchanged). The new detail overlay is `max-height:50vh` the same way. **(3)** New Settings → Behavior toggle, "Open collapsed views on hover" (`#settingsHoverExpandCollapsed`, **default ON** — unlike the neighboring "quit on last close" toggle, this one guards an *existing* behavior, so defaulting off would silently change everyone's current experience), gates both panels' `mouseenter` handlers (`hoverExpandCollapsed`, same localStorage-flag mechanism as `quitOnLastFileClose`/`fontScale`); manual click-to-expand and the keyboard shortcuts are unaffected either way. Turning the setting off while a panel happens to be mid-peek exits peek immediately instead of leaving it stuck open until the next `mouseleave`. See "Collapsible sidebar/detail panel" below; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 91 (new). 1696 passed, 0 failed.
- **Follow-up: collapsed sidebar/detail panel hide their own drag handle entirely** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested follow-up to the entry directly below). `#sidebarResizer`/`#detailResizer` used to keep their new permanent grip bar visible even while their panel was collapsed (fixed 40px rail / 34px header, neither manually resizable in that state), reading as dead UI. `#sidebarResizer` is now hidden via a plain `.hidden{display:none}` class toggled from `toggleSidebarCollapsed` — safe there since nothing else drives that element's `display`. `#detailResizer` needed a different fix: it's ALSO shown/hidden imperatively via `style.display` from three other places (`renderMainView`'s no-files/extract-mode/normal branches and `renderTable`) based on whether files are loaded and the active node's filter type — an inline style always wins over a CSS class regardless of specificity, so a `.hidden` class toggled from `toggleDetailCollapsed` was silently overridden the next time any of those functions ran (caught by the new regression test, not by eye — see "Testing approach"). Fixed by centralizing into one `updateDetailResizerVisibility()` (state.rootIds.length > 0 && !isExtract && !isDetailCollapsed()), called from all four sites instead of each recomputing/overwriting `style.display` independently. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 80 extended (not a new group — same feature surface): asserts the resizer is actually hidden via `getComputedStyle` (not just `classList`) right after each collapse, and reappears on expand. 1673 passed, 0 failed.
- **Main window visual consistency fix** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested — extends the Settings dialog's own `.btn-mini` button system to the toolbar/view-bar, which had drifted into per-control hand-tuned CSS: three separate rounds of "height-parity bugfix" comments on `.crumb`/`.level-btn`/`#btnPinBookmarks` chasing pixel parity via line-height arithmetic instead of a shared box model). Two problems fixed. **(1)** Introduced a shared 28px, flex-centered control height reused by role instead of copy-pasted padding/line-height: an icon-button shape (28x28, `border-radius:7px`) for `.toolbar-icon-btn` — now also covers `#btnPinBookmarks`/`#btnMultilineMsg`/`#btnColumns` with no id-specific override needed — and a pill shape (`border-radius:20px`, height:28px) for `.level-btn`/`.crumb`; `.view-tabs`/`.view-tab` get the same 28px height via `height:100%` filling its container instead of a hand-computed line-height. `#btnOpen`/`#btnSession` (previously two byte-for-byte-identical rule blocks) were merged into one, also given the 28px height. All the "height-parity bugfix"/ROUND-1/ROUND-2 comments this replaced are gone. **(2)** The three panel resizers (`#sidebarResizer`/`#fhSplitResizer`/`#detailResizer`) were fully transparent at rest, only showing an accent line on hover/drag — between two `--bg-panel` surfaces this read as a stray dark seam (`--bg-app` showing through) rather than an intentional divider, almost certainly the reported "gap between Files & Filters and the minimap." Now show a permanent grip bar (`var(--border)` at rest, `var(--accent)` on hover/drag). Also fixed the actual token mismatch at that seam: `#timelineMinimap`'s `border-bottom` used `--border-soft` while `#sidebar`'s `border-right` (the adjoining seam) used `--border` — both now `--border`. Pure CSS, no functional/DOM changes; `.tree-action-btn`/`.folder-watch-reconnect`/`.tree-ghost-restore-btn` deliberately left alone (not part of a row requiring cross-control alignment). See "Visual language" → "Shared control box model" above. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 90 (new); Group 26/29b rewritten in place to assert the new 28px-height model instead of the superseded line-height arithmetic; Group 86a's `#btnOpen:hover` selector-text lookup updated for the merged rule. 1669 passed, 0 failed.
- **Bugfix: a file opened via desktop launch argument now gets tailed** (this session, 2026-08-22, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item 11). `loadUrlIntoTree` — the single path behind every desktop-wrapper file open, including the very first file passed as a command-line/launch argument (`desktop/main.js` `fileArgFromArgv`/`createWindow`), a later file-association double-click (`second-instance`/`open-file`, via `window.philoggLoadUrl`), and an ordinary `?url=` CI report link — built its node purely from already-fetched text via `addFile()`, so it never got a `node.tail`: a live log opened this way just sat as a dead byte snapshot even though the desktop wrapper serves it straight off disk, where it can keep growing. Fixed by recognizing the desktop wrapper's own `philogg://local/<id>/…` scheme (`isDesktopLocalUrl`) — every fetch to it re-reads the file fresh via `fs.promises.readFile`, see `registerProtocol` in `desktop/main.js` — and attaching a `node.tail` whose handle (`urlTailHandle`) just re-fetches that same URL and wraps the bytes in a `Blob`, reusing the existing `tailTick` poll loop (see "Tailing") completely unchanged. An ordinary `http(s)` `?url=` deliberately stays untouched (no repeated background fetches to an arbitrary report link). As a side effect this also covers the backlog item's softer second ask ("periodically re-check static files for changes"): a desktop-opened file that never grows still gets polled every `TAIL_POLL_MS`, instead of never being looked at again after the initial load. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 89 (new); Groups 66/67's fetch mocks updated from `text()` to `arrayBuffer()` to match the production code's switch (needed for correct byte-offset tracking). 1650 passed, 0 failed.
- **Catppuccin background hierarchy remapped to match the style guide** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-directed follow-up to the compliance audit below — asked explicitly whether to keep the app's own pre-existing brightness ordering or remap to the guide, chose remap). `--bg-app`/`--bg-panel`/`--bg-elevated`/`--bg-elevated-2`/`--border-soft`/`--border` now map to Base/Mantle/Surface0/Surface1/Surface2/Overlay0 respectively — the same role assignment across all four Catppuccin flavors — instead of the previous Crust/Mantle/Base/Surface0/Surface1/Surface0 ordering that preserved this app's own Dark/Light hierarchy (main pane darkest, chrome brighter) at the cost of inverting the guide's intent (main pane = Base, a brighter tone; chrome = the darker Crust/Mantle). Catppuccin themes' sidebar/toolbar now read visibly darker than the log table — a real, intentional divergence from how Dark/Light look, not a bug. `--accent-on`/`--level-*-on` (On Accent text = Base) simplified to `var(--bg-app)` uniformly across all four flavors as a result (previously `--bg-elevated` for the three dark ones / `--bg-panel` for Latte, back when those held Base). See "Theming" below; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 88, Group 85a's Mocha/Latte assertions updated for the new values.
- **Catppuccin style-guide compliance audit** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested, checked against https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/blob/d09787dd98ca6fba08af5ef2ae94a7e09f17daca/docs/style-guide.md fetched fresh, not from memory). Two confirmed gaps fixed immediately: "On Accent" text now uses each flavor's own Base color (new `--accent-on`/`--level-*-on` var wiring) instead of a generic near-black/white; text selection (`::selection`) now uses Overlay 2 @ 25% opacity for the four Catppuccin flavors (new `--selection-bg` var, reusing `--level-debug` which already = Overlay 2 per flavor) instead of the accent tint every other theme still uses. A third finding (the background-pane brightness hierarchy) was initially left as a flagged, undecided deviation — see the entry above for how that was resolved. Two remaining deliberate, NOT-changed deviations: Warnings use Peach not Yellow (matches this app's own pre-Catppuccin amber warn semantic; the guide's own Code-Editor table allows Peach too); Info uses Blue not Teal (Teal is already `--accent`; reusing it would make INFO rows collide visually with active/selected state). See "Theming" above for the full writeup.
- **Accent color: re-pick the app's own accent from the theme's palette** (this session, 2026-08-22, same-day clarification: "Highlights" in the prior request meant the app's own accent/selection color — buttons and the minimap's time-range marking — not the per-filter-node highlight-color picker the prior entry built). Settings → Appearance gained an "Accent color" row (swatches from `themeOwnPalette(themeId)`, shown only for a theme that has one — the four Catppuccin flavors, or a custom theme whose JSON sets its own `highlightPalette`), letting a person re-pick `--accent` from the active theme's own colors. `setAccentColor`/`applyAccentChoice` recompute `--accent-strong`/`--accent-soft`/`--accent-on` together via a JS mix-toward-white(dark)/black(Latte) + luminance check, persisted per-theme (`localStorage["philogg-accent-choice"]`) so a Mocha pick doesn't affect Latte. Deliberately a JS computation, not `color-mix()`, so each flavor's hand-curated two-tone default (e.g. Mocha's Teal+Sky) stays untouched until someone actually picks something else. See "Theming" above; Group 87.
- **Theming follow-up: stylesheet color audit + theme-aware highlight-color picker** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested: "Prüfe, ob konsequent umgesetzt... gib mir eine Option, eine Highlightfarbe zu wählen, die auf die im Theme festgelegten Farben freigegeben ist"). Audited every CSS rule outside the theme-definition blocks for leftover hardcoded colors — found ~18 (scrollbar thumb, toolbar button hover borders, brand mark, Save/active-chip text, and the highest-impact group: breadcrumb/level-pill/level-badge/log-row-hover/token-chip alpha-tinted borders and backgrounds) still hardcoding the DARK theme's own hex/rgba values regardless of the active theme — invisible on Dark, wrong everywhere else. Fixed with two new vars (`--accent-on` for text-on-accent surfaces, `--border-hover` derived via `color-mix()` from `--border`/`--text-tertiary`) and `color-mix(in srgb, var(--x) N%, transparent)` for the alpha-tinted cases (CSS's only way to apply alpha to a themed `var()` color). The timeline minimap needed no fixes — already fully `fill:var(--level-error)`-etc. from when it was built. Separately, the per-filter-node highlight-color picker (previously theme-independent) gained a "Free"/"Theme" mode toggle: Theme mode restricts its preset swatches to the ACTIVE theme's own `highlightPalette` (the 14 named Catppuccin accent colors per flavor, or a custom theme's own optional array) instead of the generic curated set, so a chosen highlight color is guaranteed to belong to the current theme. See "Theming" above; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 86.
- **Configurable themes + Catppuccin** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested). Settings → Appearance's Light/Dark button pair became a `#settingsThemeSelect` dropdown: Dark, Light, and four new Catppuccin flavors (Latte/Frappé/Macchiato/Mocha — https://catppuccin.com/palette/) as built-ins, each its own `:root[data-theme="..."]` CSS block on the existing var set, plus a new "Custom themes" card for importing themes as JSON (download a template pre-filled from whichever theme is currently active, edit the hex values, import it back — validated against the full required-key set, persisted to `localStorage["philogg-custom-themes"]`, applied via inline CSS custom properties on `<html>` instead of a CSS block since there's no way to pre-author one for an arbitrary import). New `--level-*-on` vars replace the old hardcoded near-black text on the active level-quick-filter buttons, themeable via a per-theme `activeTextLight` boolean instead of a hand-written CSS override per light-background theme. - New: absolute-value wildcard conditions + separator change (this session,
  2026-08-22, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below — person asked
  whether the `;`-separated syntax was sensible/conventional and requested
  an absolute-value option, *"größer als absolut zehn"*). The condition
  separator changed from `;` to `,` (`[value:int<20,>10]`), and a condition's
  operator can now be prefixed with `|` to compare against `|value|` instead
  of `value` — `[value:float|>=10]` matches both `10` and `-15`. Per-condition,
  not per-placeholder, so an abs and a plain condition can mix on the same
  placeholder. Every visualization spot (pattern preview badge, pattern-view
  chip, table header) shows the `|` prefix too, and the pattern preview's
  sample-match search can now legitimately land on a negative value for an
  abs condition. See "Value-extraction pattern language" → "Value
  conditions". 1539 passed, 0 failed (Group 81 extended, not a new group).

- Bugfix, four rounds (this session, 2026-08-22, person-reported via
  screenshots, same-session follow-up to the Settings/collapse redesign
  directly below): **(1)** the sidebar's hover-peek overlay
  (`#sidebarNormalContent` while `.peeking`, see that entry) had no
  `background` of its own — visually detached from `#sidebar`'s 40px box via
  `position:absolute`, so it painted transparent and the log table/timeline
  minimap underneath bled straight through it; fixed with an explicit
  `background:var(--bg-panel)` on that rule. **(2)** The detail panel's
  collapse/expand chevron pointed the wrong way in both states — `.dc.html`
  names its two path variants by open/closed state, not by the actual
  direction the chevron draws, and both the static HTML fallback and
  `ICON_CHEVRON_DOWN`/`ICON_CHEVRON_UP` were copied over with the names and
  visual directions crossed; swapped both path bodies so `_DOWN` truly
  points down and `_UP` truly points up, matching their already-correct
  `collapsed ? ... : ...` usage. **(3)** Settings' left-nav category buttons
  looked unclickable: their `.active` highlight was driven solely by the
  scroll-position `IntersectionObserver` (see the entry below), so clicking
  a category whose section was already fully on screen — no scrolling
  needed — produced zero visible feedback even though the click handler
  (and `scrollIntoView`) fired correctly. The click handler now also sets
  `.active` directly, immediately, independent of whatever the observer
  decides once scrolling (if any) settles. **(4)** The font-size stepper's
  `−`/`+` glyphs rendered top-left instead of centered in their 32×30 boxes
  — the shared base `.btn-icon` class is `display:flex` but never sets
  `align-items`/`justify-content` (fine for its usual single-SVG-child
  usage elsewhere, invisible until text content needed real centering);
  added both to the `.font-scale-stepper .btn-icon` override. All four
  verified against a real Chromium render (Playwright), not just jsdom,
  which can't catch pure paint/geometry bugs like these — see "Known gaps"
  in `tests/README.md`. 1509 passed, 0 failed (no behavior change, so no
  new test group).

- New: Settings dialog redesign + collapsible sidebar/detail panel (this
  session, 2026-08-22, implementing a Claude-Design handoff bundle —
  "Settings Dialog und Panel-Navigation" — carried over from a prior
  session's design-mockup pass; no new open questions, straight
  implementation against the mockup + a written handoff doc).

  **Settings dialog** (`#settingsDialog`): added a left section-nav column
  (`#settingsNav`, Appearance/Behavior/Log Formats, 216px) next to the
  existing single scrollable body (`.settings-page-body` unchanged in
  substance — all three sections still render there, in order) — clicking a
  nav item scrolls to its section; an `IntersectionObserver` (guarded with
  `typeof IntersectionObserver !== "undefined"` — jsdom has none) keeps the
  nav highlighted on whichever section is actually in view, `rootMargin:
  "0px 0px -70% 0px"` so the top-of-viewport section wins. Every settings row
  now sits inside a `.settings-card` (`--bg-panel`/`--border-soft`/10px
  radius, matching the design's exact color values, which happened to equal
  existing tokens 1:1) with a unified `.settings-row` grid
  (`grid-template-columns:1fr auto`, label+hint left / control right at a
  shared edge), one control height (30px) and radius throughout, and bigger
  type (13.5px labels, 12px hints, up from 12.5px/10.5px). Button hierarchy:
  the filled accent button (`.btn-mini`) is now reserved for the one primary
  action per panel (Save format/Save rule) — list-row `Edit`/`Reset` moved
  from `.btn-mini` to the existing `.btn-mini-outline`, and `+ Add
  format…`/`+ Add rule…` got a new `.btn-mini-dashed` (dashed border,
  transparent fill) distinct from both. The boolean "Closing the last log
  file quits the app" row now renders as an on/off switch
  (`.settings-switch`/`.settings-switch-track`, CSS-only over the same real
  `<input type=checkbox>` — no JS/behavior change, still just
  `checked`/`change`) instead of the plain accent-colored checkbox used for
  filter/plot checkboxes elsewhere, so "app preference" reads visually
  distinct from "filter option." Font-size stepper (`−`/value/`+`) regrouped
  into its own `.font-scale-stepper` sub-container so those three sit in one
  seamless bordered pill (previously each had a full border with a gap
  between, since `.font-scale-row`'s flex `gap` applied to all four children
  including `Reset`). GROUP 79.

  **Collapsible sidebar** (`#sidebar`, left/tree): new `#sidebarHeader` (34px
  — "Files & filters" label + chevron) sits above a `#sidebarScroll` wrapper
  now holding the pre-existing `#treeActionBar`/`#folderWatchList`/`#tree`
  (unchanged internally); both together are `#sidebarNormalContent`. Collapse
  sets `#sidebar`'s width to 40px directly (`sidebarEl.style.width`, same
  mechanism the existing drag-resizer already used — no new competing CSS
  width rule) and swaps in `#sidebarRail`, a small hand-built strip (not a
  second tree renderer) showing one clickable marker per node in the
  **active node's ancestor chain** (root file down to whichever's active —
  or per root file if nothing's active yet; see "Known limitations" for why
  not the whole tree), reusing the same `ICON_FILE`/`ICON_MERGE` glyphs and
  `node.highlightColor` a normal tree row would; refreshed from
  `renderTree()` itself so it never goes stale. **Hover-peek**: hovering a
  collapsed rail (`mouseenter`/`mouseleave` on `#sidebar`, gated by
  `hoverExpandSidebar` — see "Open Files & Filters/Entry Detail on hover"
  below) adds
  `.peeking`, which makes `#sidebarNormalContent` — the exact same
  element/markup used when expanded, not a separate peek-only render —
  `position:absolute` over the rail and the content area
  (`width:max-content; max-width:50vw` — content-sized, capped at half the
  application's width, not the old fixed 270px; box-shadow, `overflow:
  visible` on the still-40px `#sidebar` parent so nothing reflows
  underneath); the header's chevron swaps to a pin icon (reusing the
  existing `ICON_PIN` from the bookmarks-pin feature) whose click is the
  *same* toggle function, exiting `.collapsed` entirely (pins it open)
  rather than a separate pinned sub-state. `#sidebarToggle` (the header's
  own button) is consequently only ever visible in two of the three states
  (expanded, or collapsed+peeking) — the third (collapsed, not peeking) is
  the rail's own separately rendered button; `updateSidebarToggleIcon()`
  only distinguishes the two states it's actually shown in.

  **Collapsible detail panel** (`#detailPanel`, bottom): its two children,
  `#detailPanelHeader` (34px — fixed "Entry detail" label + `#detailMeta`
  (`flex:1 1 auto`, single-line, clips instead of wrapping) + chevron) and
  `#detailBody` (placeholder + message), are wrapped together in
  `#detailPanelInner`, which carries the actual flex-column layout while
  peeking (see "Hover-peek" below for why the split exists). `#detailPanel`
  itself stays `display:flex; flex-direction:column` too (own CSS rule,
  **not** just inline — `renderMainView`/`renderTable` also set
  `detailPanel.style.display` directly to show/hide the whole panel based
  on whether any file is loaded, but an inline style only overrides the
  specific property it sets; dropping `flex-direction:column` from the CSS
  rule during the `#detailPanelInner` refactor left it falling back to
  row whenever that inline style fired "flex", shrinking
  `#detailPanelInner` to its content width instead of stretching full —
  see the "Bugfix: Entry Detail bar stopped reaching the right edge"
  changelog entry above). Its `min-height` matters too: `min-height:80px`
  (a floor for the manual drag-resizer) is immediately followed by
  `min-height:0` — dead-looking, but that's the point: last declaration of
  the same property wins the cascade, so the 80px is permanently
  overridden and never actually applies, INCLUDING against the collapsed
  34px height set from `toggleDetailCollapsed` (`min-height` clamps upward
  regardless of where the smaller height comes from). Losing that second
  declaration during the same refactor silently reintroduced the 80px
  floor, clamping the collapsed panel to 80px and leaving an empty gap
  below the header — see the "round 2" changelog entry above. Collapse sets
  `#detailPanel`'s height to 34px directly (same style-driven mechanism as
  the sidebar) and hides `#detailBody` + `#detailResizer` (nothing to drag
  at 34px). `updateDetailPanel()` branches on `isDetailCollapsed() &&
  !isDetailPeeking()`: that condition true (collapsed, not hovering) swaps
  the header to level/time/first-message-line (`.detail-collapsed-msg`, CSS
  ellipsis) instead of going informationally empty; false (expanded, OR
  collapsed-but-peeking) shows the full level/time/thread/location/method
  fields. **Hover-peek** (added this session, 2026-08-22, to match the
  sidebar's; corrected same session after person-reported screenshot
  feedback — see the two "Entry Detail" changelog entries at the top):
  same mechanism as the sidebar, but expanding *upward* — `#detailPanel`
  gained `position:relative` and `overflow:visible` while collapsed;
  `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` (gated by `hoverExpandDetail`) toggle
  `.peeking`, which makes `#detailPanelInner` — header AND body together,
  the exact same wrapper/markup used when expanded, not a body-only
  overlay — `position:absolute; bottom:0` of the still-34px panel with no
  `top` set (so it grows upward from that flush bottom edge) and
  `max-height:50vh`, without pushing `#fhSplit`/`#extractWrap` down. The
  header inside it shows the full field set (per `updateDetailPanel()`
  above) and its chevron swaps to the pin icon while peeking
  (`updateDetailToggleIcon()`, mirrors `updateSidebarToggleIcon()`) — same
  button, same spot, it just now belongs to the part that peeks; clicking
  it while peeking pins the panel open via the same
  `toggleDetailCollapsed()`. The `mouseenter`/`mouseleave` handlers call
  `updateDetailPanel()` directly so the header's field set flips the
  instant peek state changes, not just on the next full render.

  **Open Files & Filters/Entry Detail on hover** (Settings → Behavior,
  this session, 2026-08-22; split into two independent toggles same
  session after person-reported feedback that a single combined setting
  couldn't gate one panel without the other): `hoverExpandSidebar`/
  `hoverExpandDetail` (`philogg-hover-expand-sidebar`/`-detail` in
  `localStorage`, both default ON, same tier/mechanism as
  `quitOnLastFileClose`) each gate their own panel's `mouseenter` handler
  above — off, hovering that collapsed panel does nothing, and it only
  expands via a click on its toggle/rail or a keyboard shortcut; the other
  panel's hover is unaffected. Flipping either off while its panel is
  mid-peek removes `.peeking` immediately rather than waiting for the next
  `mouseleave`.

  **Shared across both**: `Ctrl+B` (sidebar) / `Ctrl+J` (detail) toggle
  shortcuts (added before the existing `Ctrl+Z`/`Ctrl+Y` undo/redo branch in
  the keydown handler; `Ctrl+B` returning early there also incidentally
  fixed a latent ambiguity where it would have fallen through to the plain
  `b` bookmark-toggle shortcut too, since that check doesn't itself exclude
  `ctrlKey`) and a resizer double-click (`dblclick` on
  `#sidebarResizer`/`#detailResizer`) both toggle the same way the header
  chevron does. Collapsed state persists to `localStorage`
  (`philogg-sidebar-collapsed`/`philogg-detail-collapsed`, "1"/"0") — same
  display-preference tier as `THEME_STORAGE_KEY`/`FONT_SCALE_STORAGE_KEY`,
  restored via `initSidebarCollapsed()`/`initDetailCollapsed()` at boot.
  **Last dragged width/height is restored in-session, not via
  localStorage** — matching this codebase's existing convention that the
  sidebar/detail drag-resizers themselves are session-only (see "Five
  changes to the Filtered/Full split and the tree" above); `sidebarLastWidth`
  /`detailLastHeight` are plain JS variables, refreshed from
  `getBoundingClientRect()` right before each collapse. GROUP 80, extended
  by GROUP 91 (detail-panel hover-peek matching the expanded look,
  content-sized/capped peek sizing, the per-panel hover-toggle settings).

  Both parts came with a `.dc.html` design-tool file (colors/radii/spacing
